\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\Big(H(1/\eta)/\eta\Big)^{l^{*}-1}}\leq\delta^{\alpha}\Psi(\epsilon)

for all
\delta
sufficiently small, where
\bar{\rho}
and
\widetilde{\rho}
are the constants defined in Corollary
G.7
and
G.10
, and the event is defined as

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}(\delta)\leq\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\eta)\Big\}\cap\Big\{\exists k\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]\ \text{such that }X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}(\delta)}\in[s_{k}-\epsilon,s_{k}+\epsilon]\Big\}.

Proof.

Let
A^{\times}_{1},A^{\times}_{2}
be the events defined in the proof of Lemma
G.17
. Based on the given
\epsilon>0
, fix some
\widetilde{\epsilon}=\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2M\hat{t}(\epsilon))}
. Fix some
N>\alpha l^{*}
.

Choose
\delta
small enough so that claim in Lemma
G.20
holds for the
\epsilon
prescribed. Using the same arguments in Lemma
G.20
, we have the following inclusion of events:

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)^{c}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\Big\{\forall j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*},\ T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\bar{t}/\eta\Big\}\cap\Big\{\forall j=1,2,3,\cdots,l^{*},\ \eta|W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)|>\bar{\delta}\Big\}.

Therefore, on event
B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)^{c}
, we can apply Corollary
G.7
and
G.10
and conclude that
Z_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}
has
(-\bar{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)-
overflow, and its
(-\bar{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)-
overflow endpoint lies

(s_{k}-3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,s_{k}+3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon)

for some
k\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]
.
Using Lemma
G.15
and Corollary
G.16
, we have that (for any sufficiently small
\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n_{\text{min}}-1}\mu\big(E(-\bar{\epsilon})\cap h^{-1}\Big((s_{k}-\hat{\epsilon},s_{k}+\hat{\epsilon})\big)\Big)

where
\hat{\epsilon}=3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon
. Besides, as established in the proof of Lemma
G.17
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)=o(\eta^{N})

for all sufficiently small
\delta
. In conclusion, we only need to choose

\displaystyle\Psi(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n_{\text{min}}-1}\mu\Big(E(-\bar{\epsilon})\cap h^{-1}\big((s_{k}-3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,s_{k}+3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon)\big)\Big).

To conclude the proof, just note that by combining the continuity of measure with the conditional probability argument leading to
eq.

G.69
, we can show that
\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\Psi(\epsilon)=0
.
∎

Lastly, we establish the lower bound for the probability of the
most likely
way for SGD iterates to exit the current attraction field: making
l^{*}
large jumps in a relatively short period of time. Recall that
\bar{\epsilon}
is the fixed constant in
eq.

G.26
-
eq.

G.29
.

Lemma G.22
.

Given
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
, it holds for any sufficiently small
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\inf_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\circ}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\big(H(1/\eta)/\eta\big)^{l^{*}-1}}\geq c_{*}\delta^{\alpha}

where the event is defined as

\displaystyle A^{\circ}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\sigma(\eta)<R(\epsilon,\eta),\ \sigma(\eta)=T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}(\delta),\ X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}}\notin[s_{-}-\epsilon,s_{+}+\epsilon]\Big\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{2M}\lceil 1/\eta\rceil\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\Big\}

and the constant

c_{*}=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2b})^{l^{*}\alpha}(\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{4M})^{l^{*}-1}

is strictly positive and does not vary with
\epsilon,\delta
.

Proof.

Let
A^{\times}_{1},A^{\times}_{2}
be the events defined in the proof of Lemma
G.17
. Fix some
N
such that
N>\alpha l^{*}
. Based on the given
\epsilon>0
, fix some
\widetilde{\epsilon}=\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2M\hat{t}(\epsilon))}
. We only consider
\delta<M
. Furthermore, choose
\delta
small enough so that
eq.

G.72
and
eq.

G.73
hold for the chosen
N
and
\epsilon
. Also, we only consider
\eta
small enough so that
\eta M<b\wedge\bar{\epsilon}
.

Due to
eq.

G.26
-
eq.

G.29
, we can,
without loss of generality, assume that
r=s_{+}
, and in this case we will have

\displaystyle l^{*}b-100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}>s_{+}+100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}.

Under this assumption, we will now focus on providing a lower bound for the following event that describes the exit from the right side of
\Omega
(in other words, by crossing
s_{+}
)

\displaystyle A^{\circ}_{\rightarrow}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\sigma(\eta)<R(\epsilon,\eta),\ \sigma(\eta)=T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}(\delta),\ X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}}>s_{+}+\epsilon\Big\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{2M}\lceil 1/\eta\rceil\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\Big\}.

First, define event

\displaystyle A^{\circ}_{3}(\delta,\eta)=\Big\{W^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\geq 2b\ \forall j=1,\cdots,l^{*},\ T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{2M}\lceil 1/\eta\rceil\ \forall j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\Big\},

and observe some facts on event
A^{\circ}_{3}(\delta,\eta)\cap\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)^{c}
.

•

|X^{\eta}_{k}|\leq 3\epsilon\forall n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)
; (due to
A^{\times}_{1}
not occurring)

•

X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}\in[b-3\epsilon,b+3\epsilon]
; (due to
W^{\eta}_{1}\geq 2b
and the effect of gradient clipping at step
T^{\eta}_{1}
, as well as the fact that
X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}-1}\in[-3\epsilon,3\epsilon]
from the previous bullet point)

•

Due to
|f^{\prime}(\cdot)|\leq M
and
\delta<M
, one can see that (for any
n\geq 1
)

\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}|\eta f^{\prime}(x)|+|\eta Z_{n}^{\leq\delta,\eta}|\leq 2\eta M;

this provides an upper bound for the change in SGD iterates at each step, and gives us

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{n}\in[b-3\epsilon-\bar{\epsilon},b+3\epsilon+\bar{\epsilon}]\ \ \forall T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\leq n<T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)

where we also used
T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\leq\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{2M}\lceil 1/\eta\rceil

•

Therefore, at the arrival time of the second large jump, we must have
X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)}\geq 2b-3\epsilon-\bar{\epsilon}
;

•

By repeating the argument above inductively, we can show that (for all
j=1,2,\cdots,l^{*}
)

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{n}

\displaystyle\in[(j-1)b-3\epsilon-(j-1)\bar{\epsilon},(j-1)b+3\epsilon+(j-1)\bar{\epsilon}]\ \ \forall T^{\eta}_{j-1}\leq n<T^{\eta}_{j}

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{j}}

\displaystyle\in[jb-3\epsilon-(j-1)\bar{\epsilon},jb+3\epsilon+(j-1)\bar{\epsilon}];

In particular, we know that
X^{\eta}_{n}\in\Omega
for any
n<T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}
(so the exit does not occur before
T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}
), and at the arrival of the
l^{*}-
th large jump, we have (using
3\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}
)

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{l^{*}}(\delta)}\geq l^{*}b-l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}>s_{+}+\epsilon.

In summary, we have shown that

\displaystyle A^{\circ}_{3}(\delta,\eta)\cap\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)^{c}\subseteq A^{\circ}_{\rightarrow}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta).

To conclude the proof, just notice that (for sufficiently small
\eta
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(A^{\circ}_{3}(\delta,\eta)\cap\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)\Big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(A^{\circ}_{3}(\delta,\eta))-\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))-\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{2}(\widetilde{\epsilon},\delta,\eta))

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(A^{\circ}_{3}(\delta,\eta))-\eta^{N}\ \ \ \ \ \text{due to \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{proof atypical 1 bound rare event 1} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{proof atypical 1 bound rare event 2}}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\frac{H(2b/\eta)}{H(\delta/\eta)}\Big)^{l^{*}}\Big(\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{4M}H(\delta/\eta)/\eta\Big)^{l^{*}-1}-\eta^{N}\ \ \ \ \text{due to Lemma \ref{lemmaGeomFront}}

\displaystyle\geq 2c_{*}\delta^{\alpha}(H(1/\eta)/\eta)^{l^{*}-1}-\eta^{N}\ \ \ \ \text{for all $\eta$ sufficiently small, due to $H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}$}

\displaystyle\geq c_{*}\delta^{\alpha}(H(1/\eta)/\eta)^{l^{*}-1}.

∎

In order to present the main result of this section, we need to take into account the loss landscape outside of the current attraction field
\Omega
. Recall that there are
n_{\text{min}}
attraction fields on
f
. For all the attraction fields different from
\Omega
, we call them
(\widetilde{\Omega}_{k})_{k=1}^{n_{\text{min}}-1}
where, for each
k\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]
, the attraction field
\widetilde{\Omega}_{k}=(s^{-}_{k},s^{+}_{k})
with the corresponding local minimum located at
\widetilde{m}_{k}
. Also, recall that
\sigma(\eta)
is the first time
X^{\eta}_{n}
exits from
\Omega
. Building upon these concepts, we can define a stopping time

\displaystyle\tau(\eta,\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{n_{\text{min}}-1}[\widetilde{m}_{k}-2\epsilon,\widetilde{m}_{k}+2\epsilon]\}

(G.75)

as the first time the SGD iterates visit a minimizer in an attraction field that is different from
\Omega
. Besides, let index
J_{\sigma}(\eta)
be such that

\displaystyle J_{\sigma}(\eta)=j\iff X^{\eta}_{\sigma(\eta)}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{j}\ \ \forall j\in[n_{\text{min}}-1].

(G.76)

In other words, it is the label of the attraction field that
X^{\eta}_{n}
escapes to. Lastly, define

\displaystyle\lambda(\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}H(1/\eta)\Big(H(1/\eta)/\eta\Big)^{l^{*}-1},

(G.77)

\displaystyle\nu^{\Omega}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mu\big(E(0)\big),

(G.78)

\displaystyle\nu^{\Omega}_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mu\big(E(0)\cap h^{-1}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{k})\big)\ \ \forall k\in[n_{\text{min}}-1].

(G.79)

For definitions of the measure
\mu
, set
E
, and mapping
h
, see
eq.

G.64
and
eq.

G.65
.

Now we are ready to state Proposition
G.23
, the most important technical tool in this section. In
eq.

G.80
and
eq.

G.81
, we provide upper and lower bounds for the joint distribution of first exit time
\sigma
and the label
J_{\sigma}
indexing the attraction field we escape to; it is worth noticing that the claims hold uniformly for all
u>C
. In
eq.

G.82
and
eq.

G.83
, we provide upper and lower bounds for the joint distribution of when we first visit a different local minimum (which is equal to
\tau
) and which one we visit (indicated by
X^{\eta}_{\tau}
). The similarity between
eq.

G.80

eq.

G.81
and
eq.

G.82
eq.

G.83
suggests a strong correlation between the behavior of the SGD iterates at time
\sigma(\eta)
and that of time
\tau(\eta,\epsilon)
, and this is corroborated by
eq.

G.84
: we show that it is almost always the case that
\tau
is very close to
\sigma
, and on the short time interval
[\sigma(\eta),\tau(\eta,\epsilon)]
the SGD iterates stay within the same attraction field.

Proposition G.23
.

Given
C>0
and some
k^{\prime}\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]
, the following claims hold for all
\epsilon>0
that is sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{u\in(C,\infty)}\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\nu^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,\ J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C+\exp(-(1-C)^{3}u)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}},

(G.80)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{u\in(C,\infty)}\inf_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\nu^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,\ J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-2C+\exp(-(1+C)^{3}u)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}-C}{\nu^{\Omega}},

(G.81)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{u\in(C,\infty)}\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\nu^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\tau(\eta,\epsilon)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\tau(\eta,\epsilon)}\in B(\widetilde{m}_{k^{\prime}},2\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4C+\exp(-(1-C)^{3}u)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}},

(G.82)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{u\in(C,\infty)}\inf_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\nu^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\tau(\eta,\epsilon)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\tau(\eta,\epsilon)}\in B(\widetilde{m}_{k^{\prime}},2\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-4C+\exp(-(1+C)^{3}u)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}-C}{\nu^{\Omega}},

(G.83)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\lambda(\eta)\big(\tau(\eta,\epsilon)-\sigma(\eta)\big)<C,\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\ \ \forall n\in[\sigma(\eta),\tau(\eta,\epsilon)]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-C.

(G.84)

Before presenting the proof to Proposition
G.23
, we make some preparations. First, we introduce stopping times (for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\tau_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n>\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\eta|Z_{n}|>\delta\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n\geq\tau_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):|X^{\eta}_{n}|\leq 2\epsilon\}

with the convention that
\tau_{0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\widetilde{\tau}_{0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=0.
The intuitive interpretation is as follows. For the fixed
\epsilon
we treat
[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]
as a small neighborhood of the local minimum of the attraction field
\Omega
. All the
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
partitioned the entire timeline into different
attempts
of escaping
\Omega
. The interval
[\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{k}]
can be viewed as the
k-
th
attempt
. If for
\sigma(\eta)
, the first exit time defined in
eq.

G.70
, we have
\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
, then we consider the
k-
th attempt of escape as a
failure
because the SGD iterates returned to this small neighborhood of the local minimum again without exiting the attraction field. On the other hand, the stopping times
\tau_{k-1}
indicate the arrival time of the first large jump during the
k-
th
attempt
. The proviso that
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\geq\tau_{k-1}
can be interpreted, intuitively, as that an
attempt
is considered failed only if, after some significant efforts to exit (for instance, a large jump) has been observed, the SGD iterates still returned to the small neighborhood
[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]
. Regarding the notations, we add a remark that when there is no ambiguity we will drop the dependency on
\epsilon,\delta,\eta
and simply write
\tau_{k},\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
.

To facilitate the characterization of events during each
attempt
, we introduce the following definitions. First, for all
k\geq 1
, let

\displaystyle\textbf{j}_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\{n=\tau_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),\tau_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)+1,\cdots,\widetilde{\tau}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\wedge\sigma(\eta):\eta|Z_{n}|>\delta\}

be the number of large jumps during the
k-
th
attempt
. Two implications of this definition:

•

First, for any
k
with
\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
, we have
\textbf{j}_{k}=0
. Note that this proposition concerns the dynamics of SGD up until
\sigma(\eta)
, the first time the SGD iterates escaped from
\Omega
, so there is no need to consider an attempt that is after
\sigma(\eta)
, and we will not do so in the analysis below;

•

Besides, the random variable
\textbf{j}_{k}
is measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}
, the stopped
\sigma-
algebra generated by the stopping time
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)
.

Furthermore, for each
k=1,2,\cdots
, let

\displaystyle T_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\tau_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\wedge\sigma(\eta),

\displaystyle T_{k,j}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n>T_{k,j-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\eta|Z_{n}|>\delta\}\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\ \ \forall j\geq 2,

\displaystyle W_{k,j}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=Z_{T_{k,j}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)}\ \ \forall j\geq 1

with the convention
T_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
.
Note that for any
k\geq 1,j\geq 1
,
T_{k,j}
is a stopping time. Besides, from the definition of
\textbf{j}_{k}
, one can see that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}+1\leq T_{k,j}\leq\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)\ \ \forall j\in[\textbf{j}_{k}],

(G.85)

and the sequences
\big(T_{k,j}\big)_{j=1}^{\textbf{j}_{k}}
and
\big(W_{k,j}\big)_{j=1}^{\textbf{j}_{k}}

are the arrival times and sizes of
large
jumps during the
k-
th attempt, respectively. Again, when there is no ambiguity we will drop the dependency on
\epsilon,\delta,\eta
and simply write
T_{k,j}
and
W_{k,j}
.

In order to prove Proposition
G.23
, we analyze the most likely scenario that the exit from
\Omega
would happen. Specifically, we will introduce a series of events with superscript
\times
or
\circ
, where
\times
indicates that the event is
atypical
or unlikely to happen and
\circ
means that it is a
typical
event and is likely to be observed before the first exit from the attraction field
\Omega
. Besides, the subscript
k
indicates that the event in discussion concerns the dynamics of SGD during the
k-
th attempt. Our goal is to show that for some event
\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
its probability becomes sufficiently small as learning rate
\eta
tends to 0, so the escape from
\Omega
almost always occurs in the manner described by
(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))^{c}
. In particular, the definition of this
atypical
scenario
\textbf{A}^{\times}
involves the union of some
atypical
events
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k},\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
that occur in the
k-
th attempt. In other words, the intuition of
\textbf{A}^{\times}
is that something
abnormal
happened during one of the attempts before the final exit.

Here is one more comment for the general naming convention of these events. Events with label
A
often describe the “efforts” made in an attempt to get out of
\Omega
(such as large noises), while those with label
B
concern how the SGD iterates return to
[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]
(and how this attempt fails). For instance,
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}
discusses the unlikely scenario before
T_{k,l^{*}}
, the arrival of the
l^{*}-
th large jump in this attempt, while
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
in general discusses the abnormal cases after
T_{k,l^{*}}
and before the return to
[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]
. On the other hand,
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
describes a successful escape during
k-
th attempt, while
\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
means that during this attempt the iterates return to without spending too much time.

Now we proceed and provide a formal definition and analysis of the aforementioned series of events. As building blocks, we inspect the process
(X^{\eta}_{n})_{n\geq 1}
at a even finer granularity, and bound the probability of some events
(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,i})_{i\geq 0},\ (\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,i})_{i\geq 1}
detailing several cases that are
unlikely
to occur during the escape from or return to local minimum in the
k-
th attempt. First, for each
k\geq 1
, define the event

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists i=0,1,\cdots,l^{*}\wedge\textbf{j}_{k}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \max_{j=T_{k,i}+1,\cdots,(T_{k,i+1}-1)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}\eta|Z_{T_{k,i}+1}^{\leq\delta,\eta}+\cdots+Z_{j}^{\leq\delta,\eta}|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\bar{\rho}+3\widetilde{\rho}+3}\Big\}.

(G.86)

Intuitively speaking, the event characterizes the atypical scenario where, during the
k-
th attempt, there is some large fluctuations (compared to
\widetilde{\epsilon}
) between any of the first
l^{*}
large jumps (or the first
\textbf{j}_{k}
large jumps in case that
\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}
). Similarly, consider event (for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}\Big\}

(G.87)

that describes the atypical case where the exit occurs during the
k-
th attempt with less than
l^{*}
large jumps. Next, for all
k\geq 1
we have another atypical event (note that from
eq.

G.85
we can see that, for any
j\geq 1
,
\textbf{j}_{k}\geq j
implies
T_{k,j}\leq\sigma(\eta)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ \exists j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big\}.

(G.88)

representing the case where we have at least
l^{*}
large noises during the
k-
th attempt, but for some of the large noise (from the 2nd to the
l^{*}
-th), the inter-arrival time is unusually long. Moving on, we consider the following events (defined for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,3}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}<\sigma(\eta),\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,1}>2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big\}

(G.89)

that describes the atypical case where the
k-
th attempt failed but the return to the small neighborhood
[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]
took unusually long time.

The following event also concerns the scenario where there are at least
l^{*}
large noises during the
k-
th attempt:

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,4}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ |X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}|\geq r-\bar{\epsilon},\ \exists j=1,2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.\ \eta|W_{k,j}|\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\};

(G.90)

specifically, it describes the atypical case where, during this attempt, right after the
l^{*}-
large noise the SGD iterate is far enough from the local minimum yet some of the large noises are not that
large
.
Lastly, by defining events

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,5}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ T_{k,l^{*}}\leq\sigma(\eta)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in\bigcup_{j\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]}[s_{j}-\epsilon,s_{j}+\epsilon]\Big\},

(G.91)

we analyze an atypical case where the SGD iterates arrive at somewhere too close to the boundaries of
\Omega
at the arrival time of the
l^{*}
large noise during this attempt.
As an amalgamation of these atypical scenarios, we let

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\bigcup_{i=0}^{5}\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,i}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta).

(G.92)

Also, we analyze the probability of some events
(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})_{k\geq 1}
that concern the SGD dynamics after the
l^{*}-
th large noise during the
k-
th attempt. Let us define

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in[s_{-}+\epsilon,s_{+}-\epsilon],\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq 2\frac{\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\Big\}

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,l^{*}}>\rho(\epsilon)/\eta\}\cup\{\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\}

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}

(G.93)

where
\rho(\cdot)
is the function in Lemma
G.13
. From the definition of
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
, in particular the inclusion of
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}
, one can see that the intuitive interpretation of event
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
is that the SGD iterates
did not return
to local minimum efficiently (or simply escaped from the attraction field) after the
l^{*}-
th large noise during the
k-
th attempt.
In comparison, the following events will characterize what would typically happen during each attempt:

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ \sigma(\eta)=T_{k,l^{*}},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\notin[s_{-}-\epsilon,s_{+}+\epsilon],

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\},

(G.94)

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,1}\leq\frac{2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)+\rho(\epsilon)}{\eta}\}.

(G.95)

Intuitively speaking,
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
tells us that the exit happened right at
T_{k,l^{*}}
, the arrival time of the
l^{*}-
th large noise during the
k-
th attempt, and
\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
tells us that the first exit from
\Omega
did not occur during the
k-
th attempt, and the SGD iterates returned to local minimum rather efficiently. All the preparations above allow use to define

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\bigg(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{i}\big)^{c}\bigg)\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\big).

(G.96)

We need the next lemma in the proof of Proposition
G.23
. As mentioned earlier, the takeaway is that
\textbf{A}^{\times}
is indeed
atypical
in the sense that we will almost always observe
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
.

Lemma G.24
.

Given any
C>0
, the following claim holds for all
\epsilon>0,\delta>0
sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))<C.

Proof.

We fix some parameters for the proof. First, with out loss of generality we only consider
C\in(0,1)
, and we fix some
N>\alpha l^{*}
. Next we discuss the valid range of
\epsilon
for the claim to hold. We only consider
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\epsilon<\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{6(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)}\wedge\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{3}

where
\bar{\rho}
and
\widetilde{\rho}
are the constants in Corollary
G.7
and Corollary
G.10
respectively, and
\epsilon_{0}
is the constant in
eq.

G.8
. Moreover, recall function
\Psi
in Lemma
G.21
and the constant
c_{*}>0
in Lemma
G.22
. Due to
\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\Psi(\epsilon)=0
, it holds for all
\epsilon
small enough such that

\displaystyle\frac{3\Psi(\epsilon)}{c_{*}}

\displaystyle<C

(G.97)

In our proof we only consider
\epsilon
small enough so the inequality above holds, and the claim in Lemma
G.13
holds. Now we specify the valid range of parameter
\delta
that will be used below:

•

For all sufficiently small
\delta>0
, the claim in Lemma
G.14
will hold for the prescribed
\epsilon
and with
N_{0}=N
;

•

For all sufficiently small
\delta>0
, the claims in Lemma
G.17
, Corollary
G.18
, Corollary
G.19
and Lemma
G.20
will hold with the prescribed
\epsilon
and
N
;

•

For all sufficiently small
\delta>0
, the inequalities in Lemma
G.21
and
G.22
will hold for the
\epsilon
we fixed at the beginning.

We show that the claim holds for any
\epsilon,\delta
small enough to satisfy the conditions above.

First, recall that

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists i=0,1,\cdots,l^{*}\wedge\textbf{j}_{k}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \max_{j=T_{k,i}+1,\cdots,(T_{k,i+1}-1)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}\eta|Z_{T_{k,i}+1}^{\leq\delta,\eta}+\cdots+Z_{j}^{\leq\delta,\eta}|>\frac{\epsilon}{3\bar{\rho}+3\widetilde{\rho}+3}\Big\}.

Due to our choice of
\delta
stated earlier and Lemma
G.12
, there exists some
\eta_{0}>0
such that for all
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq\eta^{N}\ \ \forall k\geq 1.

(G.98)

Similarly, recall that
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}\Big\}.

Let us temporarily focus on the first attempt (namely the case
k=1
). From Lemma
G.17
and our choice of
\epsilon
and
\delta
, we know the existence of some
\eta_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq\eta^{N}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{1}).

(G.99)

Next, for
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ \exists j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big\},

from Corollary
G.18
and our choice of
\delta
at the beginning, we have the existence of some
\eta_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq\eta^{N}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{2}).

(G.100)

Moving on, for
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,3}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}<\sigma(\eta),\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,1}>2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big\}
,
due to Corollary
G.19
and our choice of
\epsilon,\delta
, we have the existence of some
\eta_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq\eta^{N}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{3}).

(G.101)

As for
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,4}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ |X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}|\geq r-\bar{\epsilon},\ \exists j=1,2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.\ \eta|W_{k,j}|\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\},

from Lemma
G.20
, one can see the existence of
\eta_{4}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,4}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq\eta^{N}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{4}).

(G.102)

Lastly, for
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,5}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ T_{k,l^{*}}\leq\sigma(\eta)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in\bigcup_{j\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]}[s_{j}-\epsilon,s_{j}+\epsilon]\Big\},

from Lemma
G.21
we see the existence of
\eta_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,5}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq 2\delta^{\alpha}\Psi(\epsilon)\Big(H(1/\eta)/\eta\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{5}).

(G.103)

Recall that
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\bigcup_{i=0}^{5}\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,i}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
. Also, for definitions of
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k},\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k},\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
, see
eq.

G.93
,
eq.

G.94
,
eq.

G.95
respectively. Our next goal is to establish bounds regarding the probabilities of these events. First, if we consider the event
\bigcap_{j=1}^{k}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}
, then the inclusion of the
(\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j})_{j=1}^{k}
implies that during the first
k
attempts the SGD iterates have never left the attraction field, so

\displaystyle\bigcap_{j=1}^{k}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}=\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\{\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\}.

Next, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,l^{*}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big).

From the definition of the events
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j},\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j},\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}
, one can see that
\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}
, and on this event
\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}
we have
\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}
. So by applying strong Markov property at stopping time
\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}\wedge\sigma(\eta)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq 2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\ \forall j\in[l^{*}-1]\Big)\cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Big(H(\delta/\eta)\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big)

(for all
\eta
sufficiently small due to Lemma
G.4
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\Big(\frac{\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\delta^{\alpha}}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big)

for all sufficiently small
\eta
, due to
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(\eta)
.
Meanwhile, note that

•

\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\in\mathcal{F}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}
;

•

on this event
\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}
we have
\sigma(\eta)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}>T_{k,l^{*}}
and
X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in[s_{-}+\epsilon,s_{+}-\epsilon]
.

Therefore, using Lemma
G.13
and strong Markov property again (at stopping time
T_{k,l^{*}}
), we know the following inequality holds for all
\eta
sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 1}\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 1}\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\big\{\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,l^{*}}\leq\rho(\epsilon)/\eta\big\}^{c}\ \Big|\ \big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Psi(\epsilon)\frac{\delta^{\alpha}}{4\big(\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\delta^{\alpha}\big)^{l^{*}-1}}.

Therefore, we know the existence of some
\eta_{6}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\leq\Psi(\epsilon)\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{6}),\ \forall k\geq 1.

(G.104)

Similarly, we can bound conditional probabilities of the form
\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)
. To be specific, recall that
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}=\cup_{i=0}^{5}\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,i}
. By combining
eq.

G.98
-
eq.

G.103
with Markov property, we know the existence of some
\eta_{7}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\leq 5\eta^{N}+2\Psi(\epsilon)\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{7}),\ \forall k\geq 1.

(G.105)

On the other hand, a lower bound can be established for conditional probability involving
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
, the event defined in
eq.

G.94
describing the exit from
\Omega
during an attempt with exactly
l^{*}
large noises. Using Lemma
G.22
and Markov property of
(X^{\eta}_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, one can see the existence of some
\eta_{8}>0
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\geq c_{*}\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\eta_{8}).

(G.106)

In order to apply the bounds
eq.

G.104
-
eq.

G.106
, we make use of the following inclusion relationship:

\displaystyle\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\subseteq\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}.

(G.107)

To see why this is true, let us consider a decomposition of the event on the L.H.S. of
eq.

G.107
. As mentioned above, on event
\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}
we know that
\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}
, so the
k-
th attempt occurred and there are only three possibilities on this event:

•

\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}
;

•

\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\notin\Omega
;

•

\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in\Omega
.

Let us partition the said event accordingly and analyze them one by one.

•

On
\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap\{\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}\}
, due to the exclusion of
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}
(especially
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,1}
and
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,3}
), we can see that if
\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}
, then we must have
\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,1}\leq 2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap\{\textbf{j}_{k}<l^{*}\}\subseteq\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}.

•

On
\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\notin\Omega\}
, then the exclusion of
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,2}
implies that
T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq 2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta
for all
j=2,\cdots,l^{*}
, and the exclusion of
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,5}
tells us that if
X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\notin\Omega
, then we have
X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\notin[s_{-}-\epsilon,s_{+}+\epsilon]
. In summary,

\displaystyle\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\notin\Omega\}\subseteq\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}.

•

On
\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in\Omega\}
, the exclusion of
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,2}
again implies that
T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq 2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta
for all
j=2,\cdots,l^{*}
, hence
T_{k,l^{*}}-T_{k,1}\leq 2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta
. Similarly, the exclusion of
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,5}
tells us that if
X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in\Omega
, then we have
X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in[s_{-}+\epsilon,s_{+}-\epsilon]
. Now since
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
did not occur (see the definition in
eq.

G.93
), we must have
\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,l^{*}}\leq\rho(\epsilon)/\eta
, hence
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,1}\leq\frac{2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)+\rho(\epsilon)}{\eta}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap\{\textbf{j}_{k}\geq l^{*},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{k,l^{*}}}\in\Omega\}\subseteq\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}.

Collecting results above, we have
eq.

G.107
. Now we discuss some of its implications. First, from
eq.

G.107
we can immediately get that

\displaystyle\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}=\big(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k-1}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j})^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{j}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})^{c}\cap(\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}).

(G.108)

Next, recall the definitions of
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
in
eq.

G.94
and
\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
in
eq.

G.95
, and one can see that
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
and
\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
are mutually exclusive, since the former implies that the first exit occurs during the
k-
th attempt while the latter implies that this attempt fails. This fact and
eq.

G.108
allow us to conclude that

\displaystyle\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{i}\big)^{c}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}=\Big(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k})^{c}.

(G.109)

Now we use the results obtained so far to bound the probability of

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\bigg(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{i}\big)^{c}\bigg)\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\big).

Using
eq.

G.109
, we can see that (for any
x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon])

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\bigg(\bigg(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{i}\big)^{c}\bigg)\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\bigg(\bigg(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\bigg)\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\bigcap_{i=1}^{j}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\ \Big|\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{j-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\big(\bigcap_{i=1}^{j-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\big)\cap(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{j})^{c}\ \Big|\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{j-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{j}\big)^{c}\ \Big|\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{j-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\ |\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\Bigg(1-\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{j}\ \Big|\ \bigcap_{i=1}^{j-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\big)^{c}\cap\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{i}\Big)\Bigg).

This allows us to apply
eq.

G.104
-
eq.

G.106
and conclude that (here we only consider
\eta<\min\{\eta_{i}:i\in[8]\}
),

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\Big(5\eta^{N}+2\Psi(\epsilon)\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\Big)\cdot\Big(1-c_{*}\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}\Big)^{k-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5\eta^{N}+2\Psi(\epsilon)\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}}{c_{*}\delta^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\Psi(\epsilon)+5\eta^{\alpha}}{c_{*}}\ \ \ \text{for sufficiently small $\eta$, due to $H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(\eta)$ and our choice of $N>\alpha l^{*}$ }

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3\Psi(\epsilon)}{c_{*}}<C\ \ \ \text{for all $\eta$ small enough such that $5\eta^{\alpha}<\Psi(\epsilon)$}.

The last inequality follows from our choice of
\epsilon
in
eq.

G.97
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Having established Lemma
G.24
, we return to Proposition
G.23
and give a proof. Recall that, aside from the attraction field
\Omega=(s_{-},s_{+})
, there are
n_{\text{min}}-1
other attraction fields
\widetilde{\Omega}_{k}=(s^{-}_{k},s^{+}_{k})
(for each
k\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]
). Besides, the function
\lambda(\cdot)
and constants
\nu^{\Omega},\nu^{\Omega}_{k}
are defined in
eq.

G.77
-
eq.

G.79
.

Proof of Proposition
G.23
.

We fix some parameters for the proof. First, with out loss of generality we only need to consider
C\in(0,1)
. Next we discuss the valid range of
\epsilon
for the claim to hold. We only consider
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\epsilon<\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{6(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)}\wedge\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{3}

where
\bar{\rho}
and
\widetilde{\rho}
are the constants in Corollary
G.7
and Corollary
G.10
respectively, and
\epsilon_{0}
is the constant in
eq.

G.8
. Due to continuity of measure
\mu
, it holds for all
\epsilon
small enough such that (let
\hat{\epsilon}=3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon
)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu(E(0))}{\mu\big(E\big(\hat{\epsilon}\big)\big)}

\displaystyle<1/(1-C),

(G.110)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu(E(0))}{\mu\big(E\big(-\hat{\epsilon}\big)\big)}

\displaystyle>1/(1+C),

(G.111)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu\Big(h^{-1}\big((s_{-}-2\hat{\epsilon},s_{-}+2\hat{\epsilon})\cup(s_{+}-2\hat{\epsilon},s_{+}+2\hat{\epsilon})\big)\Big)}{\mu(E(-\hat{\epsilon}))}

\displaystyle\leq C

(G.112)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu\Big(E(\hat{\epsilon})\cap\big(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}-\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}+\hat{\epsilon})\Big)}{\mu(E(\hat{\epsilon}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}}

(G.113)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu\Big(E(-\hat{\epsilon})\cap\big(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}+2\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}-2\hat{\epsilon})\Big)}{\mu(E(-\hat{\epsilon}))}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}-C}{\nu^{\Omega}}

(G.114)

In our proof we only consider
\epsilon
small enough so the inequality above holds, and the claims in Lemma
G.13
hold. Moreover, we only consider
\epsilon
and
\delta
small enough so that Lemma
G.24
hold and we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))<C.

(G.115)

We show that the desired claims hold for all
\epsilon,\delta
sufficiently small that satisfy conditions above.

First, in order to show
eq.

G.84
, we define event

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textbf{A}}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\cap

\displaystyle\Big\{\lambda(\eta)\big(\tau(\eta,\epsilon)-\sigma(\eta)\big)\geq C\text{ or }\exists n=\sigma(\eta)+1,\cdots,\tau(\eta,\epsilon)\text{ such that }X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\Big\}.

Since
\lambda\in\mathcal{RV}_{-1-l^{*}(\alpha-1)}
and
\alpha>1
, for the
\epsilon
we fixed at the beginning of this proof,
\rho(\epsilon)
is a fixed constant as well (the function
\rho
is defined in Lemma
G.13
) and we have
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\lambda(\eta)\rho(\epsilon)/\eta=0
. Next, the occurrence of
\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}
(in particular, the exclusion of all the
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,5}
defined in
eq.

G.91
), we know that
X^{\eta}_{\sigma(\eta)}\notin[s^{-}_{J_{\sigma}}-\epsilon,s^{-}_{J_{\sigma}}+\epsilon]\cup[s^{+}_{J_{\sigma}}-\epsilon,s^{+}_{J_{\sigma}}+\epsilon]
(recall that for any
k\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]
, we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{j}=(s^{-}_{j},s^{+}_{j})
; for definition of
J_{\sigma}
see
eq.

G.76
). Meanwhile, for all
\eta
sufficiently small, we have
\epsilon/\lambda(\eta)>\rho(\epsilon)/\eta
. Therefore, using Lemma
G.13
we can see that (for all
\eta
sufficiently small)

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{\textbf{A}}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\ |\ \big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)\leq C.

(G.116)

Lastly, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{\lambda(\eta)\big(\tau(\eta,\epsilon)-\sigma(\eta)\big)\geq C\text{ or }\exists n=\sigma(\eta)+1,\cdots,\tau(\eta,\epsilon)\text{ such that }X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\Big\{\lambda(\eta)\big(\tau(\eta,\epsilon)-\sigma(\eta)\big)\geq C

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \text{ or }\exists n=\sigma(\eta)+1,\cdots,\tau(\eta,\epsilon)\text{ such that }X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\Big\}\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times})

so by combining
eq.

G.115
with
eq.

G.116
, we can obtain
eq.

G.84
.

Moving on, we discuss the upper bounds
eq.

G.80
and
eq.

G.82
. Recall that the fixed constant
k^{\prime}\in[n_{\text{min}}-1]
is prescribed in the description of this proposition. Let us observe some facts on event
(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\eta,\delta))^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}
: If we let
J(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\sup\{k\geq 0:\widetilde{\tau}_{k}<\sigma(\eta)\}
be the number of attempts it took to escape, and

J^{\uparrow}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{k\geq 1:T_{k,1}\ \text{ has $\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)-$overflow}\},

then for all
\eta
sufficiently small, we must have
J\leq J^{\uparrow}
on event
(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\eta,\delta))^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}
. To see this via a proof of contradiction, let us assume that, for some arbitrary positive integer
j
, there exists some sample path on
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}
such that
J^{\uparrow}=j<J
. Then from the definition of
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
, in particular the exclusion of event
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{j,0}
(see the definition in
eq.

G.86
), for all sufficiently small
\eta
, we are able to apply Corollary
G.10
and
G.7
and conclude that
X^{\eta}_{T_{j,l^{*}}}\notin\Omega
: indeed, using Corollary
G.10
and
G.7
we can show that the distance between
X^{\eta}_{T_{j,l^{*}}}
and the perturbed ODE

\widetilde{\textbf{x}}^{\eta}\Big(T_{j,l^{*}}-T_{j,1},0;\big(0,T_{j,2}-T_{j,1},\cdots,T_{j,l^{*}}-T_{j,1}\big),\big(\eta W_{j,1},\cdots,\eta W_{j,l^{*}}\big)\Big)

is strictly less than
3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon
; on the other hand, the definition of
\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)-
overflow implies that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textbf{x}}^{\eta}\Big(T_{j,l^{*}}-T_{j,1},0;\big(0,T_{j,2}-T_{j,1},\cdots,T_{j,l^{*}}-T_{j,1}\big),\big(\eta W_{j,1},\cdots,\eta W_{j,l^{*}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \notin[s_{-}-3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,s_{+}+3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon].

Therefore, we must have
X^{\eta}_{T_{j,l^{*}}}\notin\Omega
, which contradicts our assumption
j=J^{\uparrow}<J
. In summary, we have shown that, on
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}
, we have
J^{\uparrow}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\geq J(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
.
Similarly, if we consider

J^{\downarrow}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{k\geq 1:T_{k,1}\ \text{ has $\big(-3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\big)-$overflow}\},

then by the same argument above we can show that
J^{\downarrow}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\leq J(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
. Now consider the following decomposition of events.

•

On
\{J^{\downarrow}<J^{\uparrow}\}
, we know that for the first
k
such that
T_{k,1}
has
\big(-3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\big)-
overflow, it does not have
\big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\big)-
overflow. Now we analyze the probability that
Z_{0}
does not have
(\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)-
overflow conditioning on that it does have
(-\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)-
overflow (recall that we let
\hat{\epsilon}=3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)
). Using Lemma
G.15
and the bound
eq.

G.112
, we know that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\downarrow}<J^{\uparrow}\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu\Big(h^{-1}\big((s_{-}-2\hat{\epsilon},s_{-}+2\hat{\epsilon})\cup(s_{+}-2\hat{\epsilon},s_{+}+2\hat{\epsilon})\big)\Big)}{\mu(E(-\hat{\epsilon}))}\leq C.

(G.117)

•

On
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}=J^{\downarrow}\}
, due to
J^{\uparrow}=J^{\downarrow}=J
we know that
T_{J(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),1}
is the first among all
T_{k,1}
to have
(\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)-
overflow. Moreover, due to
\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}
and using Corollary
G.10
and
G.7
again as we did above, we know that the overflow endpoint of
T_{J(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),1}
is in
(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}-\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}+\hat{\epsilon})
(recall that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}=(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}})
). In summary, for any
n\geq 0

\displaystyle(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}=J^{\downarrow}>n\}

\displaystyle\subseteq(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}>n\}\cap\Big\{T_{J^{\uparrow},1}\text{ has overflow endpoint in $(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}-\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}+\hat{\epsilon})$}\Big\}

so using Lemma
G.15
, we obtain that (for all
\eta
sufficiently small)

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}=J^{\downarrow}>n\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}>n\}\Big)\cdot\frac{p\big(\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta;(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}-\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}+\hat{\epsilon})\big)}{p(\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}>n\}\Big)\cdot\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}}.

(G.118)

uniformly for any
n=0,1,2,\cdots
due to
eq.

G.113
.

•

On the other hand, on
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
, if
T_{J^{\downarrow},1}
has overflow endpoint in
(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}+2\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}-2\hat{\epsilon})
, then from Definition
G.1
we know that
T_{J^{\downarrow},1}
also has
(\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)-
overflow, hence
J^{\downarrow}=J^{\uparrow}=J
. Moreover, using Corollary
G.10
and
G.7
again, we know that
X^{\eta}_{T_{J^{\downarrow},l^{*}}}\in(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}})
so
J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}
. In summary, for any
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}=J^{\downarrow}>n\}

\displaystyle\supseteq(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\downarrow}>n\}\cap\Big\{T_{J^{\downarrow},1}\text{ has overflow endpoint in $(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}+2\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}-2\hat{\epsilon})$}\Big\}

so using Lemma
G.15
, we obtain that (for all
\eta
sufficiently small)

\displaystyle\inf_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J_{\sigma}=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{J^{\uparrow}=J^{\downarrow}>n\}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\downarrow}>n\}\Big)\cdot\frac{p\big(-\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta;(s^{-}_{k^{\prime}}+2\hat{\epsilon},s^{+}_{k^{\prime}}-2\hat{\epsilon})\big)}{p(-\hat{\epsilon},\delta,\eta)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\downarrow}>n\}\Big)\cdot\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}-C}{\nu^{\Omega}}.

(G.119)

uniformly for any
n=0,1,2,\cdots
due to
eq.

G.114
.

Besides, the following claim holds on event
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
.

•

From
eq.

G.109
, the definition of
\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
as well as the definition of event
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
(see
eq.

G.94
), one can see that for any
j=1,2,\cdots,J
, we have

\widetilde{\tau}_{j}\wedge\sigma(\eta)-T_{j,1}\leq\frac{2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)+\rho(\epsilon)}{\eta}.

•

Now if we turn to the interval
(\widetilde{\tau}_{j-1},T_{j,1}]
(the time between the start of the
j-
th attempt and the arrival of the first large noise during this attempt) for each
j=1,2,\cdots,J
, and the following sequence constructed by concatenating these intervals

\displaystyle\textbf{S}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}

\displaystyle\big(1,2,\cdots,T_{1,1},\widetilde{\tau}_{1}+1,\widetilde{\tau}_{1}+2,\cdots,T_{2,1},\cdots,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{k}+1,\widetilde{\tau}_{k}+2,\cdots,T_{k+1,1},\widetilde{\tau}_{k+1}+1,\widetilde{\tau}_{k+2}+1,\cdots\big),

then the discussion above have shown that, for

\displaystyle\min\{n\in\textbf{S}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\ Z_{n}\ \text{ has $\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)-$overflow}\}\geq T_{J,1}.

Meanwhile, from the definition of overflow we know that the probability that
Z_{1}
has
\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)-
overflow is equal to

\displaystyle H(\delta/\eta)p\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big).

•

Therefore, if, within the duration of each attempt, we split the attempt into two parts at the arrival time of the first large jump
(T_{k,1})_{k\geq 1}
at each attempt, and define (here the subscript
before
or
after
indicates that we are counting the steps before or after the first large jump in an attempt)

\displaystyle\textbf{S}_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{n\in\textbf{S}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\ n\leq\sigma(\eta)\},\ I_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\textbf{S}_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),

\displaystyle\textbf{S}_{\text{after}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{n\notin\textbf{S}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\ n\leq\sigma(\eta)\},\ I_{\text{after}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\textbf{S}_{\text{after}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),

then we have
\sigma(\eta)=I_{\text{before}}+I_{\text{after}}
. Moreover, the discussion above implies that

\displaystyle I_{\text{after}}

\displaystyle\leq J\big(2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)+\rho(\epsilon)\big)/\eta

\displaystyle I_{\text{before}}

\displaystyle\leq\min\{n\in\textbf{S}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\ Z_{n}\ \text{ has $\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)-$overflow}\}

and on event
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
.

Define geometric random variables with the following success rates

\displaystyle U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\sim\text{Geom}\Big(p\big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\big)\Big)

\displaystyle U_{2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\sim\text{Geom}\Big(H(\delta/\eta)p\big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\big)\Big).

Using results above to bound
I_{\text{before}}
and
I_{\text{after}}
separately on event
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
, we can show that (for all
\eta
sufficiently small and any
u>0
)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))+\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)\ \ \ \text{due to \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{proof prop first exit ineq 3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)I_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>(1-C)u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)I_{\text{after}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>Cu\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)I_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>(1-C)u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>Cu\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)I_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>(1-C)u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\cap\{J^{\downarrow}=J^{\uparrow}\}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{J^{\downarrow}<J^{\uparrow}\}\big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>Cu\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C+\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)U_{2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>(1-C)u\Big)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>Cu\Big)

(G.120)

where the last inequality follows from
eq.

G.117
and
eq.

G.118
.
Now let us analyze the probability terms on the last row of the display above. For the first term, let
a(\eta)=H(\delta/\eta)p\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)
. Due to Lemma
G.15
, we have (recall that
\nu^{\Omega}=\mu(E(0))
)

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{a(\eta)}{\lambda(\eta)\mu\big(E\big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon\big)\big)}=1.

Combining this with
eq.

G.110
, one can see that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)U_{2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>(1-C)u\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(a(\eta)\text{Geom}\big(a(\eta)\big)>(1-C)^{2}u\Big)\ \forall u>0.

Next, let
b(\eta,u)=\mathbb{P}\Big(a(\eta)\text{Geom}\big(a(\eta)\big)>(1-C)^{2}u\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\text{Geom}\big(a(\eta)\big)>\frac{(1-C)^{2}u}{a(\eta)}\Big)
. For
g(y)=\log(1-y)
, we know the existence of some
y_{0}>0
such that for all
y\in(0,y_{0})
, we have
\log(1-y)\leq-(1-C)y
. So one can see that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\log b(u,\eta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-C)^{2}u}{a(\eta)}\log(1-a(\eta))\leq-(1-C)^{3}u

\displaystyle\Rightarrow b(u,\eta)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-(1-C)^{3}u\big)

(G.121)

uniformly for all
u>0
.

For the second probability term, if we only consider
u\geq C
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>Cu\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>C^{2}\Big).

Using
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(\eta)
with
\alpha>1
, we get

\displaystyle p\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\xrightarrow{d}\text{Exp}(1)\ \ \ \text{as }\eta\downarrow 0

due to the nature of the Geometric random variable
U_{1}
. Besides, due to
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(\eta)
with
\alpha>1
and Lemma
G.15
, it is easy to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}}{p\Big(3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\Big)}=0.

Combining these results with Slutsky’s theorem, we now obtain

\displaystyle\mu(E(0))\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\xrightarrow{d}0\ \ \ \text{as }\eta\downarrow 0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{u\geq C}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mu(E(0))\lambda(\eta)\frac{\rho(\epsilon)+2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\cdot U_{1}(\delta,\eta)>Cu\Big)=0.

(G.122)

Plugging
eq.

G.121
and
eq.

G.122
back into
eq.

G.120
, we can establish the upper bound in
eq.

G.80
. To show
eq.

G.82
, note that for event

\displaystyle E(\epsilon,\eta)=\{\nu^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\tau(\eta,\epsilon)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\tau(\eta,\epsilon)}\in B(\widetilde{m}_{k^{\prime}},2\epsilon)\},

we have (for definitions of
\tau
, see
eq.

G.75
)

\displaystyle E(\epsilon,\eta)\supseteq

\displaystyle\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{X^{\eta}_{n}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\ \ \forall n\in[\sigma(\eta),\tau(\eta,\epsilon)]\},

\displaystyle E(\epsilon,\eta)\cap

\displaystyle\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=j\}\cap\{X^{\eta}_{n}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\ \ \forall n\in[\sigma(\eta),\tau(\eta,\epsilon)]\}=\emptyset\ \forall j\neq k^{\prime}.

Therefore, for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(E(\epsilon,\eta))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times})+\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\ \text{for some }n\in[\sigma(\eta),\tau(\eta,\epsilon)]\}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big((\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}\cap\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\{X^{\eta}_{n}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{J_{\sigma}(\eta)}\ \ \forall n\in[\sigma(\eta),\tau(\eta,\epsilon)]\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4C+\exp\big(-(1-C)^{3}u\big)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}}

uniformly for all
u\geq C
, due to
eq.

G.115
,
eq.

G.84
and
eq.

G.120
.

The lower bound can be shown by an almost identical approach. In particular, analogous to
eq.

G.120
, we can show that (for any
u>0
)

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-2\epsilon,2\epsilon]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\{v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)I_{\text{before}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>u,J_{\sigma}(\eta)=k^{\prime}\}\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{\Omega}\lambda(\eta)U_{2}^{\prime}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)>(1-C)u\Big)\frac{\nu^{\Omega}_{k^{\prime}}+C}{\nu^{\Omega}}-2C

due to
\mathbb{P}(E\char 92\relax F)\geq\mathbb{P}(E)-\mathbb{P}(F)
and
eq.

G.115
eq.

G.117
eq.

G.119

where

U^{\prime}_{2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\sim\text{Geom}\Big(H(\delta/\eta)p\big(-3(\bar{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}+3)\epsilon,\delta,\eta\big)\Big).

Using the similar argument leading to
eq.

G.121
, we are able to show
eq.

G.81
,
eq.

G.83
and conclude the proof.
∎

Recall that
\sigma_{i}(\eta)=\min\{n\geq 0:X_{n}\notin\Omega_{i}\}
and that value of constants
q_{i},q_{i,j}
are specified via
eq.

G.2
-
eq.

G.7
.
Define

\displaystyle\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq\sigma_{i}(\eta):X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{j}[m_{j}-2\epsilon,m_{j}+2\epsilon]\},

(G.123)

\displaystyle J_{i}(\eta)

\displaystyle=j\iff X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{i}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{j}\ \forall j\in[n_{\text{min}}].

(G.124)

The following result is simply a restatement of Proposition
G.23
under the new system of notations. Despite the reiteration, we still state it here because this is the version that will be used to prove Lemma
G.2
, which is the key tool for establishing Theorem
1
, as well as many other results in Section
H
.

Proposition G.25
.

Given
C>0
and
i,j\in[n_{\text{min}}]
such that
i\neq j
, the following claims hold for all
\epsilon>0
that are sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{u\in(C,\infty)}\sup_{x\in(m_{i}-2\epsilon,m_{i}+2\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{i}\lambda_{i}(\eta)\sigma_{i}(\eta)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{i}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\exp\big(-(1-C)u\big)\frac{q_{i,j}+C}{q_{i}},

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{u\in(C,\infty)}\inf_{x\in(m_{i}-2\epsilon,m_{i}+2\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{i}\lambda_{i}(\eta)\sigma_{i}(\eta)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{i}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-C+\exp\big(-(1+C)u\big)\frac{q_{i,j}-C}{q_{i}},

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{u\in(C,\infty)}\sup_{x\in(m_{i}-2\epsilon,m_{i}+2\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{i}\lambda_{i}(\eta)\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)}\in\Omega_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\exp\big(-(1-C)u\big)\frac{q_{i,j}+C}{q_{i}},

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{u\in(C,\infty)}\inf_{x\in(m_{i}-2\epsilon,m_{i}+2\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{i}\lambda_{i}(\eta)\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)}\in\Omega_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-C+\exp\big(-(1+C)u\big)\frac{q_{i,j}-C}{q_{i}},

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in(m_{i}-2\epsilon,m_{i}+2\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{i}\lambda_{i}(\eta)\big(\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)-\sigma_{i}(\eta)\big)<C,

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{n}\in\Omega_{J_{i}(\eta)}\ \forall n\in[\sigma_{i}(\eta),\tau^{\text{min}}_{i}(\eta,\epsilon)]\Big)\geq 1-C.

Concluding this section, we apply Proposition
G.25
and prove Lemma
G.2
.

Proof of Lemma
G.2
.

Fix some
C\in(0,1)
,
u>0
, and some
k,l\in[n_{\text{min}}]
with
k\neq l
. Let
q_{i},q_{i,j}
be the constants defined in
eq.

G.7
.

Fix some
C_{0}\in\big(0,\frac{C}{n_{\text{min}}}\wedge\frac{q_{k}}{n_{\text{min}}}C\big)
. Using Proposition
G.25
, we know that for all
\epsilon
sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in(m_{k}-2\epsilon,m_{k}+2\epsilon)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)>u,\ X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+\exp\big(-(1-C)u\big)\frac{q_{k,j}+C_{0}}{q_{k}}\ \forall j\in[n_{\text{min}}].

Summing up the inequality above over all
j\in[n_{\text{min}}]
, we can obtain
eq.

G.16
. The lower bound
eq.

G.17
can be established using an identical approach.

In order to show
eq.

G.19
, note that we can find
C_{1}\in(0,u)
sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle-C_{1}+\exp\big(-(1+C_{1})\cdot 2C_{1}\big)\frac{q_{k,l}-C_{1}}{q_{k}}\geq\frac{q_{k,l}-C}{q_{k}}.

Fix such
C_{1}
. From Proposition
G.25
, we also know that for all
\epsilon
small enough, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in(m_{k}-2\epsilon,m_{k}+2\epsilon)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)>u,X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{l}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq-C_{1}+\exp\big(-(1+C_{1})\cdot 2C_{1}\big)\frac{q_{k,l}-C_{1}}{q_{k}}.

Then using
