\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{l}\Big)\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)>u,X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{l}\Big)
we conclude the proof for
eq.

G.19
.

Moving on, we show
eq.

G.18
in the following way. Note that we can find
C_{2}\in(0,u)
small enough so that

\displaystyle 2C_{2}+\frac{q_{k,l}+C_{2}}{q_{k}}<\frac{q_{k,l}+C}{q_{k}}.

(G.125)

Fix such
C_{2}
. Since
eq.

G.17
has been established already, we can find some
u_{2}>0
such that for all
\epsilon
small enough,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in(m_{k}-2\epsilon,m_{k}+2\epsilon)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)\leq u_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle<C_{2}

(G.126)

Fix such
u_{2}
. Meanwhile, fix some
C_{3}\in(0,C_{2}\wedge u_{2})
. From Proposition
G.25
we know that for all
\epsilon
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in(m_{k}-2\epsilon,m_{k}+2\epsilon)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)>u_{2},\ X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{l}\Big)

(G.127)

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}+\exp\big(-(1-C_{3})u_{2}\big)\frac{q_{k,l}+C_{3}}{q_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}+\frac{q_{k,l}+C_{2}}{q_{k}}.

(G.128)

Lastly, observe the following decomposition of events (for any
x\in\Omega_{k}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{l}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)\leq u_{2}\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(q_{k}\lambda_{k}(\eta)\sigma_{k}(\eta)>u_{2},\ X^{\eta}_{\sigma_{k}(\eta)}\in\Omega_{l}\Big).

Combining this bound with
eq.

G.125
-
eq.

G.128
, we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix H
Proofs for Section
2.2

In this section, we show that gradient clipping scheme effectively partitions the entire optimization landscape of
f
into different regions based on the radius
r_{i}
and minimum jump number
l^{*}_{i}
for each attraction field
\Omega_{i}
.
Furthermore, when staying in each region, the behavior of SGD iterates closely resembles a Markov chain that
only
visits
wider
attraction fields in this region.
We exclude the trivial case where
n_{\text{min}}=1
and there is only one attraction field.

This structure is as follows. First we present some key lemmas that can be used to prove the Theorem
2
-
3
in the main paper. Then we devote the rest of the section to establish those lemmas. In order to prove Theorem
2
, we will make use of the following lemma, where we show that the type of claim in Theorem
2
is indeed valid if we look at a much shorter time interval. Then when we move onto the proof of Theorem
2
, it suffices to partition the entire horizon into pieces of these short time intervals, on each of which we analyze the dynamics of SGD respectively.

Lemma H.1
.

Assume the graph
\mathcal{G}
is
irreducible
, and let
\epsilon>0,\delta>0
be any positive real numbers. For the following random variables (indexed by
\eta
)

\displaystyle V^{\text{small}}(\eta,\epsilon,t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\frac{1}{\lfloor t/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}\int_{0}^{\lfloor t/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}\Big\{X^{\eta}_{\lfloor u\rfloor}\in\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\notin M^{\text{large}}}\Omega_{j}\Big\}du,

(H.1)

the following claim holds for any sufficiently small
t>0
:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(V^{\text{small}}(\eta,\epsilon,t)>\epsilon\Big)\leq 5\delta

Proof of Theorem
2
.

It suffices to show that for any
t>0,\kappa>1+(\alpha-1)l^{\text{large}},\epsilon>0,\delta\in(0,\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(V^{*}(\eta,t,\kappa)>3\epsilon\Big)<\delta

for

\displaystyle V^{*}(\eta,t,\kappa)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{1}{\lfloor t/\eta^{\kappa}\rfloor}\int_{0}^{\lfloor t/\eta^{\kappa}\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}\Big\{X^{\eta}_{\lfloor u\rfloor}(x)\in\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\notin M^{\text{large}}}\Omega_{j}\Big\}du.

Let us fix some
\epsilon>0,\delta\in(0,\epsilon)
. First, let

\displaystyle N(\eta)=\lceil\frac{\lfloor t/\eta^{\kappa}\rfloor}{\lfloor t/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}\rceil.

The regularly varying nature of
H
implies that
\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{1+l^{\text{large}}(\alpha-1)}(\eta)
. Since
\kappa>1+l^{\text{large}}(\alpha-1)
, we know that
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}N(\eta)=\infty
. Next, due to Lemma
H.1
, we can find
t_{0}>0
and
\bar{\eta}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta})

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{y}(V^{\text{small}}(\eta,\epsilon,t_{0})>\epsilon)<\delta.

(H.2)

For any
k\geq 1
, define

\displaystyle V_{k}(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\frac{1}{\lfloor t_{0}/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}\int_{(k-1)\lfloor t_{0}/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}^{k\lfloor t_{0}/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}\Big\{X^{\eta}_{\lfloor u\rfloor}\in\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\notin M^{\text{large}}}\Omega_{j}\Big\}du.

(H.3)

It is clear from its definition that
V_{k}
stands for the proportion of time that the SGD iterates are outside of
large
attraction fields on the interval
[(k-1)\lfloor\frac{t_{0}}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}\rfloor,k\lfloor\frac{t_{0}}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}\rfloor]
.
From
eq.

H.2
and Markov property, one can see that for any
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta})

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(V_{k}(\eta)>\epsilon\ |\ X^{\eta}_{0},\cdots,X^{\eta}_{(k-1)\lfloor t_{0}/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor})\leq\delta

uniformly for all
k\geq 1
. Now define
K(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\{n=1,2,\cdots,N(\eta):\ V_{k}(\eta)>\epsilon\}
. By a simple stochastic dominance argument, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K(\eta)\geq j)\leq\mathbb{P}(\text{Binomial}(N(\eta),\delta)\geq j)\ \ \forall j=1,2,\cdots.

Meanwhile, strong law of large numbers implies the existence of some
\bar{\eta}_{1}>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(\frac{\text{Binomial}(N(\eta),\delta)}{N(\eta)}>2\delta)<\delta
for all
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}_{1})
, thus

\displaystyle\sup_{|x|\leq L}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K(\eta)/N(\eta)>2\delta)\leq\delta\ \ \forall\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}_{1}\wedge\bar{\eta}).

Lastly, from the definition of
K(\eta)
and
N(\eta)
, we know that for all the
N(\eta)
intervals
[(k-1)\lfloor\frac{t_{0}}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}\rfloor,k\lfloor\frac{t_{0}}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}\rfloor]
with
k\in[N(\eta)]
, only on
K(\eta)
of them did the SGD iterates spent more then
\epsilon
proportion of time outside of the
large
attraction fields, hence

\displaystyle V^{*}(\eta,t,\kappa)\leq\epsilon+\frac{K(\eta)}{N(\eta)}.

In summary, we now have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(V^{*}(\eta,t,\kappa)>3\epsilon)<\delta

for all
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}_{1}\wedge\bar{\eta}).
This concludes the proof.
∎

When introducing Theorem
3
in the main paper, we stated that the results of eliminating sharp minima can be extended to the more general reducible case. Here we present the corresponding theoretical results in Theorem
H.2
and
H.3
below.
The main message can be summarized as follows:
(a) SGD with truncated and heavy-tailed noise naturally partitions the entire training landscape into different regions;
(b) In each region, the dynamics of
X^{\eta}_{n}
for small
\eta
closely resemble that of a continuous-time Markov chain that
only
visits local minima;
(3) In particular, any sharp minima within each region is almost completely avoided by SGD.

When the typical transition graph (see Definition
1
in the main paper) is not irreducible, there will be multiple communication classes on the graph. Suppose that there are
K
communication classes
G_{1},\cdots,G_{K}
. From now on, we zoom in on a specific communication class
G\in\{G_{1},\cdots,G_{K}\}
.
For this communication class
G
, define
l^{*}_{G}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\max\{l^{*}_{i}:\ i=1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}};\ m_{i}\in G\}.

For each local minimum
m_{i}\in G
, we call its attraction field
\Omega_{i}
a
large
attraction field if
l^{*}_{i}=l^{*}_{G}
, and a
small
attraction field if
l^{*}_{i}<l^{*}_{G}
. We have thus classified all
m_{i}
in
G
into two groups: the ones in large attraction fields
m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}}
and the ones in small attraction fields
m^{\text{small}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{small}}_{i^{\prime}_{G}}
. Also, define a scaling function
\lambda_{G}
associated with
G
as
\lambda_{G}(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}H(1/\eta)\Big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)^{l^{*}_{G}-1}.

Theorem H.2
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
, if
G
is
absorbing
, then there exists a continuous-time Markov chain
Y
on
\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}}\}
such that for any
x\in\Omega_{i},|x|\leq L
(where
i\in\{1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}\}
) with
m_{i}\in G
, and

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{\lfloor t/\lambda_{G}(\eta)\rfloor}(x)\rightarrow Y_{t}(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))\ \ \ \text{as }\eta\downarrow 0

in the sense of finite-dimensional distributions, where
\pi_{G}
is a random mapping satisfying (1)
\pi_{G}(m)\equiv m
if
m\in\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}}\}
; (2)
\pi_{G}(m)
is a random variable that only takes value in
\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}}\}
if
m\in\{m^{\text{small}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{small}}_{i^{\prime}_{G}}\}
.

We stress that Theorem
3
in the main paper follows immediately from Theorem
H.2
above.

Next, to state the corresponding result for a
transient
communication class
G
, we introduce a couple of extra definitions.
We consider a version of
X^{\eta}_{n}
that is killed when
X^{\eta}_{n}
leaves
G
. Define stopping time

\displaystyle\tau_{G}(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\in G}\Omega_{i}\}

(H.4)

as the first time the SGD iterates leave all attraction fields in
G
, and we use a cemetery state
\bm{\dagger}
to construct the following process
X^{\dagger,\eta}_{n}
as a version of
X^{\eta}_{n}
with killing at
\tau_{G}
:

\displaystyle X^{\dagger,\eta}_{n}=\begin{cases}X^{\eta}_{n}&\text{if }\ n<\tau_{G}(\eta),\\
\bm{\dagger}&\text{if }\ n\geq\tau_{G}(\eta).\end{cases}

(H.5)

Theorem H.3
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
, if
G
is
transient
, then there exists a continuous-time Markov chain
Y

with killing
that has state space
\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}},\bm{\dagger}\}
(we say the Markov chain
Y
is killed when it enters the absorbing cemetery state
\bm{\dagger}
) such that for any
x\in\Omega_{i},|x|\leq L
(where
i\in\{1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}\}
) with
m_{i}\in G
, and

\displaystyle X_{\lfloor t/\lambda_{G}(\eta)\rfloor}^{\dagger,\eta}(x)\rightarrow Y_{t}(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))\ \ \ \text{as }\eta\downarrow 0

in the sense of finite-dimensional distributions, where
\pi_{G}
is a random mapping satisfying (1)
\pi_{G}(m)\equiv m
if
m\in\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}}\}
; (2)
\pi_{G}(m)
is a random variable that only takes value in
\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}},\bm{\dagger}\}
if
m\in\{m^{\text{small}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{small}}_{i^{\prime}_{G}}\}
.

To show Theorem
H.2
and
H.3
, we introduce the following concepts. First, we consider the case where the SGD iterates
X^{\eta}_{n}
is initialized on the communication class
G
and
G
is absorbing. For some
\Delta>0,\eta>0
, define (let
B(u,v)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}[u-v,u+v]
)

\displaystyle\sigma^{G}_{0}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{i:\ m_{i}\in G}B(m_{i},2\Delta)\}

(H.6)

\displaystyle\tau^{G}_{0}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq\sigma^{G}_{0}(\eta,\Delta):\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\notin G^{\text{small}}}B(m_{i},2\Delta)\}

(H.7)

\displaystyle I^{G}_{0}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle=j\iff X^{\eta}_{\tau^{G}_{0}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta),\ \ \ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{0}(\eta,\Delta)=j\iff X^{\eta}_{\sigma^{G}_{0}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)

(H.8)

\displaystyle\sigma^{G}_{k}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n>\tau^{G}_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta):\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\in G,\ i\neq I^{G}_{k-1}}B(m_{i},2\Delta)\}\ \forall k\geq 1

(H.9)

\displaystyle\tau^{G}_{k}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq\sigma^{G}_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta):\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\notin G^{\text{small}}}B(m_{i},2\Delta)\}\ \forall k\geq 1

(H.10)

\displaystyle I^{G}_{k}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle=j\iff X^{\eta}_{\tau^{G}_{k}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta),\ \ \ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}(\eta,\Delta)=j\iff X^{\eta}_{\sigma^{G}_{k}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\forall k\geq 1.

(H.11)

Intuitively speaking, at each
\tau^{G}_{k}
the SGD iterates visits a minimizer that is not in a
small
attraction field on
G
, and we use
I^{G}_{k}
to mark the label of that large attraction field. Stopping time
\sigma^{G}_{k}
is the first time that SGD visits a minimizer that is
different
from the one visited at
\tau^{G}_{k}
, and
\tau^{G}_{k+1}
is the first time that a minimizer not in a small attraction field of
G
is visited again since
\sigma^{G}_{k}
(and including
\sigma^{G}_{k}
). It is worth mentioning that, under this definition, we could have
I^{G}_{k}=I^{G}_{k+1}
for any
k\geq 0
. Meanwhile, define the following process that only keeps track of the updates on the labels
(I^{G}_{k})_{k\geq 0}
instead of the information of the entire trajectory of
(X^{\eta}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
:

\displaystyle\hat{X}^{\eta,\Delta}_{n}=\begin{cases}\phantom{-}m_{I^{G}_{k}}&\text{if }\exists k\geq 0\ \text{such that }\tau^{G}_{k}\leq n<\tau^{G}_{k+1}\\
\ \ 0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(H.12)

In other words, when
n<\tau^{G}_{0}
we simply let
\hat{X}^{\eta,\Delta}_{n}=0
, otherwise it is equal to the latest “marker” for the last visited wide minimum up until step
n
. This marker process
\hat{X}
jumps between the different minimizers of the large attractions in
G
. In particular, if for some
n
we have
X^{\eta}_{n}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)
for some
j
with
m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}
, then we must have
\hat{X}^{\eta,\Delta}_{n}=m_{j}
, which implies that, in this case,
\hat{X}^{\eta,\Delta}_{n}
indeed indicates the location of
X^{\eta}_{n}
.

Note that results in Theorem
H.2
and
H.3
concern a
scaled
version of
X^{\eta}
. Here we also define the corresponding
scaled
version of the processes

\displaystyle X^{*,\eta}_{t}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}X^{\eta}_{\lfloor t/\lambda_{G}(\eta)\rfloor}

(H.13)

\displaystyle\hat{X}^{*,\eta,\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\hat{X}^{\eta,\Delta}_{\lfloor t/\lambda_{G}(\eta)\rfloor},

(H.14)

a mapping
\textbf{T}^{*}(n,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}n\lambda_{G}(\eta)
that translates a step
n
to the corresponding timestamp for the scaled processes, and the following series of scaled stopping times

\displaystyle\tau^{*}_{k}(\eta,\Delta)=\textbf{T}^{*}\big(\tau^{G}_{k}(\eta,\Delta),\eta\big),\ \sigma^{*}_{k}(\eta,\Delta)=\textbf{T}^{*}\big(\sigma^{G}_{k}(\eta,\Delta),\eta\big).

(H.15)

Before presenting the proof of Theorem
H.2
and
H.3
, we make several preparations. First, our proof is inspired by ideas in
Pavlyukevich 2005
and here we provide a briefing. At any time
t>0
, if we can show that
X^{*,\eta}_{t}
is almost always in set
\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{i},2\Delta)
(so the SGD iterates is almost always close to a minimizer in a large attraction field), then the marker process
\hat{X}^{*,\eta,\Delta}_{t}
is a pretty accurate indicator of the location of
X^{*,\eta}_{t}
, so it suffices to show that the marker process
\hat{X}^{*,\eta,\Delta}_{t}
converges to a continuous-time Markov chain
Y
.

Second, we construct the limiting process
Y
and the random mapping
\pi_{G}
before utilizing them in Theorem
H.2
and
H.3
. As an important building block for this purpose, we start by considering the following discrete time Markov chain (DTMC) on the entire graph
\mathcal{G}=(V,E)
. Let
\textbf{P}^{DTMC}
be a transition matrix with

\textbf{P}^{DTMC}(m_{i},m_{j})=\mu_{i}(E_{i,j})/\mu_{i}(E_{i})
for all
j\neq i
, and
Y^{DTMC}=(Y^{DTMC}_{j})_{j\geq 0}
be the DTMC induced by the said transition matrix. Let

\displaystyle T^{DTMC}_{G}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{j\geq 0:\ Y^{DTMC}_{j}\notin G^{\text{small}}\}

(H.16)

be the first time this DTMC visits a large attraction field on the communication class
G
, or escapes from
G
. Lastly, define (for any
j
such that
m_{j}\notin G^{\text{small}}
)

\displaystyle p_{i,j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mathbb{P}\big(Y^{DTMC}_{T^{DTMC}_{G}}(m_{i})=m_{j}\big)

(H.17)

as the probability that the first large attraction field on
G
visited by
Y^{DTMC}
is
m_{j}
when initialized at
m_{i}
.

We add a comment regarding the stopping times
T^{DTMC}_{G}
and probabilities
p_{i,j}
defined above. In the case that
G
is absorbing, we have
Y^{DTMC}_{j}(m_{i})\in G
for all
j\geq 0
if
m_{i}\in G
. Therefore, in this case, given any
i
with
m_{i}\in G
, we must have

\displaystyle T^{DTMC}_{G}=\min\{j\geq 0:\ Y^{DTMC}_{j}(m_{i})\in G^{\text{large}}\},\ \ \ \sum_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}p_{i,j}=1.

On the contrary, when
G
is transient we may have
\sum_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}p_{i,j}<1
and
\sum_{j:\ m_{j}\notin G}p_{i,j}>0
. Lastly, whether
G
is absorbing or transient, we always have
p_{i,j}=\mathbbm{1}\{i=j\}
if
m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}}
.

Next, consider the following definition of (continuous-time) jump processes.

Definition H.1
.

A continuous-time process
Y_{t}
on
\mathbb{R}
is a
\Big((U_{j})_{j\geq 0},(V_{j})_{j\geq 0}\Big)

jump process
if

Y_{t}=\begin{cases}\phantom{-}0&\text{if }t<U_{0}\\
\sum_{j\geq 0}V_{j}\mathbbm{1}_{[U_{0}+U_{1}+\cdots+U_{j},\ U_{0}+U_{1}+\cdots+U_{j+1})}(t)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

where
(U_{j})_{j\geq 0}
is a sequence of non-negative random variables such that
U_{j}>0\ \forall j\geq 1
almost surely, and
(V_{j})_{j\geq 0}
is a sequence of random variables in
\mathbb{R}
.

Obviously, the definition above implies that
Y_{t}=V_{j}
for any
t\in[U_{j},U_{j+1})
.

Now we are ready to construct the limiting continuous-time Markov chain
Y
. To begin with, we address the case where
G
is absorbing. For any
m^{\prime}\in G^{\text{large}}
, let
Y(m^{\prime})
be a
\big((S_{k})_{k\geq 0},(W_{k})_{k\geq 0}\big)
-jump process where
S_{0}=0,W_{0}=m^{\prime}
and (for all
k\geq 0
and
i,j
with
m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},m_{j}\notin G^{\text{small}}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(W_{k+1}=m_{j},\ S_{k+1}>t\ \Big|\ W_{k}=m_{i},\ (W_{l})_{l=0}^{k-1},\ (S_{l})_{l=0}^{k}\Big)

(H.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(W_{k+1}=m_{j},\ S_{k+1}>t\ \Big|\ W_{k}=m_{i}\Big)=\exp(-q_{i}t)\frac{q_{i,j}}{q_{i}}\ \forall t>0

(H.19)

where

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}(E_{i})

(H.20)

\displaystyle q_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}\{i\neq j\}\mu_{i}(E_{i,j})+\sum_{k:\ m_{k}\in G^{\text{small}}}\mu_{i}(E_{i,k})p_{k,j}

(H.21)

and
p_{k,j}
is defined in
eq.

H.17
. In other words, conditioning on
W_{k}=m_{i}
, the time until next jump
S_{k+1}
and the jump location
W_{k+1}
are independent, where
S_{k+1}
is Exp
(q_{i})
and
W_{k+1}=m_{j}
with probability
q_{i,j}/q_{i}
. First, it is easy to see that
Y
is a continuous-time Markov chain. Second, under this definition
Y
is allowed to have some
dummy
jumps where
W_{k}=W_{k+1}
: in this case the process
Y_{t}
does not move to a different minimizer after the
k+1
-th jump, and by inspecting the path of
Y
we cannot tell that this dummy jump has occurred. As a result, that generator
Q
of this Markov chain admits the form (for all
i\neq j
with
m_{i},m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}
)

\displaystyle Q_{i,i}=-\sum_{k:k\neq i,\ m_{k}\in G^{\text{large}}}q_{i,k},\ Q_{i,j}=q_{i,j}.

Moreover, define the following random function
\pi_{G}(\cdot)
such that for any
m_{i}\in G
,

\displaystyle\pi_{G}(m_{i})=\begin{cases}\phantom{-}m_{j}&\text{with probability $q_{i,j}/q_{i}$ if }m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\\
\phantom{-}m_{i}&\text{if }m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}}\end{cases}

(H.22)

By
Y(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))
we refer to the version of the Markov chain
Y
where we randomly initialize
W_{0}=\pi_{G}(m_{i})
. The following lemma is the key tool for proving Theorem
H.2
.

Lemma H.4
.

Assume that the communication class
G
is absorbing. Given any
m_{i}\in G
,
x\in\Omega_{i}
, finitely many real numbers
(t_{l})_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}
such that
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{k^{\prime}}
, and a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow 0}\eta_{n}=0
, there exists a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that

•

As
n
tends to
\infty
,

\displaystyle\big(\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{1}}(x),\cdots,\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k^{\prime}}}(x)\big)\Rightarrow\big(Y_{t_{1}}(\pi_{G}(m_{i})),\cdots,Y_{t_{k^{\prime}}}(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))\big)

(H.23)

•

For all
k\in[k^{\prime}]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\Big)=0.

(H.24)

Now we address the case where
G
is transient, let
\bm{\dagger}
be a real number such that
\bm{\dagger}\notin[-L,L]
, and we use
\dagger
as the cemetery state since the processes
X^{\eta}_{n}
or
X^{*,\eta}_{t}
are restricted on
[-L,L]
. Recall the definition of
\tau_{G}
defined in
eq.

H.4
. Analogous to the process
X^{\dagger}
in
eq.

H.5
, we can also define

\displaystyle X^{\dagger,*,\eta}_{t}=\begin{cases}X^{*,\eta}_{t}&\text{if }\ t<\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta),\eta)\\
\bm{\dagger}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},\ \ \hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta,\Delta}_{t}=\begin{cases}\hat{X}^{*,\eta,\Delta}_{t}&\text{if }\ t<\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta),\eta)\\
\bm{\dagger}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}.

(H.25)

Next, analogous to
\tau_{G}
, consider the stopping time

\displaystyle\tau^{Y}_{G}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{t>0:Y_{t}\notin G\}.

When
G
is transient, due to the construction of
Y
we know that
\tau^{Y}_{G}<\infty
almost surely. The introduction of
\tau^{Y}_{G}
allows us to define

\displaystyle Y^{\dagger}_{t}=\begin{cases}Y_{t}&\text{if }\ t<\tau^{Y}_{G}\\
\bm{\dagger}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(H.26)

The following Lemma will be used to prove Theorem
H.3
.

Lemma H.5
.

Assume that the communication class
G
is transient. Given any
m_{i}\in G
,
x\in\Omega_{i}
, finitely many real numbers
(t_{l})_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}
such that
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{k^{\prime}}
, and a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow 0}\eta_{n}=0
, there exists a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that

•

As
n
tends to
\infty
,

\displaystyle\big(\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{1}}(x),\cdots,\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k^{\prime}}}(x)\big)\Rightarrow\big(Y^{\dagger}_{t_{1}}(\pi_{G}(m_{i})),\cdots,Y^{\dagger}_{t_{k^{\prime}}}(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))\big)

(H.27)

•

For all
k\in[k^{\prime}]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\text{ and }X^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\neq\bm{\dagger}\Big)=0.

(H.28)

Proof of Theorem
H.2
and
H.3
.

We first address the case where
G
is absorbing. Arbitrarily choose some
\Delta>0
, a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\eta_{n}=0
, a positive integer
k^{\prime}
, a series of real numbers
(t_{j})_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}
with
0<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{k^{\prime}}
, and a sequence
(w_{j})_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}
with
w_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}
for all
j\in[k^{\prime}]
. It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\in B(w_{k},\Delta)\ \forall k\in[k^{\prime}]\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(Y_{t_{k}}(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[k^{\prime}]\Big).

Using Lemma
H.4
, we can find a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that
eq.

H.23
and
eq.

H.24
hold. From the weak convergence in
eq.

H.23
, we only need to show

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin B(\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k}},\Delta)\Big)=0\ \forall k\in[k^{\prime}].

For all
n
large enough, we have
2\Delta_{n}<\Delta
. For such large
n
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin B(\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k}},\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},2\Delta_{n})\Big)

(due to definition of the marker process
\hat{X}
, see
eq.

H.6
-
eq.

H.11
and
eq.

H.12
-
eq.

H.14
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\Big)

and by applying
eq.

H.24
we conclude the proof for Theorem
H.2
.

The proof of Theorem
H.3
is almost identical, with the only modification being that we apply Lemma
H.5
instead of Lemma
H.4
. In doing so, we are able to find a sequence of
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that
eq.

H.27
and
eq.

H.28
hold. Given the weak convergence claim in
eq.

H.27
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin B(\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k}},\Delta)\Big)=0\ \forall k\in[k^{\prime}].

If
X^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}=\bm{\dagger}
, we must have
\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k}}=\bm{\dagger}
as well. Therefore, for all
n
large enough so that
3\Delta_{n}<\Delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin B(\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k}},\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin B(\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t_{k}},\Delta),\ X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\neq\bm{\dagger}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},2\Delta_{n}),\ X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\neq\bm{\dagger}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n}),\ X^{\dagger*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\neq\bm{\dagger}\Big).

Apply
eq.

H.28
and we conclude the proof.
∎

H.1
Proof of Lemma
H.1

First, we introduce another dichotomy for
small
and
large
noises. For any
\widetilde{\gamma}>0
and any learning rate
\eta>0
, we say that a noise
Z_{n}
is small if

\displaystyle\eta|Z_{n}|>\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}}

and we say
Z_{n}
is large otherwise. For this new classification of small and large noises, we introduce the following notations and definitions:

\displaystyle Z^{\leq,\widetilde{\gamma},\eta}_{n}

\displaystyle=Z_{n}\mathbbm{1}\{\eta|Z_{n}|\leq\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\},

(H.29)

\displaystyle Z^{>,\widetilde{\gamma},\eta}_{n}

\displaystyle=Z_{n}\mathbbm{1}\{\eta|Z_{n}|>\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\},

(H.30)

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}^{\eta}_{1}(\widetilde{\gamma})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 1:\ \eta|Z_{n}|>\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\}.

(H.31)

Similar to Lemma
G.12
, the following result is a direct application of Lemma
G.11
, and shows that it is rather unlikely to observe large perturbation that are caused only by
small
noises. Specifically, since
\alpha>1
we can always find

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}

\displaystyle\in(0,(1-\frac{1}{\alpha}\wedge\frac{1}{2})

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle\in\big(1,(2-2\widetilde{\gamma})\wedge\alpha(1-\widetilde{\gamma})\big).

Now in Lemma
G.11
, if we let
\Delta=\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\Delta}=\Delta/2
and
\epsilon=\delta=1
(in other words,
u(\eta)=1/\eta^{1-\widetilde{\gamma}},\ v(\eta)=\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}/2}
), then for any positive integer
j
the condition
eq.

G.52
is satisfied, allowing us to draw the following conclusion immediately as a corollary from Lemma
G.11
.

Lemma H.6
.

Given
N>0
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})\wedge\frac{1}{2}),\ \beta\in\big(1,(2-2\widetilde{\gamma})\wedge(\alpha-\alpha\widetilde{\gamma})\big),

we have (as
\eta\downarrow 0)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{j=1,2,\cdots,\lceil(1/\eta)^{\beta}\rceil}\eta|Z^{\leq,\widetilde{\gamma},\eta}_{1}+\cdots+Z^{\leq,\widetilde{\gamma},\eta}_{j}|>\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}/2}\Big)=o(\eta^{N}).

The flavor of the next lemma is similar to that of Lemma
G.13
. Specifically, we show that, with high probability, the SGD iterates would quickly return to the local minimum as long as they start from somewhere that are not too close the boundary of an attraction field (namely, the points
s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{n_{\text{min}}}
). To this end, we consider a refinement of function
\hat{t}(\cdot)
defined in
eq.

G.38
. For any
i=1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}
, any
x\in\Omega_{i}
and any
\eta>0,\gamma\in(0,1)
, we can define the return time to
\eta^{\gamma}-
neighborhood for the ODE
\textbf{x}^{\eta}
as

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{(i)}_{\gamma}(x,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{t\geq 0:|\textbf{x}^{\eta}(t,x)-m_{i}|\leq\eta^{\gamma}\}.

Given the bound in
eq.

G.37
(which is stated for a specific attraction field) and the fact that there only exists finitely many attraction fields, we know the existence of some
c_{2}<\infty
such that for any
i=1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}
, any
\eta>0
, any
\gamma\in(0,1)
and any
x\in\Omega_{i}
such that
|x-s_{i}|\vee|x-s_{i-1}|>\eta^{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{t}^{(i)}_{\gamma}(x,\eta)\leq c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta

and define function
t^{\uparrow}
as

\displaystyle t^{\uparrow}(\eta,\gamma)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta).

Lastly, define the following stopping time for any
i=1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}
, any
x\in\Omega_{i}
and any
\Delta>0

\displaystyle T^{(i)}_{\text{return}}(\eta,\Delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)\}

where we adopt the notation
B(u,v)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}[u-v,u+v]
for the
v-
neighborhood around point
u
.

Lemma H.7
.

Given

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})\wedge(\frac{1}{2})),\ \gamma\in(0,\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{16Mc_{2}}\wedge\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{4}),

and any
i=1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}
, any
\Delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in\Omega_{i}:|x-s_{i-1}|\vee|x-s_{i}|\geq 2\eta^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T^{(i)}_{\text{return}}(\eta,\Delta)\leq\frac{2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)}{\eta},\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\Omega_{i}\ \forall n\leq T^{(i)}_{\text{return}}(\eta,\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle=1.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we only consider
\eta
small enough such that

\displaystyle 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta<\lceil(1/\eta)^{\beta}\rceil,\ \ \eta M\leq\eta^{2\widetilde{\gamma}},\ \ 2\eta^{\gamma}<\Delta/2,\ \ 2\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}/4}<\eta^{\gamma}.

(H.32)

The condition above holds for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small because
\beta>1
,
2\widetilde{\gamma}<1
, and
\gamma<\widetilde{\gamma}/4
. Also, fix some
\beta\in\big(1,(2-2\widetilde{\gamma})\wedge(\alpha-\alpha\widetilde{\gamma})\big)

Define the following events

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\max_{j=1,2,\cdots,\lceil(1/\eta)^{\beta}\rceil}\eta|Z^{\leq,\widetilde{\gamma},\eta}_{1}+\cdots+Z^{\leq,\widetilde{\gamma},\eta}_{j}|>\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}/2}\Big\}

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\widetilde{T}^{\eta}_{1}(\widetilde{\gamma})\leq\lceil(1/\eta)^{\beta}\rceil\}\ \ \ \text{(see \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{def tilde T arrival time of large jump rescaled} for definition of the stopping time involved)}

and fix some
N>0
. From Lemma
H.6
, we see that (for all sufficiently small
\eta
)

\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta))\leq\eta^{N}.

Besides, using Lemma
G.4
together with the fact that
\beta<\alpha(1-\widetilde{\gamma})
, we know the existence of some constant
\theta>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{2}(\eta))\leq\eta^{\theta}

for all sufficiently small
\eta
.

Now we focus on the behavior of the SGD iterates on event
\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cap A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\Big)^{c}
. Let us arbitrarily choose some
x\in\Omega_{i}
such that
|x-s_{i}|\vee|x-s_{i-1}|>2\eta^{\gamma}
. First, from Lemma
G.6
and
eq.

H.32
, we know that

\displaystyle|\textbf{x}^{\eta}_{t}(x)-\textbf{y}^{\eta}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta M\exp\big(2Mc_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta^{2\widetilde{\gamma}-2Mc_{2}\gamma}\leq 2\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\leq\eta^{\gamma}\ \ \forall t\leq 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta

(H.33)

Next, from the definition of the function
t^{\uparrow}(\cdot)
and
eq.

H.33
, we know that for

\displaystyle T_{\text{GD,return}}(x;\eta,\Delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:\textbf{y}^{\eta}_{n}(x)\in B(m_{i},\frac{\Delta}{2}+\eta^{\gamma})\},

(H.34)

we have

\displaystyle T_{\text{GD,return}}(x;\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta

(H.35)

\displaystyle\textbf{y}^{\eta}_{n}>s_{i-1}+\eta^{\gamma},\ \textbf{y}^{\eta}_{n}

\displaystyle<s_{i}-\eta^{\gamma}\ \ \forall n\leq 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta.

(H.36)

Furthermore, on event
\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cap A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\Big)^{c}
, due to Lemma
G.5
and
eq.

H.32
, we have that

\displaystyle|X^{\eta}_{n}(x)-\textbf{y}^{\eta}_{n}(x)|\leq\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}/2}\exp\big(2Mc_{2}\log(1/\eta)\big)=\eta^{\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{2}-2Mc_{2}\gamma}\leq\eta^{\widetilde{\gamma}/4}<\eta^{\gamma}\ \ \forall n\leq 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta.

Combining this with
eq.

H.35
,
eq.

H.36
, we can conclude that (recall that due to
eq.

H.32
we have
2\eta^{\gamma}<\Delta/2
)

\displaystyle T^{(i)}_{\text{return}}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{n}\in\Omega_{i}\ \

\displaystyle\forall n\leq 2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta

on event
\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cap A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\Big)^{c}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in\Omega_{i}:|x-s_{i-1}|\vee|x-s_{i}|\geq 2\eta^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T^{(i)}_{\text{return}}(\eta,\Delta)\leq\frac{2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)}{\eta},\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\Omega_{i}\ \forall n\leq T^{(i)}_{\text{return}}(\eta,\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cap A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\Big)^{c}\Big)\geq\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}1-\eta^{N}-\eta^{\theta}=1.

This concludes the proof.
∎

The takeaway of the next lemma is that, almost always, the SGD iterates will quickly escape from the neighborhood of any
s_{i}
, the boundaries of each attraction fields.

Lemma H.8
.

Given any
\gamma\in(0,1),t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\min\big\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{i}B(s_{i},2\eta^{\gamma})\big\}\leq\frac{t}{H(1/\eta)}\Big)=1.

Proof.

We only consider
\eta
small enough so that

\displaystyle\min_{i=2,3,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}-1}|s_{i}-s_{i-1}|

\displaystyle>3\eta^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}},

\displaystyle\eta M

\displaystyle<\eta^{\gamma}.

Also, the claim is trivial if
x\notin\cup_{j}B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})
, so without loss of generality we only consider the case where there is some
j\in[n_{\text{min}}]
and
x\in[-L,L],x\in B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})
.
Let us define stopping times

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 1:\eta|Z_{n}|>5\eta^{\gamma}\};

(H.37)

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\cup_{j}B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})\},

(H.38)

and the following two events

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{T^{\gamma}>\frac{t}{H(1/\eta)}\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\eta|Z_{T^{\gamma}}|>\eta^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\}.

First, using Lemma
G.3
and the regularly varying nature of
H(\cdot)=\mathbb{P}(|Z_{1}|>\cdot)
, we know the existence of some
\theta>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta))\leq\exp(-1/\eta^{\theta})

for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small. Next, by definition of
T^{\gamma}
, one can see that (for any
\eta\in(0,1)
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{2}(\eta))

\displaystyle=\frac{H(1/\eta^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}})}{H(5/\eta^{1-\gamma})}.

Again, due to
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}
and
1-\gamma>0
, we know the existence of some
\theta_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}_{2}(\eta))<\eta^{\theta_{1}}

for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small. To conclude the proof, we only need to note the following fact on event
\big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\big)^{c}
. There are only two possibilities on this event:
T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}\leq T^{\gamma}-1
, or
T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}\geq T^{\gamma}
. Now we analyze the two cases respectively.

•

On
\big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\big)^{c}\cap\{T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}\leq T^{\gamma}-1\}
, we must have
T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}<T^{\gamma}\leq t/H(1/\eta)
.

•

On
\big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\big)^{c}\cap\{T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}\geq T^{\gamma}\}
, we know that at
n=T^{\gamma}-1
, there exists an integer
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,n_{\text{min}}-1\}
such that
X^{\eta}_{n}\in B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})
. Now since
\eta M<\eta^{\gamma}
and
\eta|Z_{T^{\gamma}}|>5\eta^{\gamma}
, we must have

\displaystyle|X^{\eta}_{T^{\gamma}}-X^{\eta}_{T^{\gamma}-1}|>4\eta^{\gamma}\Rightarrow X^{\eta}_{T^{\gamma}}\notin B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma}).

On the other hand, the exclusion of event
A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)
tells us that
|X^{\eta}_{T^{\gamma}}-X^{\eta}_{T^{\gamma}-1}|<2\eta^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}
. Due to
eq.

H.37
, we then have
X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\cup_{i}B(s_{i},2\eta^{\gamma})
.

In summary,
\big(A^{\times}_{1}(\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\eta)\big)^{c}\subseteq\{T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}\leq t/H(1/\eta)\}
and this conclude the proof.
∎

In the next lemma, we analyze the number of transitions needed to visit a certain local minimizer in the loss landscape. In general, we focus on a communication class
G
and, for now, assume it is absorbing. Next, we introduce the following concepts to record the transitions between different local minimum. To be specific, for any
\eta>0
and any
\Delta>0
small enough so that
B(m_{j},\Delta)\cap\Omega_{j}^{c}=\emptyset
for all
j
, define

\displaystyle T_{0}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n\geq 0:\ X_{n}^{\eta}\in\cup_{j}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\};

(H.39)

\displaystyle I_{0}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle=j\text{ iff }X^{\eta}_{T_{0}(\eta,\Delta)}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta);

(H.40)

\displaystyle T_{k}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n>T_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta):\ X_{n}^{\eta}\in\cup_{j\neq I_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta)}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\}\ \ \forall k\geq 1

(H.41)

\displaystyle I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle=j\text{ iff }X^{\eta}_{T_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\ \ \forall k\geq 1.

(H.42)

As mentioned earlier, the next goal is to analyze the transitions between attraction fields it takes to visit
m_{j}
when starting from
m_{i}
when
m_{i},m_{j}\in G
. Define

\displaystyle K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{k\geq 0:\ I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)=i\}.

Lemma H.9
.

Assume that
G
is an absorbing communication class on the graph
\mathcal{G}
. Then there exists some constant
p>0
such that for any
i
with
m_{i}\in G
, any
\epsilon>0
, and any
\Delta>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})\leq\mathbb{P}(\text{Geom}(p)\geq u)+\epsilon\ \ \forall u=1,2,\cdots,

\displaystyle\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists k\in[K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)]\ s.t.\ m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}\notin G\Big)\leq\epsilon

hold for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small.

Proof.

The claim is trivial if, for the initial condition, we have
x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)
. Next, let us observe the following facts.

•

Define (recall the definitions of measure
\mu_{i}
and sets
E_{i},E_{i,j}
in
eq.

G.1
eq.

G.5
eq.

G.6
)

\displaystyle J(j)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\arg\min_{\widetilde{j}:\mu_{i}(E_{j,\widetilde{j}})>0}|i-\widetilde{j}|\ \ \forall j\neq i

\displaystyle p^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min_{j:\ j\neq i,\ m_{j}\in G}\frac{\mu_{j}(E_{j,J(j)})}{\mu_{j}(E_{j})}.

•

From the definition of
J(j)
and the fact that there are only finitely many attraction fields we can see that
p^{*}>0
. Moreover,
G
being a communication class implies that

|J(j)-i|<|j-i|\ \ \ \forall j\neq i,\ m_{j}\in G.

Indeed, if
i<j
, then since
G
is a communication class and there are some
m_{i}\in G
with
i<j
, we will at least have
\mu_{j}(E_{j,j-1})>0
, so
|J(j)-i|\leq|i-j|-1
; the case that
i>j
can be approached analogously.

•

Now from the definition of
J(j)
and Proposition
G.25
, together with the previous bullet point, we know that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(|I_{k+1}-i|\leq|I_{k}-i|-1,\ m_{I_{k+1}}\in G\ |\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>k,\ m_{I_{k}}\in G)\geq p^{*}/2

uniformly for all
k\geq 0
.

•

Meanwhile, since
p^{*}>0
, we are able to fix some
\delta>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\frac{n_{\text{min}}\delta}{({p^{*}}/{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}}<\epsilon.

•

On the other hand, for any
\widetilde{j}
with
m_{\widetilde{j}}\notin G
, by definition of the typical transition graph we must have
\mu_{j}(E_{j,\widetilde{j}})=0
for any
j
with
m_{j}\in G
. Then due to Proposition
G.25
again, one can see that for all
\eta>0
that is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(m_{I_{k+1}}\notin G\ |\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>k,\ m_{I_{k}}\in G)<\delta

uniformly for all
k\geq 0
.

•

Repeat this argument for
n_{\text{min}}
times, and we can see that for all
\eta
sufficiently small

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)\leq k+n_{\text{min}}\ |\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>k,\ m_{I_{k}}\in G)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\frac{p^{*}}{2}\Big)^{n_{\text{min}}}

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\exists l\in[n_{\text{min}}]\ \text{s.t.}\ m_{I_{k+l}}\notin G\ |\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>k,\ m_{I_{k}}\in G)

\displaystyle\leq n_{\text{min}}\delta

uniformly for all
k\geq 1
.

•

Lastly, to apply the bounds established above, we will make use of the following expression of several probabilities. For any
j
with
m_{j}\in G
and
x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)
and any
u=1,2,\cdots
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(\exists l\in[un_{\text{min}}]\ \text{s.t. }m_{I_{l}}\notin G,\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v=0}^{u-1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists k\in[n_{\text{min}}]\ \text{s.t. }m_{I_{k+vn_{\text{min}}}}\notin G\ \Big|\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>v\cdot n_{\text{min}},\ m_{I_{l}}\in G\ \forall l\leq vn_{\text{min}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \cdot\prod_{w=0}^{v-1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>(w+1)n_{\text{min}},\ m_{I_{k+wn_{\text{min}}}}\in G\ \forall k\in[n_{\text{min}}]\ \Big|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>w\cdot n_{\text{min}},\ m_{I_{l}}\in G\ \forall l\leq wn_{\text{min}}\Big)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(m_{I_{l}}\in G\ \forall l\in[un_{\text{min}}],\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{v=0}^{u-1}\mathbb{P}\Big(K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>(v+1)n_{\text{min}},\ m_{I_{k+vn_{\text{min}}}}\in G\ \forall k\in[n_{\text{min}}]\ \Big|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>vn_{\text{min}},\ m_{I_{k}}\in G\ \forall k\in[vn_{\text{min}}]\Big)

In summary, now we can see that (for sufficiently small
\eta
)

\displaystyle\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists k\in[K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)]\ s.t.\ m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}\notin G\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists v\in[n_{\text{min}}]\text{ such that }m_{I_{v+un_{\text{min}}}}\notin G,

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>un_{\text{min}},m_{I_{k}}\in G\ \forall k\in[un_{\text{min}}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists v\in[n_{\text{min}}]\text{ such that }m_{I_{v+un_{\text{min}}}}\notin G\ \Big|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>un_{\text{min}},m_{I_{k}}\in G\ \forall k\in[un_{\text{min}}]\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{v=0}^{u-1}

\displaystyle\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>(v+1)n_{\text{min}},m_{I_{k+vn_{\text{min}}}}\in G\ \forall k\in[n_{\text{min}}]\Big|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>vn_{\text{min}},m_{I_{k}}\in G\ \forall k\in[vn_{\text{min}}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u\geq 0}n_{\text{min}}\delta\Big(1-(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}\Big)^{u-1}=\frac{n_{\text{min}}\delta}{({p^{*}}/{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}}\leq\epsilon

and

\displaystyle\sup_{j:m_{j}\in G,\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{j:m_{j}\in G,\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\exists l\in[un_{\text{min}}]\ \text{s.t. }m_{I_{l}}\notin G,\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{j:m_{j}\in G,\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(m_{I_{l}}\in G\ \forall l\in[un_{\text{min}}],\ K_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{u}n_{\text{min}}\delta\Big(1-(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}\Big)^{v-1}+\Big(1-(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}\Big)^{u}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n_{\text{min}}\delta}{({p^{*}}/{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}}+\Big(1-(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}\Big)^{u}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon+\Big(1-(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}\Big)^{u}

uniformly for all
u=1,2,\cdots
. To conclude the proof, it suffices to set
p=(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}
.
∎

The proof above can be easily adapted to the case when the communication class
G
is transient. Define

\displaystyle K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{k\geq 0:\ I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)=i\ \text{or }m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}\notin G\}.

Lemma H.10
.

Assume that
G
is a transient communication class on the graph
\mathcal{G}
. Then there exists some constant
p>0
such that for any
i
with
m_{i}\in G
and any
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G;\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\text{Geom}(p)\geq u)\ \ \forall u=1,2,\cdots

(H.43)

hold for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small.

Proof.

The structure of this proof is analogous to that of Lemma
H.9
. Again, the claim is trivial if, for the initial condition, we have
x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)
. Next, let us observe the following facts.

•

Define (recall the definitions of measure
\mu_{i}
and sets
E_{i},E_{i,j}
in
eq.

G.1
eq.

G.5
eq.

G.6
)

\displaystyle J(j)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\arg\min_{\widetilde{j}:\mu_{i}(E_{j,\widetilde{j}})>0}|i-\widetilde{j}|\ \ \forall j\neq i

\displaystyle p^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min_{j:\ j\neq i,\ m_{j}\in G}\frac{\mu_{j}(E_{j,J(j)})}{\mu_{j}(E_{j})}.

•

From the definition of
J(j)
and the fact that there are only finitely many attraction fields we can see that
p^{*}>0
. Moreover,
G
being a communication class implies that

|J(j)-i|<|j-i|\ \ \ \forall j\neq i,\ m_{j}\in G.

Indeed, if
i<j
, then since
G
is a communication class and there are some
m_{i}\in G
with
i<j
, we will at least have
\mu_{j}(E_{j,j-1})>0
, so
|J(j)-i|\leq|i-j|-1
; the case that
i>j
can be approached analogously.

•

Now from the definition of
J(j)
and Proposition
G.25
, together with the previous bullet point, we know that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(|I_{k+1}-i|\leq|I_{k}-i|-1,\ m_{I_{k+1}}\in G\ |\ K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>k\geq p^{*}/2

uniformly for all
k\geq 0
.

•

Repeat this argument for
n_{\text{min}}
times, and we can see that for all
\eta
sufficiently small

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)\leq k+n_{\text{min}}\ |\ K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>k)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\frac{p^{*}}{2}\Big)^{n_{\text{min}}}

uniformly for all
k\geq 1
.

•

Lastly, for any
j\neq i
with
m_{j}\in G
and
x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)
and any
u=1,2,\cdots
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}(K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>u\cdot n_{\text{min}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{v=0}^{u-1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>(v+1)n_{\text{min}}\ \Big|\ K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>v\cdot n_{\text{min}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{v=0}^{u-1}\bigg(1-\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)\leq(v+1)n_{\text{min}}\ \Big|\ K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)>v\cdot n_{\text{min}}\Big)\bigg)

In summary, now we can see that (for suffciently small
\eta
)

\displaystyle\sup_{j:m_{j}\in G,\ x\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K^{G}_{i}(\eta,\Delta)\geq u\cdot n_{\text{min}})\leq\Big(1-(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}\Big)^{u}

uniformly for all
u=1,2,\cdots
. To conclude the proof, it suffices to set
p=(\frac{p^{*}}{2})^{n_{\text{min}}}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
H.1
, which, as demonstrated earlier, is the key tool in proof of Theorem
2
.

Proof of Lemma
H.1
.

The claim is trivial if
l^{\text{large}}=1
, so we focus on the case where
l^{\text{large}}\geq 2
. Fix some

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})\wedge(\frac{1}{2})),\ \beta\in\big(1,(2-2\widetilde{\gamma})\wedge(\alpha-\alpha\widetilde{\gamma})\big),\ \gamma\in(0,\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{16Mc_{2}}\wedge\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{4}).

Let
q^{*}=\max_{j}\mu_{j}(E_{j}(0))
. We show that for any
t\in(0,\frac{\delta}{4q^{*}})
the claim is true.

Now we only consider
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
and
\eta
small enough so that
\eta M\leq\eta^{\gamma}
and
\eta^{\gamma}<\Delta
. Consider the following stopping times

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\cup_{j}B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})\};

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\in\cup_{j}B(m_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})\}.

First, from Lemma
H.8
, we know that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}>1/H(1/\eta))<\delta/2

for all
\eta
sufficiently small. Besides, by combining Lemma
H.7
with Markov property (applied at
T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\ T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}-T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}>2c_{2}\gamma\log(1/\eta)/\eta\ \Big|\ T^{\gamma}_{\text{escape}}\leq\frac{1}{H(1/\eta)}\Big)<\delta/2

for all
\eta
sufficiently small. Therefore, for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}\Big(T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}>\frac{1}{H(1/\eta)}+2c_{2}\gamma\frac{\log(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)<\delta.

(H.44)

Let
J
be the unique index such that
X^{\eta}_{T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}}\in\Omega_{J}
. Our next goal is to show that, almost always, the SGD iterates will visit the local minimum at some
large
attraction fields. Therefore, without loss of generality, we can assume that
m_{J}\notin M^{\text{large}}
, and define

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}_{\text{large}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\in M^{\text{large}}}B(m_{i},2\Delta)\}

and introduce the following definitions:

\displaystyle\tau_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}},\ J_{0}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}J

\displaystyle\tau_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n>\tau_{k-1}:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{j\neq J_{k-1}}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\}

\displaystyle J_{k}

\displaystyle=j\Leftrightarrow X^{\eta}_{\tau_{k}}\in\Omega_{j}\ \ \forall k\geq 1

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{k\geq 0:\ m_{J_{k}}\in M^{\text{large}}\}.

In other words, the sequence of stopping times
(\tau_{k})_{k\geq 1}
is the time that, starting from
T^{\gamma}_{\text{return}}
, the SGD iterates visited a local minimum that is different from the one visited at
\tau_{k-1}
, and
(J_{k})_{k\geq 0}
records the label of the visited local minima. The random variable
K
is the number of transitions required to visit a minimizer in a
large
attraction field. From Lemma
H.9
, we know the existence of some
p^{*}>0
such that (for all
\eta
sufficiently small)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(K\geq u\cdot n_{\text{min}})\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\text{Geom}(p^{*})\geq u\Big)+\frac{\delta}{2}\ \ \forall u=1,2,3,\cdots.

where
\text{Geom}(a)
is a Geometric random variable with success rate
a\in(0,1)
. Therefore, one can find integer
N(\delta)
such that (for all sufficiently small
\eta
)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}(K\geq N(\delta))\leq\delta.

(H.45)

Next, given results in Proposition
G.25
and the fact that there are only finitely many attraction fields, one can find a real number
u(\delta)
such that (for all sufficiently small
\eta
)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\leq\frac{u(\delta)}{\lambda_{J_{k-1}}(\eta)})\leq\delta/N(\delta)

(H.46)

uniformly for all
k=1,2,\cdots,N(\delta)
. From
eq.

H.44
,
eq.

H.45
,
eq.

H.46
, we now have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in M^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\ \forall n\leq N(\delta)u(\delta)\frac{H(1/\eta)/\eta}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}+\frac{1}{H(1/\eta)}+2c_{2}\gamma\frac{\log(1/\eta)}{\eta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta

(H.47)

for any sufficiently small
\eta
. To conclude the proof we just observe the following facts. First, due to
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}
and
l^{\text{large}}\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}H(1/\eta)/\eta=0,\ \ \lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}{H(1/\eta)}=0,\ \ \lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\log(1/\eta)}{\eta}\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)=0.

Therefore, for sufficiently small
\eta
, we will have (note that
\epsilon,\delta
are fixed constants in this proof, so
N(\delta),u(\delta)
are also fixed)

\displaystyle\frac{N(\delta)u(\delta)\frac{H(1/\eta)/\eta}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}+\frac{1}{H(1/\eta)}+2c_{2}\gamma\frac{\log(1/\eta)}{\eta}}{\lfloor t/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor}\leq\epsilon.

(H.48)

Second, recall that we fixed some
t\in(0,\frac{\delta}{4q^{*}})
where
q^{*}=\max_{j}\mu_{j}(E_{j})
. Also, choose some
C>0
small enough so that

\displaystyle C<\delta/2,\ 2(1+C)^{2}<4.

From Proposition
G.23
and the fact that there are only finitely many attraction fields, there exists some
\bar{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}_{0})
and any
\Delta>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{i:m_{i}\in M^{\text{large}}}\sup_{x\in[m_{i}-2\Delta,m_{i}+2\Delta]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma_{i}(\eta)\leq\frac{t}{\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{i:m_{i}\in M^{\text{large}}}\sup_{x\in[m_{i}-2\Delta,m_{i}+2\Delta]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\mu_{i}(E_{i})\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\sigma_{i}(\eta)\leq q^{*}t\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+2(1+C)^{2}q^{*}t\leq 2\delta.

Combine this bound with Markov property (applied at
\tau_{K}
), and we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists n\in\big[\lfloor t/\lambda^{\text{large}}(\eta)\rfloor\big]\ s.t.\ X^{\eta}_{n+\tau_{K}}\notin\bigcup_{i:m_{i}\in M^{\text{large}}}\Omega_{i}\Big)\leq 2\delta

for all
\eta
sufficiently small. Together with
eq.

H.47
eq.

H.48
, we have shown that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(V^{\text{small}}(\eta,\epsilon,t)>\epsilon\Big)\leq 5\delta

holds for all
\eta
sufficiently small.
∎

H.2
Proof of Lemma
H.4
,
H.5

We shall return to the discussion about the dynamics of SGD iterates on a communication class
G
. Recall that

G^{\text{large}}=\{m^{\text{large}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{large}}_{i_{G}}\},\ G^{\text{small}}=\{m^{\text{small}}_{1},\cdots,m^{\text{small}}_{i_{G}^{\prime}}\}.

If
X^{\eta}_{n}
is initialized at some sharp minimum on
G
, then we are interested in the behavior of
X^{\eta}_{n}
at the first visit to some large attraction fields on
G
. Define

\displaystyle T_{G}(\eta,\Delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{i:\ m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{i},2\Delta)\text{ or }X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\cup_{i:\ m_{i}\in G}\Omega_{i}\}.

(H.49)

Not only is this definition of
T_{G}
analogous to the one for
T^{DTMC}_{G}
in
eq.

H.16
, but, as illustrated in the next lemma,
T_{G}
also behaves similarly as
T_{G}
on a communication class
G
in the following sense: the probabilities
p_{i,j}
defined in
eq.

H.17
govern the dynamics regarding which large attraction field on
G
is the first one to be visited. Besides,
T_{G}
is usually rather small, meaning that the SGD iterates would efficiently arrive at a large attraction field on
G
or simply escape from
G
.

Lemma H.11
.

Given any
\theta\in(0,(\alpha-1)/2)
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
i,j\in[n_{\text{min}}]
such that
m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}},\ m_{j}\notin G^{\text{large}}
, the following claims hold for all
\Delta>0
that is sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{G}(\eta,\Delta)\leq\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq p_{i,j}+5\epsilon,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{G}(\eta,\Delta)\leq\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq p_{i,j}-5\epsilon,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{G}(\eta,\Delta)>\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon.

Proof.

For
G^{\text{small}}\neq\emptyset
to hold (and the discussion to be meaningful), we must have
l^{*}_{G}\geq 2
. Throughout the proof, we assume this is the case. Besides, we require that
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
so we have

\displaystyle B(m_{i},3\Delta)\cap\Omega_{i}^{c}=\emptyset\ \forall i\in[n_{\text{min}}]

and the
3\Delta
-neighborhood of each local minimum will not intersect with each other. In this proof we will only consider
\Delta
in this range.

From Lemma
H.9
(if
G
is absorbing) or Lemma
H.10
(if
G
is transient), we know the existence of some integer
N(\epsilon)
such that for (see the definition of
I_{k}
in
eq.

H.39
-
eq.

H.42
)

\displaystyle N_{G}(\eta,\Delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{k\geq 0:\ m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}\in G^{\text{large}}\ \text{or }m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}\notin G\},

we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(N_{G}(\eta,\Delta)>N(\epsilon)\Big)<\epsilon

for all
\eta
sufficiently small. Fix such
N(\epsilon)
. Next, from Proposition
G.25
, we can find
u(\epsilon)\in(0,\infty)
and
\bar{\Delta}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
such that for all
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\Delta})
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{k}(\eta,\Delta)-T_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta)>u(\epsilon)/\Lambda\big(I_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta),\eta\big)\Big)\leq\epsilon/N(\epsilon)\ \ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]

for all
\eta
sufficiently small. Fix such
u(\epsilon)
and
\bar{\Delta}
. Now note that on the event

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{N_{G}\leq N(\epsilon)\Big\}\cap\Big\{T_{k}(\eta,\Delta)-T_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta)\leq u(\epsilon)/\Lambda\big(I_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta),\eta\big)\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big\},

due to the choice of
\theta\in(0,(\alpha-1)/2)
and
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}
, we have (when
\eta\in(0,1)
)

\displaystyle T_{k}(\eta,\Delta)-T_{k-1}(\eta,\Delta)\leq\frac{\eta^{2\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\ \forall k<N_{G}(\eta,\Delta)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle T_{G}(\eta,\Delta)=T_{N_{G}(\eta,\Delta)}(\eta,\Delta)\leq N(\epsilon)u(\epsilon)\frac{\eta^{2\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}.

For any
\eta
sufficiently small, we will have
N(\epsilon)u(\epsilon)\frac{\eta^{2\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}<\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}
. In summary, we have established that for all
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\Delta)}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{G}>\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\Big)\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{c})<2\epsilon.

(H.50)

Next, let

\displaystyle\textbf{S}(\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\big(m^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,m^{\prime}_{N(\epsilon)}\big)\in\{m_{1},\cdots,m_{n_{\text{min}}}\}^{N(\epsilon)}:\ \exists k\in[N(\epsilon)]\ \text{s.t. }m^{\prime}_{k}=m_{j}\Big\}.

We can see that
\textbf{S}(\epsilon)
contains all the possible transition path for
Y^{DTMC}
where the state
m_{j}
is visited within the first
N(\epsilon)
steps. Obviously,
|\textbf{S}(\epsilon)|<\infty
.
Let
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon/|\textbf{S}(\epsilon)|
. If we are able to show the existence of some
\bar{\Delta}_{1}>0
such that for all
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\Delta}_{1})
, the following claim holds for any
(m^{\prime}_{k})_{k=1}^{N(\epsilon)}\in\textbf{S}(\epsilon)
:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(m_{I_{k}}=m^{\prime}_{k}\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(Y^{DTMC}_{k}(m_{i})=m^{\prime}_{k}\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big)\Big|<\epsilon_{1},

(H.51)

then we must have (for all
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\Delta}\wedge\bar{\Delta}_{1})
)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\Big)-p_{i,j}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta),\ T_{G}\leq N(\epsilon)\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta),\ T_{G}>N(\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ -\mathbb{P}\Big(Y^{DTMC}_{T^{DTMC}_{G}}(m_{i})=m_{j},\ T^{DTMC}_{G}\leq N(\epsilon)\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(Y^{DTMC}_{T^{DTMC}_{G}}(m_{i})=m_{j},\ T^{DTMC}_{G}>N(\epsilon)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta),\ T_{G}\leq N(\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}\Big(Y^{DTMC}_{T^{DTMC}_{G}}(m_{i})=m_{j},\ T^{DTMC}_{G}\leq N(\epsilon)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{G}>N(\epsilon))+\mathbb{P}({T^{DTMC}_{G}}(m_{i})>N(\epsilon))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\textbf{S}(\epsilon)|\epsilon_{1}+\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}(T_{G}>N(\epsilon))+\mathbb{P}({T^{DTMC}_{G}}(m_{i})>N(\epsilon))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\epsilon.

To show that
eq.

H.51
is true, we fix some
(m^{\prime}_{k})_{k=1}^{N(\epsilon)}\in\textbf{S}(\epsilon)
and let
(\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k))_{k=1}^{N(\epsilon)}
be the sequence with
m_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}=m^{\prime}_{k}
for each
k\in[N(\epsilon)]
. From the definition of
Y^{DTMC}
we have (let
\textbf{k}^{\prime}(0)=i
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(Y^{DTMC}_{k}(m_{i})=m^{\prime}_{k}\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big)=\prod_{k=0}^{N(\epsilon)-1}\frac{\mu_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}(E_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k),\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k+1)})}{\mu_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}(E_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)})}.

On the other hand, using Proposition
G.25
, we know that for any arbitrarily chosen
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}=m^{\prime}_{k}\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big)\leq\prod_{k=0}^{N(\epsilon)-1}\frac{\mu_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}(E_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k),\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k+1)})}{\mu_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}(E_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)})}\cdot(1+\epsilon^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(m_{I_{k}(\eta,\Delta)}=m^{\prime}_{k}\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big)\geq\prod_{k=0}^{N(\epsilon)-1}\frac{\mu_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}(E_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k),\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k+1)})}{\mu_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)}(E_{\textbf{k}^{\prime}(k)})}\cdot(1-\epsilon^{\prime}),

for all
\Delta>0
sufficiently small. The arbitrariness of
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, together with
|\textbf{S}(\epsilon)|<\infty
, allows us to see the existence of some
\bar{\Delta}_{1}>0
such that with
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\Delta}_{1})
,
eq.

H.51
holds for any
(m^{\prime}_{k})_{k=1}^{N(\epsilon)}\in\textbf{S}(\epsilon)
. To conclude the proof, observe that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\Big)-\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{G}(\eta,\Delta)\leq\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)},\ X^{\eta}_{T_{G}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in B(m_{i},2\Delta)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(T_{G}>\frac{\eta^{\theta}}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\Big)<\epsilon

due to
eq.

H.50
.
∎

Recall that continuous-time process
X^{*,\eta}
is the
scaled
version of
X^{\eta}
defined in
eq.

H.13
, and the mapping
\textbf{T}^{*}(n,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}n\lambda_{G}(\eta)
returns the timestamp
t
for
X^{*,\eta}_{t}
corresponding to the unscaled step
n
on the time horizon of
X^{\eta}_{n}
.
As an
inverse
mapping of
\textbf{T}^{*}
, we define the mapping
\textbf{N}^{*}(t,\eta)=\lfloor t/\lambda_{G}(\eta)\rfloor
that maps the scaled timestamp
t
back to the step number
n
for the unscaled process
X^{\eta}
.

In the next lemma, we show that, provided that
X^{*,\eta}
stays on a communication class
G
before some time
t
, the scaled process
X^{*,\eta}_{t}
is almost always in the largest attraction fields of a communication class
G
.

Lemma H.12
.

Let
G
be a communication class on the graph
\mathcal{G}
. Given any
\epsilon_{1}>0
,
t>0
and any
x\in\Omega_{i}
with
m_{i}\in G
, the following claim holds for all
\Delta>0
small enough:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{t}\notin\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},3\Delta)\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\Big\}\Big)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}.

Proof.

Let
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
for the constant
\bar{\epsilon}
in
eq.

G.28
eq.

G.29
, so we are certain that each
B(m_{i},2\Delta)
lies entirely in
\Omega_{i}
and would not intersect with each other since

\displaystyle B(m_{i},3\Delta)\cap\Omega_{i}^{c}=\emptyset\ \ \forall i\in[n_{\text{min}}].

Besides, with
\epsilon=\Delta/3
, we know the existence of some
\delta>0
such that claims in Lemma
G.14
would hold of the chosen
\epsilon,\delta
. Fix such
\delta
for the entirety of this proof. Lastly, fix some

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}\in(0,(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})\wedge(\frac{1}{2})),\ \beta\in\big(1,(2-2\widetilde{\gamma})\wedge(\alpha-\alpha\widetilde{\gamma})\big),\ \gamma\in(0,\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{16Mc_{2}}\wedge\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}}{4}).

The blueprint of this proof is as follows. We will define a sequence of stopping times
(N_{j})_{j=1}^{6}
such that the corresponding scaled timestamps
\textbf{T}^{*}_{j}=\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{j},\eta)
gradually approach
t
. By analyzing the behavior of
X^{*,\eta}
on a time interval
[t-\Delta_{t},t]
that is very close to
t
(in particular, on the aforementioned stopping times
\textbf{T}^{*}_{j}
), we are able to establish the properties of a series of events
A_{1}\supseteq A_{2}\supseteq A_{3}
. Moreover, we will show that
A_{3}\subseteq\{X^{*,\eta,\Delta}_{t}\in\bigcup_{i:\ m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{i},3\Delta)\}
, so the properties about events
A,A_{2},A_{3}
can be used to bound the probability of the target event.

Arbitrarily choose some
\Delta_{t}\in(0,t)
. To proceed, let
N_{0}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\textbf{N}^{*}(t-\Delta_{t},\eta)
be the stopping time corresponding to timestamp
t-\Delta_{t}
for the scaled process. Using Lemma
H.8
, we know that for stopping time
N_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq N_{0}:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\cup_{j}B(s_{j},2\eta^{\gamma})\}
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(N_{1}-N_{0}<\frac{\Delta_{t}/4}{H(1/\eta)})=1.

Next, let
N_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq N_{1}:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\cup_{j}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\}
.
From Lemma
H.7
and
H\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}
(so that
\log(1/\eta)/\eta=o(H(1/\eta))
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(N_{2}-N_{1}<\frac{\Delta_{t}/4}{H(1/\eta)})=1.

Collecting results above, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}(N_{2}-N_{0}<\frac{\Delta_{t}/2}{H(1/\eta)})=1.

(H.52)

Now note the following fact on the event
\{N_{2}-N_{0}<\frac{\Delta_{t}/2}{H(1/\eta)}\}
. The definition of the mapping
\textbf{T}^{*}
implies that, for any pair of positive integers
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
, we have
\textbf{T}^{*}(n_{2},\eta)-\textbf{T}^{*}(n_{1},\eta)=(n_{2}-n_{1})\lambda_{G}(\eta)\leq(n_{2}-n_{1})\cdot H(1/\eta)
. Therefore, on
\{N_{2}-N_{0}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/2}{H(1/\eta)}\}
we have

\displaystyle\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{2},\eta)-\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{0},\eta)<\Delta_{t}/2\Rightarrow\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{2},\eta)<t-\frac{\Delta_{t}}{2}.

Besides, let
\textbf{T}^{*}_{2}=\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{2},\eta)
. Now we can see that for event

\displaystyle A_{0}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\Big\}\cap\Big\{N_{2}-N_{0}<\frac{\Delta_{t}/2}{H(1/\eta)}\Big\},

we have

\displaystyle A_{0}\subseteq A_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{T}^{*}_{2}<t-\frac{\Delta_{t}}{2}\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,\textbf{T}^{*}_{2}]\Big\}.

Meanwhile, from
eq.

H.52
we obtain that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(A_{1}^{c}\cap\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\}\Big)=0.

(H.53)

Moving on, we consider the following stopping times

\displaystyle N_{3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq N_{2}:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\text{ or }X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\},

\displaystyle\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{3},\eta).

Using Lemma
H.11
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(N_{3}-N_{2}>\frac{\Delta_{t}/4}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\ \Big|\ A_{1}\Big)\leq\epsilon_{1}.

(H.54)

Meanwhile, on event
A_{1}\cap\Big\{N_{3}-N_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/4}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\Big\},

we have
\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}-\textbf{T}^{*}_{2}\leq\Delta_{t}/4
, hence
\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}\in[t-\Delta_{t},t-\Delta_{t}/4]
. In summary,

\displaystyle A_{1}\cap\Big\{N_{3}-N_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/4}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\Big\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\Big\{\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}\in[t-\Delta_{t},t-\Delta_{t}/4]\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}]\Big\}

Moreover, on event
\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\Big\}
, if we let
J_{3}
be label of the local minimum visited at
\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}
such that
J_{3}=j\iff X^{*,\eta}_{T^{*}_{3}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta)
, then we must have
m_{J_{3}}\in G^{\text{large}}
. Meanwhile, consider the following stopping times
