\displaystyle\textbf{T}^{\sigma}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{s>\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}:\ X^{*,\eta}_{s}\notin\Omega_{J_{3}}\}.

From Proposition
G.25
, we know that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\textbf{T}^{\sigma}-\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}\leq\Delta_{t}\ \Big|\ \Big\{\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}\in[t-\Delta_{t},t-\Delta_{t}/4]\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}]\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}+1-\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon_{1})q^{*}\Delta_{t}\big)

(H.55)

where
q^{*}=\max_{j}\mu_{j}(E_{j})
. Now we define the event

\displaystyle A_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}A_{1}\cap\Big\{N_{3}-N_{2}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/4}{\lambda_{G}(\eta)}\Big\}\cap\Big\{\textbf{T}^{\sigma}-\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}>\Delta_{t}\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}]\Big\}.

Using
eq.

H.53
-
eq.

H.55
, we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(A_{2}^{c}\cap\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\}\Big)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}+1-\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon_{1})q^{*}\Delta_{t}\big).

(H.56)

Furthermore, on event
A_{2}
, due to
\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}\in[t-\Delta_{t},t-\Delta_{t}/4]
as established above, we must have

\displaystyle X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\Omega_{J_{3}}\ \forall s\in[\textbf{T}^{*}_{3},t].

Now let us focus on a timestamp
\textbf{T}^{*}_{4}=t-\frac{\Delta_{t}/8}{H(1/\eta)}\lambda_{G}(\eta)
and
N_{4}=\textbf{N}^{*}(\textbf{T}^{*}_{4},\eta)
. Obviously,
\textbf{T}^{*}_{4}>\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}
on event
A_{2}
. Next, define

\displaystyle N_{5}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n\geq N_{4}:\ X^{\eta}_{n}\in\bigcup_{j}B(m_{j},2\Delta)\}

\displaystyle\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\textbf{T}^{*}(N_{5},\eta).

Using Lemma
H.7
and
H.8
again as we did above when obtaining
eq.

H.52
, we can show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(N_{5}-N_{4}>\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\Big)=0.

(H.57)

On the other hand, on event
A_{2}\cap\Big\{N_{5}-N_{4}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\Big\}
we must have

•

\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}-\textbf{T}^{*}_{4}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\lambda_{G}(\eta)
, so
\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}\in[t-\frac{\Delta_{t}/8}{H(1/\eta)}\lambda_{G}(\eta),t-\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\lambda_{G}(\eta)]
;

•

X^{*,\eta}_{\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}}\in\Omega_{J_{3}}
, due to
\textbf{T}^{\sigma}-\textbf{T}^{*}_{3}>\Delta_{t}
.

This implies that for event

\widetilde{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}\in[t-\frac{\Delta_{t}/8}{H(1/\eta)}\lambda_{G}(\eta),t-\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\lambda_{G}(\eta)],\ X^{*,\eta}_{\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}}\in\Omega_{J_{3}}\Big\}\cap\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall s\in[0,\textbf{T}^{*}_{5}]\},

we have
A_{2}\cap\Big\{N_{5}-N_{4}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\Big\}\subseteq\widetilde{A}
. Lastly, observe that

•

From Lemma
G.4
, we know that for
N_{6}(\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{n>N_{5}:\eta|Z_{n}|>\delta\}

we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}\big(N_{6}(\delta)-N_{5}\leq\Delta_{t}/H(1/\eta)\big)\leq\Delta_{t}/\delta^{\alpha};

•

As stated at the beginning of the proof, our choice of
\delta
allows us to apply Lemma
G.14
and show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\exists n=N_{5},\cdots,N_{6}-1\ \text{s.t. }X^{\eta}_{n}\notin B(m_{J_{3}},3\Delta)\ |\ \widetilde{A}\big)=0;

•

Combining the two bullet points above, we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists s\in[\textbf{T}^{*}_{5},t]\text{ such that }X^{*,\eta}_{s}\notin B(m_{J_{3}},3\Delta)\ \Big|\ \widetilde{A}\Big)\leq\Delta_{t}/\delta^{\alpha}.

(H.58)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}\cap\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in B(m_{J_{3}},3\Delta)\ \forall s\in[\textbf{T}^{*}_{5},t]\}\subseteq\{X^{*,\eta}_{t}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{k},3\Delta)\}.

In summary, for event

\displaystyle A_{3}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}A_{2}\cap\Big\{N_{5}-N_{4}\leq\frac{\Delta_{t}/16}{H(1/\eta)}\Big\}\cap\Big\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in B(m_{J_{3}},3\Delta)\ \forall s\in[\textbf{T}^{*}_{5},t]\Big\},

we have
A_{3}\subseteq\{X^{*,\eta}_{t}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{k},3\Delta)\}
. Besides, due to
eq.

H.56
eq.

H.57
eq.

H.58
, we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in[-L,L]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(A_{3}^{c}\cap\{X^{*,\eta}_{s}\in\bigcup_{k:\ m_{k}\in G}\Omega_{k}\ \forall s\in[0,t]\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\epsilon_{1}+1-\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon_{1})q^{*}\Delta_{t}\big)+\frac{\Delta_{t}}{\delta^{\alpha}}.

Remember that
\delta,\epsilon_{1},q^{*}
are fixed constants while
\Delta_{t}
can be made arbitrarily small, so by driving
\Delta_{t}
to 0 we can conclude the proof.
∎

Recall the definition of jump processes in Definition
H.1
. Central to the proof of Lemma
H.4
, the next result provides a set of sufficient conditions for the convergence of a sequence of such jump processes in the sense of finite dimensional distributions.

Lemma H.13
.

For a sequence of processes
(Y^{n})_{n\geq 1}
that, for each
n\geq 1
,
Y^{n}
is a
\Big((U^{n}_{j})_{j\geq 0},(V^{n}_{j})_{j\geq 0}\Big)
jump process, and a
\Big((U_{j})_{j\geq 0},(V_{j})_{j\geq 0}\Big)
jump process
Y
, if

•

U_{0}\equiv 0
;

•

(U^{n}_{0},V^{n}_{0},U^{n}_{1},V^{n}_{1},U^{n}_{2},V^{n}_{2},\cdots)
converges in distribution to
(0,V_{0},U_{1},V_{1},U_{2},V_{2},\cdots)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
;

•

For any
x>0
and any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{1}+\cdots+U_{n}=x)=0;

•

For any
x>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(U_{1}+U_{2}+\cdots U_{n}>x)=1,

then the finite dimensional distribution of
Y^{n}
converges to that of
Y
in the following sense: for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and any
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{k}<\infty
, the random element
(Y^{n}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n}_{t_{k}})
converges in distribution to
(Y_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y_{t_{k}})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

Fix some
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{k}<\infty
. For notational simplicity, let
t=t_{k}
. Let
(\mathbb{D},\textbf{d})
be the metric space where
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}_{[0,t]}
, the space of all càdlàg functions in
\mathbb{R}
on the time interval
[0,t]
, and
d
is the Skorokhod metric defined as

\displaystyle\textbf{d}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\inf_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\left\lVert\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}\circ\lambda\right\rVert\vee\left\lVert\lambda-I\right\rVert

where
\Lambda
is the set of all nondecreasing homeomorphism from
[0,t]
onto itself, and
I(s)=s
is the identity mapping. Also, we arbitrarily choose some
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and some open set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{k}
.

From the assumption, we can find integer
J(\epsilon)
such that
\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J(\epsilon)}U_{j}\leq t\Big)<\epsilon
, as well as an integer
N(\epsilon)
such that, for all
n\geq N(\epsilon)
, we have
\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J(\epsilon)}U^{n}_{j}\leq t\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(U^{n}_{0}\geq t_{1}\Big)<\epsilon
. We fix such
J(\epsilon),N(\epsilon)
(we may abuse the notation slightly and simply write
J,N
when there is no ambiguity).

Using Skorokhod’s representation theorem, we can construct a probability space
(\bm{\Omega},\mathcal{F},\mathbb{Q})
that supports random variables
(\widetilde{U}^{n}_{0},\widetilde{V}^{n}_{0},\cdots,\widetilde{U}^{n}_{J},\widetilde{V}^{n}_{J})_{n\geq 1}
and
(\widetilde{U}_{0},\widetilde{V}_{0},\cdots,\widetilde{U}_{J},\widetilde{V}_{J})
and satisfies the following conditions:

•

\mathcal{L}(U^{n}_{0},V^{n}_{0},\cdots,U^{n}_{J},V^{n}_{J})=\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{U}^{n}_{0},\widetilde{V}^{n}_{0},\cdots,\widetilde{U}^{n}_{J},\widetilde{V}^{n}_{J})
for all
n\geq 1
;

•

\mathcal{L}(U_{0},V_{0},\cdots,U_{J},V_{J})=\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{U}_{0},\widetilde{V}_{0},\cdots,\widetilde{U}_{J},\widetilde{V}_{J})
;

•

U^{n}_{j}\xrightarrow{a.s.}U_{j}
and
V^{n}_{j}\xrightarrow{a.s.}V_{j}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
for all
j\in[J]
.

Therefore, on
(\bm{\Omega},\mathcal{F},\mathbb{Q})
we can define the following random elements (taking values in the space of càdlàg functions):

\displaystyle Y_{s}^{n,\downarrow J}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\phantom{-}\widetilde{V}^{n}_{0}&\text{if }s<\widetilde{U}^{n}_{0}\\
\sum_{j=0}^{J}\widetilde{V}^{n}_{j}\mathbbm{1}_{[\widetilde{U}^{n}_{0}+\widetilde{U}^{n}_{1}+\cdots+\widetilde{U}^{n}_{j},\ \widetilde{U}^{n}_{0}+\widetilde{U}^{n}_{1}+\cdots+\widetilde{U}^{n}_{j+1})}(s)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

\displaystyle Y^{\downarrow J}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{J}\widetilde{V}_{j}\mathbbm{1}_{[\widetilde{U}_{1}+\cdots+\widetilde{U}_{j},\ \widetilde{U}_{1}+\cdots+\widetilde{U}_{j+1})}(s)\ \ \forall s\geq 0.

Note that (1) for the first jump time of
Y^{\downarrow J}
we have
\widetilde{U}_{0}\equiv 0
, hence
Y^{\downarrow J}_{0}=\widetilde{V}_{0}
; (2) when defining
Y^{n,\downarrow J}
we set its value on
[0,\widetilde{U}^{n}_{0})
to be
\widetilde{V}^{n}_{0}
instead of 0.

Since
U^{n}_{j}\xrightarrow{a.s.}U_{j}
and
V^{n}_{j}\xrightarrow{a.s.}V_{j}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
for all
j\in[J]
, we must have

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\textbf{d}(Y_{s}^{n,\downarrow J},Y_{s}^{\downarrow J})=0

almost surely, which further implies that
Y_{s}^{n,\downarrow J}\Rightarrow Y_{s}^{\downarrow J}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
on
(\mathbb{D},\textbf{d})
. Now from our assumption that, for the jump times
U_{1}+\cdots+U_{j}
, we have
\mathbb{P}(U_{1}+\cdots+U_{j}=x)=0\ \forall x>0,j\geq 1
, as well as (13.3) in
Billingsley 2013
, we then obtain

\displaystyle(Y^{n,\downarrow J}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n,\downarrow J}_{t_{k}})\Rightarrow(Y^{\downarrow J}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{\downarrow J}_{t_{k}})

(H.59)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Recall that
A
is the open set we arbitrarily chose at the beginning of the proof, and
\epsilon>0
is also chosen arbitrarily. Now we observe the following facts.

•

Using
eq.

H.59
, we can see that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n}\mathbb{Q}\big((Y^{n,\downarrow J}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n,\downarrow J}_{t_{k}})\in A\big)\geq\mathbb{Q}\big(Y^{\downarrow J}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{\downarrow J}_{t_{k}})\in A\big).

•

The choice of
N(\epsilon)
and
J(\epsilon)
above implies that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{Q}\big((Y^{\downarrow J(\epsilon)}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{\downarrow J(\epsilon)}_{t_{k}})\in A\big)-\mathbb{P}\big((Y_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y_{t_{k}})\in A\big)\big|\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J(\epsilon)}U_{j}\leq t\Big)<\epsilon,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{Q}\big((Y^{n,\downarrow J(\epsilon)}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n,\downarrow J(\epsilon)}_{t_{k}})\in A\big)-\mathbb{P}\big((Y^{n}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n}_{t_{k}})\in A\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J(\epsilon)}U^{n}_{j}\leq t\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(U^{n}_{0}\geq t_{1}\Big)<\epsilon\ \ \forall n\geq N(\epsilon).

Collecting the two results above, we have established that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n}\mathbb{P}\big((Y^{n}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n}_{t_{k}})\in A\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big((Y_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y_{t_{k}})\in A\big)-2\epsilon.

From Portmanteau theorem, together with arbitrariness of
\epsilon>0
and open set
A
, we can now conclude that
(Y^{n}_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y^{n}_{t_{k}})
converges in distribution to
(Y_{t_{1}},\cdots,Y_{t_{k}})
.
∎

The following lemma concerns the scaled version of the marker process
\hat{X}^{*,\eta,\Delta}
defined in
eq.

H.13
-
eq.

H.14
. Obviously, it is a jump process that complies with Definition
H.1
. When there is no ambiguity about the sequences
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
and
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, let
\hat{X}^{(n)}_{t}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}_{t}
. From
eq.

H.6
-
eq.

H.11
and
eq.

H.15
, we know that for any
n\geq 1
,
\hat{X}^{(n)}
is a
\Big(\big(\tau^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})-\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})\big)_{k\geq 0},\ \big(m_{I_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})}\big)_{k\geq 0}\Big)
-jump process (with the convention that
\tau^{*}_{-1}=0
). Also, for clarity of the exposition, we let (for all
n\geq 1,k\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})-\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}),

\displaystyle S^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\tau^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})-\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=m_{\widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})},

\displaystyle W^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=m_{I^{G}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})}.

Lastly, remember that
Y
is the continuous-time Markov chain defined in
eq.

H.18
-
eq.

H.21
and
\pi_{G}(\cdot)
is the random mapping defined in
eq.

H.22
that is used to initialize
Y
. Besides,
Y
is a
\big((S_{k})_{k\geq 0},(W_{k})_{k\geq 0}\big)
jump process under Definition
H.1
, with
S_{0}=0
and
W_{0}=\pi_{G}(m_{i})
(here
x\in\Omega_{i}
and
X^{\eta}_{0}=x
, so
i
is the index of the attraction field where the SGD iterate is initialized). The following result states that, given a sequence of learning rates
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
that tend to
0
, we are able to find a sequence of
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
to parametrize
\hat{X}^{(n)}=\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}},{X}^{(n)}={X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}
so that they have several useful properties, one of which is that the jump times and locations of
\hat{X}^{(n)}
converges in distribuiton to those of
Y(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))
.

Lemma H.14
.

Assume the communication class
G
is absorbing. Given any
m_{i}\in G
,
x\in\Omega_{i}
, finitely many real numbers
(t_{l})_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}
such that
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{k^{\prime}}
, and a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow 0}\eta_{n}=0
, there exists a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that

•

Under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
(so
X^{\eta}_{0}=x
), as
n
tends to
\infty
,

\displaystyle(S^{(n)}_{0},W^{(n)}_{0},S^{(n)}_{1},W^{(n)}_{1},S^{(n)}_{2},W^{(n)}_{2},\cdots)\Rightarrow(S_{0},W_{0},S_{1},W_{1},S_{2},W_{2},\cdots)

(H.60)

•

(Recall the definition of
T_{k},I_{k}
in
eq.

H.39
-
eq.

H.42
) Given any
\epsilon>0
, the following claim holds for all
n
sufficiently large:

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists j\in[T_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}),T_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})]\ s.t.\ X^{\eta}_{j}\notin\bigcup_{l:\ m_{l}\in G}\Omega_{l}\ |\ m_{I_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})}\in G\Big)<\epsilon;

(H.61)

•

Given any
\epsilon>0
, the following claim holds for all
n
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{P}_{k}\Big(m_{I_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})+v}\notin G^{\text{large}}\ \forall v\in[un_{\text{min}}]\ \Big|\ m_{I_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})}\in G\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Geom(p^{*})\geq u)+\epsilon\ \forall u=1,2,\cdots;

(H.62)

•

For any
l\in[k^{\prime}]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{l}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n}),\ X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{s}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall s\in[0,t_{k^{\prime}}]\Big)=0

(H.63)

where
p^{*}>0
is a constant that does not vary with our choices of
\eta_{n}
or
\Delta_{n}
.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\nu_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}q_{j}=\mu_{j}(E_{j})

\displaystyle\nu_{j,k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mu_{j}(E_{j,k})

so from the definition of
q_{j,k}
we have
q_{j,k}=\mathbbm{1}\{j\neq k\}\nu_{j,k}+\sum_{l:\ m_{l}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{j,l}p_{l,k}
.

In order to specify our choice of
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, we consider a construction of sequences
(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j))_{j\geq 0},(\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j))_{j\geq 0}
as follows. Fix some
\theta\in(0,\alpha-1)/2)
. Let
\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(0)=\bar{\bm{\eta}}(0)=1
. One can see the existence of some
(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j))_{j\geq 1},(\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j))_{j\geq 1}
such that

•

\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\in\big(0,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j-1)/2\big],\ \bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)\in\big(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j-1)/2\big]
for all
j\geq 1
;

•

(Due to Lemma
H.7
) for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
, (remember that
x
and
i
are the fixed constants prescribed in the description of the lemma)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma^{*}_{0}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)<\eta^{\theta},\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{0}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i\Big)>1-\frac{1}{2^{j}}.

For definitions of
\sigma^{G}_{k},\tau^{G}_{k},I^{G}_{k},\widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}
, see
eq.

H.6
-
eq.

H.11
.

•

(Due to Lemma
H.11
) for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\tau^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)-\sigma^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)<\eta^{\theta},\ I^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{2}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)-p_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle<1/2^{j}

uniformly for all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
. Also, by definition of
\sigma^{*}
and
\tau^{*}
, we must have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\tau^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)-\sigma^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=0,\ I^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)=1

for all
k\geq 0
and
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}}
.

•

(Due to Proposition
G.25
) for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2^{j}}+\exp\big(-(1+\frac{1}{2^{j}})q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}-\frac{1}{2^{j}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma^{*}_{k+1}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)-\tau^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)>u,\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k+1}=i_{2}\ \Big|\ I^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{j}}+\exp\big(-(1-\frac{1}{2^{j}})q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\frac{1}{2^{j}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

uniformly for all
k\geq 1
, all
u>1/2^{j}
, and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}},m_{i_{2}}\in G
.

•

(Due to
G
being absorbing and, again, Proposition
G.25
) for any
j\geq 1
, for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
, (Recall the definition of
T_{k},I_{k}
in
eq.

H.39
-
eq.

H.42
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(I_{k+1}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{2}\ |\ I_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)<\frac{1}{2^{j}}

(H.64)

uniformly for all
k\geq 0
,
m_{i_{1}}\in G,m_{i_{2}}\notin G
.

•

(Due to Lemma
H.9
) There exists some
p^{*}>0
such that for any
j\geq 1
, for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(m_{v+I_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)}\notin G^{\text{large}}\ \forall v\in[un_{\text{min}}]\ \Big|\ I_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Geom(p^{*})\geq u)+1/2^{j}

(H.65)

uniformly for all
k\geq 0,u\geq 1
and
m_{i_{1}}\in G
.

•

(Due to Lemma
H.12
) for any
j\geq 1
, for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)),\ X^{*,\eta_{n}}_{s}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall s\in[0,t_{k^{\prime}}]\Big)<1/2^{j}

(H.66)

uniformly for all
k\in[k^{\prime}]
.

Fix such
(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j))_{j\geq 0},(\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j))_{j\geq 0}
. Define a function
\textbf{J}(\cdot):\mathbb{N}\mapsto\mathbb{N}
as

\displaystyle\textbf{J}(n)=0\vee\max\{j\geq 0:\ \bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)\geq\eta_{n}\}

with the convention that
\max\emptyset=-\infty
. Lastly, let

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(\textbf{J}(n))\ \forall n\geq 1.

Note that, due to
\lim_{n}\eta_{n}=0
, we have
\lim_{n}\textbf{J}(n)=\infty
, hence
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
. Besides, the definition of
\textbf{J}(\cdot)
tells us that in case that
\textbf{J}(n)\geq 1
(which will hold for all
n
sufficiently large), the claims above holds with
\eta=\eta_{n}
and
j=\textbf{J}(n)
. In particular, by combining
\lim_{n}\textbf{J}(n)=\infty
with
eq.

H.64
eq.

H.65
eq.

H.66
respectively, we have
eq.

H.61
eq.

H.62
eq.

H.63
.

Now it remains to prove
eq.

H.60
. To this end, it suffices to show that, for any positive integer
K
, we have
(S^{(n)}_{0},W^{(n)}_{0},\cdots,S^{(n)}_{K},W^{(n)}_{K})
converges in distribution
(S_{0},W_{0},\cdots,S_{K},W_{K})
as
n
tends to infinity. In particular, note that
S_{0}=0,W_{0}=\pi_{G}(m_{i})
, so
W_{0}=m_{j}
with probability
p_{i,j}
if
m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}
, and
W_{0}\equiv m_{i}
if
m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}}
.

For clarity of the exposition, we restate some important claims above under the new notational system with
\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k},\widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k},S^{(n)}_{k},W^{(n)}_{k}
we introduced right above this lemma. Given any
\epsilon>0
, the following claims hold for all
n
sufficiently large:

•

First of all,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{0}<\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{0}=m_{i}\Big)>1-\epsilon.

(H.67)

•

For all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k}<\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ {W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)-p_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bigg|<\epsilon.

(H.68)

•

For all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k}=0,\ {W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)=1.

(H.69)

•

For all
k\geq 0
, all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}},m_{i_{2}}\in G
and all
u>\epsilon
,

\displaystyle-\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}-\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}>u,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}>u-\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.70)

•

Here is one implication of
eq.

H.68
. Since
|G|\leq n_{\text{min}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k}\geq\eta_{n}^{\theta}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)<n_{\text{min}}\cdot\epsilon

(H.71)

for all
k\geq 0
and
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}}
.

•

Note that for any
m_{i_{1}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
and any
k\geq 0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}\{i_{2}\neq i_{1}\}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}>u,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\int_{s>0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big).

Fix some
i_{3}
with
m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}
. On the one hand, due to
eq.

H.71
,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(0,u-\eta^{\theta}_{n}]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}\geq u-s,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n_{\text{min}}\epsilon.

On the other hand, by considering the integral on
(u-\eta_{n}^{\theta},\infty)
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(u-\eta^{\theta}_{n},\infty)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(u,\infty)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(p_{i_{3},i_{2}}-\epsilon)\Big(-\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}-\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}\Big)

due to
eq.

H.68
and
eq.

H.70
. Meanwhile,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(u-\eta^{\theta}_{n},\infty)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(n_{\text{min}}\epsilon+p_{i_{3},i_{2}}+\epsilon)\Big(\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}+\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}\Big)

due to
eq.

H.68
,
eq.

H.70
and
eq.

H.71
.

•

Therefore, for any
m_{i_{1}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
and any
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\mathbbm{1}\{i_{2}\neq i_{1}\}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}p_{i_{3},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{q_{i_{1},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.72)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k+1}-S^{(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\mathbbm{1}\{i_{2}\neq i_{1}\}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}p_{i_{3},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{q_{i_{1},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.73)

where
q^{*}=\max_{i}q_{i}
and

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}

\displaystyle\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{q^{*}}+n_{\text{min}}(1+\epsilon)\epsilon+\epsilon\frac{1+\epsilon}{q^{*}}n_{\text{min}}

\displaystyle+n_{\text{min}}(\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{q^{*}})+n_{\text{min}}(n_{\text{min}}\epsilon+\epsilon+1)(1+\frac{1}{q^{*}})\epsilon.

Note that
\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}g(\epsilon)=0
.

Now we apply the bounds in
eq.

H.67
eq.

H.72
eq.

H.73
to establish the weak convergence claim regarding
(S^{(n)}_{0},W^{(n)}_{0},\cdots,S^{(n)}_{K},W^{(n)}_{K})
. Fix some positive integer
K
, some strictly positive real numbers
(s_{k})_{k=0}^{K}
, a sequence
(w_{k})_{k=0}^{K}\in\big(G^{\text{large}}\big)^{K+1}
with
w_{k}=m_{i_{k}}
for each
k
, and some
\epsilon>0
such that
\epsilon<\min_{k=0,1,\cdots,K}\{s_{k}\}
. On the one hand, the definition of the CTMC
Y
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{0}<t_{0},W_{0}=w_{0};\ S_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\pi_{G}(m_{i})=w_{0})\prod_{k=1}^{K}\Big(S_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W_{k}=w_{k}\ \Big|\ W_{k-1}=w_{k-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},\ i_{0}=i\}+\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\}p_{i_{0},i_{1}}\Big)\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\exp(-q_{i_{k-1}}s_{k})\frac{q_{i_{k-1},i_{k}}}{q_{i_{k-1}}}.

On the other hand, using
eq.

H.67
eq.

H.72
eq.

H.73
, we know that for all
n
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{0}<s_{0},W^{(n)}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{(n)}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{(n)}_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},\ i_{0}=i\}+\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\}(p_{i_{0},i_{1}}-\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\Big(-g(\epsilon)+\exp(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{k-1}}s_{k})\frac{q_{i_{k-1},i_{k}}}{q_{i_{k-1}}}\Big)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{0}<s_{0},W^{(n)}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{(n)}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{(n)}_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},\ i_{0}=i\}+\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\}(p_{i_{0},i_{1}}+\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\Big(g(\epsilon)+\exp(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{k-1}}s_{k})\frac{q_{i_{k-1},i_{k}}}{q_{i_{k-1}}}\Big).

Since
\epsilon>0
can be arbitrarily small, we now obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{0}<t_{0},W^{(n)}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{(n)}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{0}<s_{0},W_{0}=w_{0};\ S_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big),

and the arbitrariness of the integer
K
, the strictly positive real numbers
(s_{k})_{k=0}^{K}
, and the sequence
(w_{k})_{k=0}^{K}\in\big(G^{\text{large}}\big)^{K+1}
allows us to conclude the proof.
∎

To extend the result above to the case where the communication class
G
is transient, we revisit the definition of the
Y^{\dagger}
in
eq.

H.26
. Let
\bar{G}=G^{\text{large}}\cup\{\bm{\dagger}\}
and let
m_{0}=\bm{\dagger}
(remember that all the local minimizers of
f
on
[-L,L]
are
m_{1},\cdots,m_{\text{min}}
). Meanwhile, using
q_{i}
and
q_{i,j}
in
eq.

H.20
eq.

H.21
, we can define

\displaystyle q^{\dagger}_{i,j}=\begin{cases}q_{i,j}&\text{if }i\geq 1,\ j\geq 1\\
\mathbbm{1}\{j=0\}&\text{if }i=0\\
\sum_{j\in[n_{\text{min}}],m_{j}\notin G}q_{i,j}&\text{if }i\geq 1,\ j=0.\end{cases}

and
q^{\dagger}_{0}=1,\ q^{\dagger}_{i}=q_{i}\ \forall i\geq 1.

Next, fix some
i
with
m_{i}\in G
and
x\in\Omega_{i}
. Define a sequence of random variables
(S^{\dagger}_{k})_{k\geq 0},(W^{\dagger}_{k})_{k\geq 0}
such that
S^{\dagger}_{k}=0
and
W_{0}=\pi_{G}(m_{i}),W^{\dagger}_{0}=\bm{\dagger}\mathbbm{1}\{W_{0}\notin G^{\text{large}}\}+W_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{W_{0}\in G^{\text{large}}\}
(see the definition of random mapping
\pi_{G}
in
eq.

H.22
) and (for all
k\geq 0
and
i,j
with
m_{j},m_{l}\in\bar{G}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(W^{\dagger}_{k+1}=m_{l},\ S^{\dagger}_{k+1}>t\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger}_{k}=m_{j},\ (W^{\dagger}_{l})_{l=0}^{k-1},\ (S^{\dagger}_{l})_{l=0}^{k}\Big)

(H.74)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(W^{\dagger}_{k+1}=m_{l},\ S^{\dagger}_{k+1}>t\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger}_{k}=m_{j}\Big)=\exp(-q^{\dagger}_{j}t)\frac{q^{\dagger}_{j,l}}{q^{\dagger}_{j}}\ \forall t>0

(H.75)

Then it is easy to see that
Y^{\dagger}(\pi_{G}(m_{i}))
defined in
eq.

H.26
is a
\big((S^{\dagger}_{k})_{k\geq 0},(W^{\dagger}_{k})_{k\geq 0}\big)
jump process. In particular, from at any state that is not
\bm{\dagger}
(namely, any
m_{j}
with
m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}
), the probability that
Y^{\dagger}
moves to
\bm{\dagger}
in the next transition is equal to the chance that, starting from the same state,
Y
moves to a state that is not in
G
. Once entering
m_{0}=\bm{\dagger}
, the process
Y^{\dagger}
will only make dummy jumps (with interarrival times being iid Exp(1)): indeed, we have
q^{\dagger}_{0}=q^{\dagger}_{0,0}=1
and
q^{\dagger}_{0,j}=0
for any
j\geq 1
, implying that, given
W^{\dagger}_{k}=m_{0}=\bm{\dagger}
, we must have
W^{\dagger}_{k+1}=m_{0}=\bm{\dagger}
. These dummy jumps ensure that
Y^{\dagger}
is stuck at the cemetery state
\bm{\dagger}
after visiting it.

Similarly, we can characterize the jump times and locations of the jump process
\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta,\Delta}
(for the definition, see
eq.

H.25
). When there is no ambiguity about the sequences
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1},(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, let
\hat{X}^{\dagger,(n)}=\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}
and
X^{\dagger,(n)}=X^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}}
. Also, recall that
\tau_{G}
defined in
eq.

H.4
is the step
n
when
X^{\eta}_{n}
exits the communication class
G
. Now let
(E_{k})_{k\geq 0}
be a sequence of iid Exp(1) random variables that is also independent of the noises
(Z_{k})_{k\geq 1}
(so they are independent from the SGD iterates
X^{\eta}_{n}
). For all
n\geq 1,k\geq 0
, define (see
eq.

H.6
-
eq.

H.11
and
eq.

H.15
for definitions of the quantities involved)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sigma^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})\wedge\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})-\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})&\text{if }\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})<\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\tau^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})\wedge\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})-\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})&\text{if }\tau^{*}_{k-1}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})<\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})\\
E_{k}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}m_{\widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})}&\text{if }\sigma^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})<\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})\\
\bm{\dagger}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}m_{I^{G}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})}&\text{if }\tau^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})<\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})\\
\bm{\dagger}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

with the convention that
\tau^{*}_{-1}=0
. Note that
\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})
is the scaled timestamp for
X^{(n)}=X^{*,\eta}
corresponding to
\tau_{G}(\eta_{n})
, hence
\textbf{T}^{*}(\tau_{G}(\eta_{n}),\eta_{n})=\min\{t\geq 0:X^{(n)}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\}
. One can see that
\hat{X}^{\dagger,(n)}
is a
\big((S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k})_{k\geq 0},(W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k})_{k\geq 0}\big)
jump process. The next lemma is similar to Lemma
H.14
and discusses the convergence of the jump times and locations of
\hat{X}^{\dagger,(n)}
on a communication class
G
in the transient case.

Lemma H.15
.

Assume that the communication class
G
is transient. Given any
m_{i}\in G
,
x\in\Omega_{i}
, finitely many real numbers
(t_{l})_{l=1}^{k^{\prime}}
such that
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{k^{\prime}}
, and a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\eta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow 0}\eta_{n}=0
, there exists a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that

•

Under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
(so
X^{\eta}_{0}=x
), as
n
tends to
\infty
,

\displaystyle(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{0},W^{\dagger,(n)}_{0},S^{\dagger,(n)}_{1},W^{\dagger,(n)}_{1},S^{\dagger,(n)}_{2},W^{\dagger,(n)}_{2},\cdots)\Rightarrow(S^{\dagger}_{0},W^{\dagger}_{0},S^{\dagger}_{1},W^{\dagger}_{1},S^{\dagger}_{2},W^{\dagger}_{2},\cdots)

(H.76)

•

For any
l\in[k^{\prime}]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{\dagger,(n)}_{t_{l}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n}),\ X^{\dagger,(n)}_{s}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall s\in[0,t_{l}]\Big)=0

(H.77)

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\nu_{j,k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mu_{j}(E_{j,k})\ \forall j,k\geq 1,\ j\neq k

\displaystyle p_{j,\dagger}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\sum_{\widetilde{j}:\ m_{\widetilde{j}}\notin G}p_{j,\widetilde{j}}\ \ \ \forall m_{j}\in G^{\text{small}}

\displaystyle q_{j,\dagger}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\sum_{k:m_{k}\notin G}\nu_{j,k}+\sum_{k:\ m_{k}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{j,k}p_{k,\dagger}\ \ \ \forall m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}.

In order to specify our choice of
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, we consider a construction of sequences
(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j))_{j\geq 0},(\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j))_{j\geq 0}
as follows. Fix some
\theta\in(0,\alpha-1)/2)
. Let
\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(0)=\bar{\bm{\eta}}(0)=1
. One can see the existence of some
(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j))_{j\geq 1},(\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j))_{j\geq 1}
such that

•

\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\in\big(0,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j-1)/2\big],\ \bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)\in\big(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j-1)/2\big]
for all
j\geq 1
;

•

(Due to Lemma
H.7
) for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
, (remember that
x
and
i
are the fixed constants prescribed in the description of the lemma)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma^{*}_{0}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)<\eta^{\theta},\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{0}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i\Big)>1-\frac{1}{2^{j}}.

For definitions of
\sigma^{G}_{k},\tau^{G}_{k},I^{G}_{k},\widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}
, see
eq.

H.6
-
eq.

H.11
.

•

(Due to Lemma
H.11
) for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\tau^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)-\sigma^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)<\eta^{\theta},\ I^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{2}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)-p_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle<1/2^{j}

uniformly for all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}},m_{i_{2}}\notin G^{\text{small}}
. Also, by definition of
\sigma^{*}
and
\tau^{*}
, we must have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\tau^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)-\sigma^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=0,\ I^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)=1

for all
k\geq 0
and
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}}
.

•

(Due to Proposition
G.25
) for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2^{j}}+\exp\big(-(1+\frac{1}{2^{j}})q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}-\frac{1}{2^{j}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma^{*}_{k+1}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)-\tau^{*}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)>u,\ \widetilde{I}^{G}_{k+1}=i_{2}\ \Big|\ I^{G}_{k}\big(\eta,\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)\big)=i_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{j}}+\exp\big(-(1-\frac{1}{2^{j}})q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\frac{1}{2^{j}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

uniformly for all
k\geq 1
, all
u>1/2^{j}
, and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}},m_{i_{2}}\in G
.

•

(Due to Lemma
H.12
) for any
j\geq 1
, for any
j\geq 1
,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{(n)}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j)),\ X^{(n)}_{s}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall s\in[0,t_{k}]\Big)<1/2^{j}

(H.78)

uniformly for all
k\in[k^{\prime}]
.

Fix such
(\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(j))_{j\geq 0},(\bar{\bm{\eta}}(j))_{j\geq 0}
. Define a function
\textbf{J}(\cdot):\mathbb{N}\mapsto\mathbb{N}
as

\displaystyle\textbf{J}(n)=0\vee\max\{j\geq 0:\ \bar{\bm{\eta}}(j)\geq\eta_{n}\}

with the convention that
\max\emptyset=-\infty
. Lastly, let

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}=\bar{\bm{\Delta}}(\textbf{J}(n))\ \forall n\geq 1.

Note that, due to
\lim_{n}\eta_{n}=0
, we have
\lim_{n}\textbf{J}(n)=\infty
, hence
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
. Besides, since
X^{\dagger,(n)}_{t}=X^{(n)}_{t}
given
X^{(n)}_{s}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}
for all
s\in[0,t]
, by combining
\lim_{n}\textbf{J}(n)=\infty
with
eq.

H.78
we obtain
eq.

H.77
.

Now it remains to prove
eq.

H.76
. To this end, it suffices to show that, for any positive integer
K
, we have
(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{0},W^{\dagger,(n)}_{0},\cdots,S^{\dagger,(n)}_{K},W^{\dagger,(n)}_{K})
converges in distribution
(S^{\dagger}_{0},W^{\dagger}_{0},\cdots,S^{\dagger}_{K},W^{\dagger}_{K})
as
n
tends to infinity. In particular, due to introduction of the dummy jumps, we know that for any
k
with
\tau^{*}_{k}(\eta_{n},\Delta_{n})\geq\tau_{G}(\eta_{n})
(in other words,
\hat{X}^{\dagger,(n)}
has reached state
\bm{\dagger}
within the first
k
jumps) we have
S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\sim\text{Exp}(1)
and
W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\equiv\bm{\dagger}
. Similarly, for any
k
with
S^{\dagger}_{0}+\cdots+S^{\dagger}_{k}\leq\tau^{Y}_{G}
, we have
S^{\dagger}_{k+1}\sim\text{Exp}(1)
and
W^{\dagger}_{k+1}\equiv\bm{\dagger}
. Therefore, it suffices to show that, for any positive integer
K
, any series of strictly positive real numbers
(s_{k})_{k=0}^{K}
, any sequence
(w_{k})_{k=0}^{K}\in\big(\bar{G}\big)^{K+1}
such that
w_{j}\neq\bm{\dagger}
for any
j<K
, indices
i_{k}
such that
w_{k}=m_{i_{k}}
for each
k
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{0}<t_{0},W^{\dagger,(n)}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S^{\dagger}_{0}<s_{0},W^{\dagger}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{\dagger}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{\dagger}_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

(H.79)

Fix some
(s_{k})_{k=0}^{K}
,
(w_{k})_{k=0}^{K}\in\big(\bar{G}\big)^{K+1}
, and indices
(i_{k})_{k=1}^{K}
satisfying the conditions above. Besides, arbitrarily choose some
\epsilon>0
so that
\epsilon<\min_{k=0,\cdots,K}s_{k}
. To proceed, we start by translating the inequalities established above under the new system of notations.

•

First of all, for all
n
sufficiently large, (remember that
x
and
i
are prescribed constants in the description of this lemma)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{0}<\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{0}=m_{i}\Big)>1-\epsilon.

(H.80)

•

For all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
, it holds for all
n
sufficiently large that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}<\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ {W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)-p_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bigg|<\epsilon.

(H.81)

•

On the other hand, for all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}}
, it holds for all
n
sufficiently large that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}<\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ {W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)-\sum_{i_{2}:\ m_{i_{2}}\notin G}p_{i_{1},i_{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}<\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ {W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)-p_{i_{1},\dagger}\bigg|<\epsilon.

(H.82)

•

For all
k\geq 0
and all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}}
, it holds for all
n
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=0,\ {W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)=1.

(H.83)

•

For all
k\geq 0
, all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}},m_{i_{2}}\in G
and all
u>\epsilon
, the following claim holds for all
n
sufficiently large:

\displaystyle-\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}-\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u-\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.84)

•

On the other hand, for all
k\geq 0
, all
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}}
, the following claim holds for all
n
sufficiently large:

\displaystyle-\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{-\epsilon+\sum_{i_{2}:\ m_{i_{2}}\notin G}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u-\eta_{n}^{\theta},\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\epsilon+\sum_{i_{2}:\ m_{i_{2}}\notin G}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.85)

•

Here is one implication of
eq.

H.81
eq.

H.82
. Since
|G|\leq n_{\text{min}}
, we have (when
n
is sufficiently large)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{k}-\widetilde{S}^{(n)}_{k}\geq\eta_{n}^{\theta}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)<n_{\text{min}}\cdot\epsilon

(H.86)

for all
k\geq 0
and
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{small}}
.

•

Note that for any
m_{i_{1}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
and any
k\geq 0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}\{i_{2}\neq i_{1}\}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\int_{s>0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big).

Fix some
i_{3}
with
m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}
. Due to
eq.

H.86

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(0,u-\eta^{\theta}_{n}]}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\geq u-s,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n_{\text{min}}\epsilon.

Meanwhile, by considering the integral on
(u-\eta_{n}^{\theta},\infty)
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(u-\eta^{\theta}_{n},\infty)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(u,\infty)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(p_{i_{3},i_{2}}-\epsilon)\Big(-\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}-\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}\Big)

due to
eq.

H.81
and
eq.

H.84
. As for the upper bound,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in(u-\eta^{\theta}_{n},\infty)}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(n_{\text{min}}\epsilon+p_{i_{3},i_{2}}+\epsilon)\Big(\epsilon+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}+\epsilon}{q_{i_{1}}}\Big)

due to
eq.

H.81
,
eq.

H.84
and
eq.

H.86
.

•

Therefore, for any
m_{i_{1}},m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{large}}
and any
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\mathbbm{1}\{i_{2}\neq i_{1}\}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}p_{i_{3},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{q_{i_{1},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.87)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{2}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\mathbbm{1}\{i_{2}\neq i_{1}\}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{i_{1},i_{3}}p_{i_{3},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{q_{i_{1},i_{2}}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.88)

where
q^{*}=\max_{i}q_{i}
and

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}

\displaystyle 2\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{q^{*}}+n_{\text{min}}(1+\epsilon)\epsilon+\epsilon\frac{1+\epsilon}{q^{*}}n_{\text{min}}

\displaystyle+n_{\text{min}}(\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{q^{*}})+n_{\text{min}}(n_{\text{min}}\epsilon+\epsilon+1)(1+\frac{1}{q^{*}})\epsilon.

Note that
\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}g(\epsilon)=0
.

•

On the other hand, for the case where the marker process
\hat{X}^{\dagger,(n)}
jumps to the cemetery state
\bm{\dagger}
from some
m_{i_{1}}\in G^{\text{large}}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{3}:\ m_{i_{3}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\int_{s>0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}\geq(u-s)\vee 0,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\widetilde{S}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=ds,\ \widetilde{W}^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=m_{i_{3}}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big).

Arguing similarly as we did above by considering the integral on
[0,u-\eta^{\theta}_{n}]
and
(u-\eta^{\theta}_{n},\infty)
separately, and using
eq.

H.82
and
eq.

H.85
, we then get (for all
n
sufficiently large)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\sum_{i_{2}:m_{i_{2}}\notin G}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{2}:m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}p_{i_{2},\dagger}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{q_{i_{1},\dagger}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.89)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}-S^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}>u,\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k+1}=\bm{\dagger}\ \Big|\ W^{\dagger,(n)}_{k}=m_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{\sum_{i_{2}:m_{i_{2}}\notin G}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{2}:m_{i_{2}}\in G^{\text{small}}}\nu_{i_{1},i_{2}}p_{i_{2},\dagger}}{q_{i_{1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-g(\epsilon)+\exp\big(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{1}}u\big)\frac{q_{i_{1},\dagger}}{q_{i_{1}}}

(H.90)

For simplicity of presentation, we also let
q_{j,0}=q_{j,\dagger}
and
p_{j,0}=p_{j,\dagger}
. First of all, remember that we have fixed some series of strictly positive real numbers
(s_{k})_{k=0}^{K}
, some sequence
(w_{k})_{k=0}^{K}\in\big(\bar{G}\big)^{K+1}
such that
w_{j}\neq\bm{\dagger}
for any
j<K
, and indices
i_{k}
such that
w_{k}=m_{i_{k}}
for each
k
. The definition of the continuous-time Markov chain
Y^{\dagger}
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{0}<t_{0},W_{0}=w_{0};\ S_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\pi_{G}(m_{i})=w_{0})\prod_{k=1}^{K}\Big(S_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W_{k}=m_{k}\ \Big|\ W_{k-1}=w_{k-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},\ i_{0}=i\}+\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\}p_{i_{0},i_{1}}\Big)\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\exp(-q_{i_{k-1}}s_{k})\frac{q_{i_{k-1},i_{k}}}{q_{i_{k-1}}}.

On the other hand, using
eq.

H.87
-
eq.

H.90
, we know that for all
n
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{0}<s_{0},W^{(n)}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{(n)}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{(n)}_{k}=w_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},\ i_{0}=i\}+\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\}(p_{i_{0},i_{1}}-\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\Big(-g(\epsilon)+\exp(-(1+\epsilon)q_{i_{k-1}}s_{k})\frac{q_{i_{k-1},i_{k}}}{q_{i_{k-1}}}\Big)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(S^{(n)}_{0}<s_{0},W^{(n)}_{0}=w_{0};\ S^{(n)}_{k}>s_{k}\ \text{and }W^{(n)}_{k}=m_{k}\ \forall k\in[K]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{large}},\ i_{0}=i\}+\mathbbm{1}\{m_{i}\in G^{\text{small}}\}(p_{i_{0},i_{1}}+\epsilon)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdot\prod_{k=1}^{K}\Big(g(\epsilon)+\exp(-(1-\epsilon)q_{i_{k-1}}s_{k})\frac{q_{i_{k-1},i_{k}}}{q_{i_{k-1}}}\Big).

The arbitrariness of
\epsilon>0
then allows us to establish
eq.

H.79
and conclude the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
H.4
and Lemma
H.5
.

Proof of Lemma
H.4
.

From Lemma
H.14
, one can see the existence of some
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that
eq.

H.60
-
eq.

H.63
hold. For simplicity of notations, we let
\hat{X}^{(n)}=\hat{X}^{*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}},X^{(n)}=X^{*,\eta_{n}}
, and let
\bar{t}=t_{k^{\prime}}

Combine
eq.

H.60
with Lemma
H.13
, and we immediately get
eq.

H.23
. In order to prove
eq.

H.24
, it suffices to show that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{(n)}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\Big)\leq 4\epsilon\ \forall k\in[k^{\prime}].

Fix
\epsilon>0
, and observe following bound by decomposing the events

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{(n)}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(X^{(n)}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n}),\ X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall t\in[0,\bar{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists t\in[0,\bar{t}]\ \text{such that }X^{(n)}_{t}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\Big)

Therefore, given
eq.

H.63
, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists t\in[0,\bar{t}]\ \text{such that }X^{(n)}_{t}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\Big)\leq 3\epsilon.

(H.91)

Let

\displaystyle T^{(n)}_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{t\geq 0:\ X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in G}B(m_{j},2\Delta_{n})\}

\displaystyle I^{(n)}_{0}

\displaystyle=j\iff X^{(n)}_{T^{(n)}_{0}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle T^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{t>T^{(n)}_{k-1}:\ X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in G,\ j\neq I^{(n)}_{k-1}}B(m_{j},2\Delta_{n})\}

\displaystyle I^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle=j\iff X^{(n)}_{T^{(n)}_{k}}\in B(m_{j},2\Delta_{n}).

Building upon this definition, we define the following stopping times and marks that only records the hitting time to minimizer in
large
attraction fields in
G
(with convention
\textbf{k}^{(n),\text{large}}(-1)=-1,T^{(n),\text{large}}_{-1}=0,T^{(n)}_{-1}=0
)

\displaystyle\textbf{k}^{(n),\text{large}}(k)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{l>\textbf{k}^{(n),\text{large}}({k-1}):\ m_{I^{(n)}_{l}}\in G^{\text{large}}\}

\displaystyle T^{(n),\text{large}}_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}T^{(n)}_{\textbf{k}^{(n),\text{large}}(k)},\ \ \ I^{(n),\text{large}}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}I^{(n)}_{\textbf{k}^{(n),\text{large}}(k)}.

Now by defining

\displaystyle J^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\{k\geq 0:\ T^{(n)}_{k}\leq t\},

\displaystyle J^{(n)}_{\text{large}}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\{k\geq 0:\ T^{(n),\text{large}}_{k}\leq t\},

\displaystyle J^{(n)}(s,t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\{k\geq 0:\ T^{(n)}_{k}\in[s,t]\},

we use
J^{(n)}(t)
to count the numbers of visits to local minima on
G
, and
J^{(n)}_{\text{large}}(t)
for the number of visits to minimizers in the
large
attraction fields on
G
.
J^{(n)}(s,t)
counts the indices
k
such that at
T^{(n)}_{k}
a minimizer on
G
is visited and regarding the hitting time we have
T^{(n)}_{k}\in[s,t]
.

First of all, the weak convergence result in
eq.

H.60
implies the existence of some positive integer
N(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}(J^{(n)}_{\text{large}}(\bar{t})>N(\epsilon))<\epsilon.

Fix such
N(\epsilon)
. Next, from
eq.

H.62
, we know the existence of some integer
K(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(J^{(n)}\big(T^{(n),\text{large}}_{k-1},T^{(n),\text{large}}_{k}\big)>K(\epsilon)\Big)\leq\epsilon/N(\epsilon).

Fix such
K(\epsilon)
as well. From the results above, we know that for event

\displaystyle A_{1}(n)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{J^{(n)}_{\text{large}}(\bar{t})\leq N(\epsilon)\}\cap\Big\{J^{(n)}\big(T^{(n),\text{large}}_{k-1},T^{(n),\text{large}}_{k}\big)\leq K(\epsilon)\ \forall k\in[N(\epsilon)]\Big\},

we have
\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\big(A_{1}(n)\big)^{c}\Big)\leq 2\epsilon
. On the other hand, on event
A_{1}(n)
, we must have

\displaystyle J^{(n)}(\bar{t})\leq N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon).

Meanwhile, it follows immediately from
eq.

H.61
that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\sup_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists t\in[T^{(n)}_{k-1},T^{(n)}_{k}]\ \text{such that }X^{(n)}_{t}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\Big)<\frac{\epsilon}{N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon)},

hence for event

\displaystyle A_{2}(n)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall t\in[0,T^{(n)}_{N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon)}]\Big\},

we must have
\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\big(A_{2}(n)\big)^{c}\Big)\leq\epsilon
. To conclude the proof, note that

\displaystyle A_{1}(n)\cap A_{2}(n)

\displaystyle\subseteq\{J^{(n)}(\bar{t})\leq N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon)\}\cap\Big\{X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall t\in[0,T^{(n)}_{N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon)}]\Big\}

\displaystyle=\{T^{(n)}_{N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon)}\geq\bar{t}\}\cap\Big\{X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall t\in[0,T^{(n)}_{N(\epsilon)K(\epsilon)}]\Big\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\Big\{X^{(n)}_{t}\in\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall t\in[0,\bar{t}]\Big\}

so we have established
eq.

H.91
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
H.5
.

From Lemma
H.15
, one can see the existence of some
(\Delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\lim_{n}\Delta_{n}=0
such that
eq.

H.76
and
eq.

H.77
hold. For simplicity of notations, we let
\hat{X}^{\dagger,(n)}=\hat{X}^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n},\Delta_{n}},X^{\dagger,(n)}=X^{\dagger,*,\eta_{n}}
, and let
\bar{t}=t_{k^{\prime}}

Combine
eq.

H.76
with Lemma
H.13
, and we immediately get
eq.

H.27
. In order to prove
eq.

H.28
,
note that

\displaystyle\Big\{X^{\dagger,(n)}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\text{ and }X^{\dagger,(n)}_{t_{k}}\neq\bm{\dagger}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{X^{\dagger,(n)}_{t_{k}}\notin\bigcup_{j:\ m_{j}\in G^{\text{large}}}B(m_{j},\Delta_{n})\text{ and }X^{\dagger,(n)}_{s}\in\bigcup_{j:m_{j}\in G}\Omega_{j}\ \forall s\in[0,t_{k}]\Big\}

so the conclusion of the proof follows directly from
eq.

H.77
.
∎

Appendix I
First Exit Time of Truncated Heavy-tailed SGD in
\mathbb{R}^{d}

I.1
Main Result

The object of interests is the following truncated heavy-tailed SGD iterates

\displaystyle X^{\eta}_{k+1}(x)

\displaystyle=X^{\eta}_{k}(x)+\varphi_{b}\big(-\eta\nabla f(X^{\eta}_{k}(x))+\eta Z_{k+1}\big)\ \forall k\geq 0

where the initial condition is prescribed by
X_{0}(x)=x
,
f
is a real-valued function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\eta>0
is the learning rate,
(Z_{k})_{k\geq 1}
is the sequence of heavy-tailed noises, the standard gradient clipping operator is
\varphi_{b}(v)=\min\{1,\frac{b}{\left\lVert v\right\rVert}\}\cdot v
with
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert
being
L_{2}
norm.
To ease notations, we also use
\mathbb{P}_{x}
to denote the conditional law on
\{X_{0}^{\eta}=x\}
.

Specifically, we are interested in the first exit time of
X^{\eta}_{n}
from a domain
\mathcal{G}
, i.e. the stopping time

\sigma(\eta)=\min\{n\geq 0:X^{\eta}_{n}\notin\mathcal{G}\}.

We work with following assumptions.

Assumption I.1
.

The region
\mathcal{G}
is connected, bounded and open, and
\textbf{0}\in\mathcal{G}
.

Assumption I.2
.

The function
f
is smooth, i.e.
f\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Assumption I.3
.

The boundary set
\partial\mathcal{G}
is a
(n-1)
-dimensional manifold of class
C^{2}
such that the vector field
n(\cdot)
of the outer normals on
\partial\mathcal{G}
exists with

\displaystyle\nabla f(v)^{T}n(v)\geq c_{0}\ \ \ \forall v\in\partial\mathcal{G}

(I.1)

for some constant
c_{0}>0
.

Assumption I.4
.

For
\nabla^{2}f(\bm{0})
, the Hessian of
f(\cdot)
at point
\bm{0}
, all the eigenvalues are strictly positive.

For any
x\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}
, let
\bm{x}_{t}(x)
be the ODE flow with
\bm{x}_{0}(x)=x
solving

\displaystyle\bm{\dot{x}}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=-\nabla f\big(\bm{x}_{t}(x)\big)\ \ \forall t\geq 0,

Assumption I.5
.

W.L.O.G., the origin
\bm{0}
is an attractor of the domain, i.e.
\nabla f(\bm{0})=\bm{0}
and
0
is asymptotically stable in
\mathcal{G}
in the sense that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\textbf{x}_{t}(x)=\textbf{0}\ \ \ \forall x\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}.

We have the following assumption regarding the heavy-tailed noises
(Z_{n})_{n\geq 1}
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},x\neq 0
, define the following polar transformation

\displaystyle\textbf{T}(x)=(\|x\|,x/\|x\|)

(I.2)

with
\textbf{T}_{r}(x)=\left\lVert x\right\rVert,\textbf{T}_{\theta}(x)=x/\left\lVert x\right\rVert
. Also, let
\mathbb{O}=\{\bm{0}\}

Assumption I.6
.

\mathbb{E}Z_{1}=\textbf{0}
.
Besides, there exist a positive integer
m\geq 1
, a sequence
1<\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}<\cdots<\alpha_{m}<\infty
, a sequence of slowly-varying functions
(l_{1},\cdots,l_{m})
, a sequence of probability measures
(S_{1},\cdots,S_{m})
on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with support
F_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\text{supp}(S_{j})
as closed sets on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that

•

F_{i}\cap F_{j}=\emptyset
for any
i\neq j
;

•

For any
j\in[m]
, define the cone
E_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\textbf{T}_{\theta}^{-1}(F_{j})\cup\mathbb{O}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and measure
\mathbb{P}^{(j)}(\cdot)=\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}\in\cdot\cap E_{j})
, we have

\displaystyle t^{\alpha_{j}}\cdot l_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbb{P}^{(j)}\circ\textbf{T}^{-1}\big(t\cdot dr\times d\theta\big)\rightarrow\nu_{\alpha_{j}}(dr)\times S_{j}(d\theta)\ \ \ \text{as }t\rightarrow\infty

(I.3)

in the sense of
\mathbb{M}(E_{j}\char 92\relax\mathbb{O})
. Here
\nu_{\alpha}
is a Borel measure defined on
(0,\infty)
satisfying
\nu_{\alpha}[t,\infty)=t^{-\alpha}\ \ \forall t>0
;

•

For measure
\mathbb{P}^{(0)}(\cdot)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{1}\in\cdot\ \char 92\relax(\cup_{j=1}^{m}E_{j})\big)
and any
\alpha>0
,

\displaystyle t^{\alpha}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{(0)}\circ\textbf{T}^{-1}\big(t\cdot dr\times d\theta\big)\rightarrow 0\ \ \ \text{as }t\rightarrow\infty

(I.4)

in the sense of
\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\char 92\relax\mathbb{O})
;

•

For any
j\in[m]
, the measure
S_{j}
is absolutely continuous w.r.t. spherical measure on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

A function
l:\mathbb{R}_{+}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
is slowly varying (at
\infty
) if
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}l(tx)/l(x)=1
holds for any
x>0
. For details on
\mathbb{M}-
convergence and regular variation in general metric spaces, see
Lindskog et al. 2014
. Here we state one implication of the assumption. For any
j=0,1,\cdots,m
, let

\displaystyle H_{j}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\mathbb{P}_{j}\Big(\{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\ \|\bm{y}\|\geq x\}\Big).

(I.5)

The assumption above immediately implies that, for any
j=1,\cdots,m
,
H_{j}(\cdot)
is regularly varying (at
\infty
) with index
-\alpha_{j}
(denoted as
H_{j}\in RV_{-\alpha_{j}}
), namely
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}H_{j}(tx)/H_{j}(t)=x^{-\alpha_{j}}\ \ \forall x>0.

Meanwhile, for any
\alpha>0
,
H_{0}(x)=o(1/x^{\alpha})
as
x\rightarrow\infty
.

The asymptotically behavior of the first exit time hinges on the following geometric characterization of the domain
\mathcal{G}
.
For any integer
k\geq 1
,
a sequence of strictly positive real numbers
(t_{2},\cdots,t_{k})
with
t_{1}=0
,
a sequence of non-zero vectors
(w_{1},\cdots,w_{k})

and some
\eta>0
,
let
\bm{t}^{(k)}=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k}),\bm{w}^{(k)}=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{k})
, and define the ODE path with
k
jumps (clipped at size
b
) by

\bm{w}^{(k)}=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{k})
, we define perturbed ODE path
\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}(0;\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{b}(\eta w_{1});

\displaystyle\frac{d\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}(t,x;\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})}{dt}

\displaystyle=-\eta\nabla f\big(\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}(t,x;\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})\big)\ \ \ \forall t\notin\{t_{1},t_{1}+t_{2},\cdots,\sum_{j=1}^{k}t_{j}\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}(t,x;\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}(t-,x;\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})+\varphi_{b}(\eta w_{j})\ \ \ \ \text{if }t=\sum_{i=1}^{j}t_{i}\text{ for some }j

with the convention that
g(t-)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\lim_{s\uparrow t}g(s)
for any function
g
. Also, when
\eta=1
we simply write
\widetilde{\bm{x}}
.
Now we can assign a
cost
to each jump
w_{j}
based on the direction using the following function:

\displaystyle J(w)=\begin{cases}\alpha_{j}-1&\text{if }\ w\neq 0,w\in E_{j},\\
\infty&\text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

(I.6)

Given any set of perturbations described by
\bm{t}^{(k)}\in\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k-1}_{+},\bm{w}^{(k)}\in\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}\char 92\relax\mathbb{O}\big)^{k}
, we identify the
destination
of the flow as

\displaystyle h(k;\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})=\widetilde{\bm{x}}(\sum_{j=1}^{k}t_{k};\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)}).

Besides, define function
\mathcal{I}(\bm{w}^{(k)})=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})
if
\#\{i\in[k]:\ w_{i}\in E_{j}\}=i_{j}
for all
j\in[m]
. This allows us to define the following configuration sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\big\{\bm{w}^{(k)}\in\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}\char 92\relax\mathbb{O}\big)^{k}:\ \mathcal{I}(\bm{w}^{(k)})=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m}),\ k=\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{j}\big\}

(I.7)

for any
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
, i.e., some set of jumps
\bm{w}^{(k)}
is said to have configuration
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})
or belong to the configuration set
\mathcal{A}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})
if the number of jumps in cone
E_{j}
is equal to
i_{j}
.
We can also define the cost for each configuration as

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\alpha_{j}-1)i_{j}.

(I.8)

Now we can characterize the minimum
cost
to exit
\mathcal{G}
:

\displaystyle J_{\mathcal{G}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}

\displaystyle\min\{\sum_{j=1}^{k}J(w_{j}):\ \exists k\in\mathbb{N},\bm{t}^{(k)}\in\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k-1}_{+},\bm{w}^{(k)}\in\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}\char 92\relax\mathbb{O}\big)^{k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{ s.t. }\widetilde{\bm{x}}(\sum_{j=1}^{k}t_{k},\bm{0};\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})\notin\mathcal{G}\}.

(I.9)

From the boundedness of
\mathcal{G}
, one can see that
0<J_{\mathcal{G}}<\infty
regardless of the actual value of the clipping threshold
b>0
.
We need the following technical assumption regarding the configurations of jumps that can trigger the exit with the minimum cost.

Assumption I.7
.

There exists only one array
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{j}(\alpha_{j}-1)=J_{\mathcal{G}}
and (for
k=\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{m}
)

\displaystyle\exists\bm{t}^{(k)}\in\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k-1}_{+},\bm{w}^{(k)}\in\mathcal{A}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\text{ s.t. }\widetilde{\bm{x}}(\sum_{j=1}^{k}t_{k},\bm{0};\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)})\notin\mathcal{G}.

We use
\bm{i}^{*}=(i^{*}_{1},\cdots,i^{*}_{m})
to denote the unique configuration in Assumption
I.7
and
k^{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}i^{*}_{j}
. The implication is that, for a set of jumps
\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)}
with
any other configuration
, one of the following must happen: (i) this set of jumps has a cost strictly higher than
J_{G}
; (ii) this set of jumps cannot send the ODE flow out of
\mathcal{G}
.

Meanwhile, we introduce the following concept as the
coverage
of a certain configuration set.

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\big\{\widetilde{\bm{x}}(s,\bm{0};\bm{t}^{(k)},\bm{w}^{(k)}):

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ k=\sum_{j=1}^{m}i_{j},\ \bm{t}^{(k)}\in\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k-1}_{+},\bm{w}^{(k)}\in\mathcal{A}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m}),\ s\in[0,\sum_{j=1}^{k}t_{j}]\big\}.

(I.10)

It is easy to see that, for any configuration
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})
with cost
\mathcal{J}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\leq J_{\mathcal{G}}
, the coverage
\mathcal{G}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})
is a closed set, and the following technical assumption holds for (Lebesgue) almost every
b>0
. Here the distance
\bm{d}(A,B)=\inf_{x\in A,y\in B}\left\lVert x-y\right\rVert
for any
A,B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
A^{\circ}
is the interior of the set
A
.

Assumption I.8
.

For any
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
, one of the following must occur:

•

\bm{d}\big(\mathcal{G}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m}),\mathcal{G}^{c})>0
;

•

\big(\mathcal{G}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\cap\mathcal{G}^{c}\big)^{\circ}\neq\emptyset

Note that with
\bm{i}^{*}
and
k^{*}
defined above, we can define the following mapping
h
from
\bm{r}=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{k^{*}}),\bm{\theta}=(\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{k^{*}})\in(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})^{k^{*}},\bm{t}=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k^{*}})\in\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}_{+}
such that

\displaystyle h(\bm{r},\bm{\theta},\bm{t})=\widetilde{\bm{x}}(\sum_{i=1}^{k^{*}}t_{i},\bm{0};\ \bm{t},\bm{w})

where
\bm{w}=(w_{i})_{i=1}^{k^{*}}
with
w_{i}=r_{i}\theta_{i}
.
Also, we introduce the concept of
type
for configuration
\bm{i}^{*}
. Specifically, define

\displaystyle\bm{j}(i_{1},\cdots,i_{m})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{(j_{1},\cdots,j_{k^{*}})\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,m\}^{k^{*}}:\ \#\{n:\ j_{n}=k\}=i_{k}\ \forall k\in[m]\big\}

and for any
\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})
, we say that
\bm{w}=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{k^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}(\bm{i}^{*})
has type
\bm{j}
if

\displaystyle w_{i}\in E_{j_{i}}\ \forall i\in[k^{*}].

In other words, based on the direction of each jump in
\bm{w}\in\mathcal{A}(\bm{i}^{*})
we group them into different types. Note that
|\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})|<\infty
.
Now for any type
\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})
, define a (Borel) measure on
\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}}_{+}\times(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})^{k^{*}}\times\mathbb{R}^{k^{*}-1}_{+}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{\bm{j}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(\prod_{i=1}^{k^{*}}\nu_{\alpha_{\bm{j}_{i}}})\times(\prod_{i=1}^{k^{*}}S_{\bm{j}_{i}})\times\bm{m}_{\text{Leb}}^{k^{*}-1}

where, for any
\alpha>0
, the measure
\nu_{\alpha}
is the Borel measure on
(0,\infty)
with
\nu_{\alpha}(x,\infty)=1/x^{1+\alpha}
.
Lastly, define measure
\mu
as

\mu=\sum_{\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})}\mu_{\bm{j}}.

As will be established in Lemma
K.2
, the following technical assumption is also a very moderate one since it holds for (Lebesgue) almost every
b>0
under the current setting.

Assumption I.9
.

The set
h^{-1}\big(\partial\mathcal{G}\big)
has zero mass under the measure
\mu
.

Having specified the problem setting, we are now ready to present Theorem
I.1
, the main result of this section.
The implication of the theorem is clear:
under proper scaling, the first exit time
\sigma(\eta)
converges in distribution to an Exponential random variable.
Moreover, the scaling
\lambda(\eta)
is roughly of order
\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}}
, implying that the first exit time
\sigma(\eta)
is roughly of order
1/\eta^{1+\mathcal{G}}
as the learning rate
\eta
approaches
0
.

Theorem I.1
.

Let Assumptions
I.1
-
I.9
hold.
There exists a function
\lambda(\eta)
that is regularly varying (as
\eta\downarrow 0
) with index
(1+J_{\mathcal{G}})
such that,
for any
x\in\mathcal{G}
and any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\sigma(\eta)\lambda(\eta)>t)=\exp(-qt)

where the constant
q=\mu\big(h^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\big)
.

The proof is provided in Section
J
.
As a concluding remark,
we stress that the order of the first exit time is dictated by
J_{\mathcal{G}}
, the minimum cost for exit we introduced above.
Bearing obvious similarity to the first exit time analysis of SDE driven by heavy-tailed Lévy processes in
Imkeller et al. 2010a
,
our result can be viewed as a natural generalization when gradient clipping is applied and heavy-tailed noises may not always align with a finite number of lines.
In
Imkeller et al. 2010a
, the unclipped setting implies that the escape from the domain
\mathcal{G}
can always be achieved with one big jump.
However, a big perturbation (in noise) along different directions may correspond to different heavy-tailed indices, i.e., induce different costs given our definition of cost function
J(w)
in
eq.

I.6
.
In Theorem 1 of
Imkeller et al. 2010a
, we see that the order of the first exit time is determined by
\alpha_{1}
, the smallest heavy-tailed index.
The underlying reason is that the exit is almost always trigger by a single big jump with the smallest cost.
Similarly, in our setting where multiple jumps are required for escape due to the clipping mechanism,
we see that the order of the first exit time is not dictated by the number of jumps or the accumulated distances of the jumps, but the smallest possible accumulated
cost
defined as the summation of
J(w_{i})
where
w_{i}
’s are the jumps the lead to escape from
\mathcal{G}
.
As detailed in the proof, this is because such jumps with the smallest costs
J_{\mathcal{G}}
dictates the most likely way for exiting
\mathcal{G}
.

I.2
A Special Case: Uniform Heavy Tail Index Along All Directions
