\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)}\wedge\frac{\epsilon}{\bar{\rho}(\hat{t}(\epsilon))}\wedge\frac{\epsilon}{\widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t}(\epsilon))}

(J.51)

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.
Furthermore, define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup A^{\times}_{2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

(J.52)

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)\Big\}

(J.53)

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.
By definition of
l^{*}
, we must have
K^{\eta}(\delta)<l^{*}
.
Lastly, define events (for any
K\in[l^{*}]
)

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{1}(K)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}<J_{\mathcal{G}},\ K\leq K^{\eta}(\delta)\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\forall j\in[K],\ [T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)]-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\leq\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\},

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{2}(K)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}\leq J_{\mathcal{G}},\ K\leq K^{\eta}(\delta)\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\exists j\in[K]\text{ s.t. }[T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)]-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\}.

Recall that the minimum cost for exit
J_{\mathcal{G}}
is defined in
eq.

I.9
. Note that, in the definition of
B^{\times}_{1}(K)
and
B^{\times}_{2}(K)
above,
T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)
only if
j=K=K^{\eta}(\delta)
.

Lemma J.15
.

For any
K\in[l^{*}]
, any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
and any
\eta>0
sufficiently small, the following claim holds on event
B^{\times}_{1}(K)
:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}(x)-\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,X^{(1)}(x);\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\right\rVert<2\epsilon

where

\bm{T}=\big(0,T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),T^{\eta}_{3}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{K}(\delta)\big)
,

\bm{W}=\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\big)
,
and
X^{(1)}(x)=X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)-1}(x)
.

Proof.

We focus on the distances between the following three objects:
X^{\eta}_{n}(x)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{n}(x)=\begin{cases}X^{\eta}_{n}(x)&\text{ if }n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta);\\
\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{n-1}(x)+\varphi_{b}\big(-\eta\nabla f\big(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{n-1}(x)\big)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathbbm{1}\{n=T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\}\eta Z_{n}\big)&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\bm{z}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,X^{(1)}(x);\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0.

First of all, for
X^{(1)}=X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)-1}(x)=\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)-1}(x)
, by definition of event
(A^{\times})^{c}
we have
\left\lVert X^{(1)}\right\rVert<3\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}
.
Then due to
eq.

J.20
and the definition of event
B^{\times}_{1}(K)
(in particular, the fact that
\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}<J_{\mathcal{G}}
), we know that

\displaystyle\bm{d}(\bm{z}(s),\mathcal{G}^{c})>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}\ \ \forall s\in\big[0,T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\big).

In light of the definition of
B^{\times}_{1}(K)
(i.e. the upper bound on
T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)
), by applying Corollary
J.6
, we can show that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\left\lVert\bm{z}(s)-\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}(x)\right\rVert<\epsilon.

In particular, by applying Corollary
J.6
inductively for all
n\leq T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)
, we know that the line segment between
\bm{z}(s)
and
\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}(x)
is in
\mathcal{G}
for all
s<T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)
so Corollary
J.6
can be further applied to
n+1
and establish the inequality above.

Similarly, due to our choice of
\hat{\epsilon}(\epsilon)
in
eq.

J.51
and the upper bound on
T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)
in definition of event
B^{\times}_{1}(K)
, by applying Corollary
J.9
inductively, we know that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{j=0,1,2,\cdots,T^{\eta}_{K+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}\left\lVert\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}(x)-X^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}(x)\right\rVert<\epsilon

holds on event
B^{\times}_{1}(K)
and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma J.16
.

For any
K\in[l^{*}]
, any
\epsilon>0
and any
\eta>0
sufficiently small,

B^{\times}_{2}(K)=\emptyset.

Proof.

The definition of event
B^{\times}_{2}(K)
ensures that we can define some
j^{*}
as the smallest integer in
[K^{\eta}(\delta)]
such that for
T^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}T^{\eta}_{j^{*}}(\delta)+\lceil 2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\rceil
, we have

\displaystyle T^{\eta}_{j^{*}}(\delta)<T^{*}<T^{\eta}_{j^{*}+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta).

(J.54)

Analogous to the proof of the previous lemma, we focus on the pair-wise distances between the following three objects:
X^{\eta}_{n}(x)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{n}(x)=\begin{cases}X^{\eta}_{n}(x)&\text{ if }n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta);\\
\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{n-1}(x)+\varphi_{b}\big(-\eta\nabla f\big(\widetilde{Y}^{\eta}_{n-1}(x)\big)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathbbm{1}\{n=T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\}\eta Z_{n}\big)&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\bm{z}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,X^{(1)};\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0.

with

\bm{T}=\big(0,T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),T^{\eta}_{3}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,T^{\eta}_{j^{*}+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j^{*}}(\delta)\big)
,

\bm{W}=\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{j^{*}+1}(\delta)\big)
,
and
X^{(1)}=X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}
.
Again, using Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
, one can see that for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small, we must have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{*}-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}(x)-\bm{z}(s)\right\rVert<2\epsilon

on event
B^{\times}_{2}(K)
.
However,
eq.

J.20
and
\left\lVert X^{(1)}\right\rVert<3\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{\eta}_{j^{*}}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\bm{d}\big(\bm{z}(s),\ \mathcal{G}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}.

In the meantime, Lemma
J.4
and our choice of
\widetilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)
in
eq.

J.51
implies that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{z}(T^{*}-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta))\right\rVert<\epsilon\text{ and }\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{T^{*}}(x)\right\rVert<3\epsilon,

thus dictating that
R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<T^{*}
and contradicting
eq.

J.54
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to apply the tools above and analyze some atypical scenarios regrading the first exit and first return time.
In the next result we show that, when starting from the local minimum, it is very unlikely to escape if the total cost of large jumps is less than
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.

Lemma J.17
.

For any fixed
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3),N>0
,
the following claim holds for any sufficiently small
\delta>0
:

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma(\eta)<R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),\ \mathcal{J}^{\downarrow}_{\eta,\delta}(\sigma(\eta))<J_{\mathcal{G}}\Big)=o(\eta^{N})\ \ \text{as }\eta\downarrow 0.

Proof.

Since the constant
\epsilon>0
is fixed, one can see that
\hat{t}=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
is also fixed where
c_{1}
is the constant in Lemma
J.4
.
Besides, we can fix some
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}<\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)},\ \ \bar{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon,\ \ \widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.

Let us define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}\cup A^{\times}_{2}
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}\Big\}

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.
By definition of
l^{*}
, we must have
K^{\eta}(\delta)<l^{*}
on event
\{\mathcal{J}^{\downarrow}_{\eta,\delta}(\sigma(\eta))<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}
.

Now let us analyze the dynamics of
X^{\eta}_{n}
on event
(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\downarrow}_{\eta,\delta}(\sigma(\eta))<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}
.
In particular, we decompose it into two events

\displaystyle B_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\downarrow}_{\eta,\delta}(\sigma(\eta))<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\forall j=1,2,\cdots,K^{\eta}(\delta),\ [T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)]-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\leq 2\hat{t}/\eta\},

\displaystyle B_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\downarrow}_{\eta,\delta}(\sigma(\eta))<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\exists j=1,2,\cdots,K^{\eta}(\delta)\text{ s.t. }[T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)]-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>2\hat{t}/\eta\}.

Using Lemma
J.15
and the fact that
B_{1}=\big(\bigcup_{K=1}^{l^{*}}B^{\times}_{1}(K)\cap\{K^{\eta}(\delta)=K\}\big)\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}
, one can see that for any
\eta>0
sufficiently small, we must have

\displaystyle\bm{d}(X^{\eta}_{\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)}(x),\mathcal{G}^{c})>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}-2\epsilon>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}-\bar{\epsilon}>0.

on event
B_{1}
.
In other words, on event
B_{1}
we must have
R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<\sigma(\eta)
.

On the other hand, Lemma
J.16
the fact that
B_{2}\subseteq\bigcup_{K=1}^{l^{*}}B^{\times}_{2}(K)\cap\{K^{\eta}(\delta)=K\}
implies that
B_{2}=\emptyset
whenever
\eta>0
is sufficiently small.

In summary, we have established that

\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sigma(\eta)<R(\epsilon,\eta),\ \mathcal{J}^{\downarrow}_{\eta,\delta}(\sigma(\eta))<J_{\mathcal{G}}\Big)\leq\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times}).

Lastly, from Lemma
J.10
and
J.12
,
one can see that for any
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})=o(\eta^{N}),

and this concludes the proof.
∎

Using an almost identical approach, we can establish the next two results and conclude that it is also rather unlikely to have scenarios where

•

The accumulated cost of large jumps exceeds
J_{\mathcal{G}}
before the first return or exit,

•

Or the first return occurs before the first exit,

yet it takes rather long for the said event to occur.

Lemma J.18
.

Given
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3),N>0
, the following claim holds for any sufficiently small
\delta>0
:

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\overline{B(\bm{0},2\epsilon)}}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists K\in\mathbb{N}\text{ s.t. }\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}\text{ and }T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\text{ for some }j=2,3,\cdots,K\Big)

\displaystyle=o(\eta^{N})

as
\eta\downarrow 0
where
\hat{t}(\epsilon)=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
.

Proof.

Given the fixed
\epsilon>0
, define
\hat{t}=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
. Also, fix
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
as the largest possible value such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)},\ \ \bar{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon,\ \ \widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.

Let us define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}\cup A^{\times}_{2}
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}\Big\}

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.
Furthermore,
define event

\displaystyle B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\Big\{\exists K\in\mathbb{N}\text{ s.t. }\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}\text{ and }T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for some }j=2,3,\cdots,K\Big\}.

Note that on event
B
, the index

\displaystyle K^{*}=\max\{k\geq 1:\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{j}<J_{\mathcal{G}}\ \forall j=1,2,\cdots,k-1\}

is well defined with
1\leq K^{*}<l^{*}
(due to the definition of
l^{*}
).
As a consequence, we must have
T^{\eta}_{K^{*}}(\delta)\leq\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
.

Now based on the exact value of
K^{*}
, we can decompose the event
B
into
B=\cup_{K=0}^{l^{*}}B(K)
where

\displaystyle B(K)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{*}-1=K\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\{T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\ \text{for some }j\in[K]\}.

By applying Lemma
J.16
, we know that for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small,
B(K)=\emptyset
. Therefore, for all sufficiently small
\eta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\overline{B(\bm{0},2\epsilon)}}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\exists K\in\mathbb{N}\text{ s.t. }\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}\text{ and }T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\text{ for some }j=2,3,\cdots,K\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\overline{B(\bm{0},2\epsilon)}}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\times}).

Lastly, from Lemma
J.10
and
J.12
,
one can see that for any
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})=o(\eta^{N}),

and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma J.19
.

Given
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3),N>0
, the following claim holds for any sufficiently small
\delta>0
:

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\overline{B(\bm{0},2\epsilon)}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<\sigma(\eta),\ J^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}<J_{\mathcal{G}},

\displaystyle T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\text{ for some }j\in[K^{\eta}(\delta)]\Big)=o(\eta^{N})\ \ \text{ as }\eta\downarrow 0

where
\hat{t}(\epsilon)=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
.

Proof.

Given the fixed
\epsilon>0
, define
\hat{t}=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
. Also, fix
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
as the largest possible value such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)},\ \ \bar{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon,\ \ \widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.

Let us define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}\cup A^{\times}_{2}
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}\Big\}

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.
By definition of
l^{*}
, we must have
K^{\eta}(\delta)<l^{*}
on event
\{J^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}
.
Furthermore,
define event

\displaystyle B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\Big\{R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<\sigma(\eta),\ J^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}<J_{\mathcal{G}},

\displaystyle T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\text{ for some }j\in[K^{\eta}(\delta)]\Big\}.

Now observe that
B\subseteq\bigcup_{K=1}^{l^{*}}B(K)
where

\displaystyle B(K)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta}(\delta)=K\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\exists j\in[K^{\eta}(\delta)]\text{ s.t. }[T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)]-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\}.

By applying Lemma
J.16
, we know that for all
\eta>0
sufficiently small,
B(K)=\emptyset
. Therefore, for all sufficiently small
\eta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\overline{B(\bm{0},2\epsilon)}}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<\sigma(\eta),\ J^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}<J_{\mathcal{G}},

\displaystyle T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\text{ for some }j\in[K^{\eta}(\delta)]\Big)\leq\sup_{x\in\overline{B(\bm{0},2\epsilon)}}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\times}).

Lastly, from Lemma
J.10
and
J.12
,
one can see that for any
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})=o(\eta^{N}),

and this concludes the proof.
∎

Recall that in Lemma
J.17
, we have shown that it is rather unlikely to have the first exit with accumulated cost of large jumps less than
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
In the next result, we show that even with large jumps of accumulated cost
J_{\mathcal{G}}
,
if some jumps are still not large enough,
or the inter-arrival times are too long,
then it is still very unlikely for the SGD iterates to even get close the the boundary.
Define

\displaystyle K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\{k\in[K^{\eta}(\delta)]:\ \mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{k}\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

and let
K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}=\infty
if
\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}<J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
Also, if
K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}<\infty
, let

\displaystyle T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}T^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}(\delta)

In other words,
T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)
is the first time that, prior to first exit or return, the accumulated cost of large jumps has reached
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
Regarding the
o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta})
term in the next Lemma, we note that the function
\widetilde{\lambda}
defined in
eq.

J.50
is regularly varying (as
\eta\downarrow 0
) with index
1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}
.
Therefore,
\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta}=o(\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta))
as
\eta
approaches
0
.

Lemma J.20
.

There exists some
\Delta>0
such that the following claim holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
and all
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(B^{\times}_{2,\text{(I)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cup B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)=o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta})

as
\eta\downarrow 0
where
\hat{t}(\epsilon)=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
and

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{2,\text{(I)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}<\infty\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}>J_{\mathcal{G}}\},

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}<\infty\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

\displaystyle\cap\Big\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\ s.t.T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>2\bar{t}/\eta\text{ or }\exists j=1,2,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\ s.t.\ \eta\left\lVert W^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \cap\big\{\min_{j=0,1,\cdots,T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)}\bm{d}\big(X^{\eta}_{j},\mathcal{G}^{c}\big)\leq\bar{\epsilon}\big\}.

Proof.

From Assumption
I.8
, we know the existence of some
\Delta>0
satisfying the following condition:
For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\bm{j}\in\{1,\cdots,m\}^{k}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(\alpha_{j_{i}}-1)<J_{\mathcal{G}}<\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\alpha_{j_{i}}-1)
(note that there are only finitely many possible choices for such
\bm{j}
), we have

\displaystyle J_{\mathcal{G}}+\Delta<\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\alpha_{j_{i}}-1)

(J.55)

Fix such
\Delta>0
.
Meanwhile, given the fixed
\epsilon>0
, we can define
\hat{t}=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
and choose
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
as the largest possible value such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)},\ \ \bar{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon,\ \ \widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.
We stress that
\bar{t}
is a fixed constant while
\hat{t}
depends on the value of
\epsilon
, and for any sufficiently small
\epsilon
we will have
\hat{t}>\bar{t}
.

Let us define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}\cup A^{\times}_{2}
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}\Big\}

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.
By definition of
l^{*}
, we must have
K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<l^{*}
on event
\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}
.

First, we analyze the following event

\displaystyle B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for some }j=2,3,\cdots,K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}\Big\}.

In particular, note that
B=\bigcup_{K=0}^{l^{*}-1}B(K)
where

\displaystyle B(K)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}

\displaystyle(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}=K+1\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K}<J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

\displaystyle\ \ \cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for some }j=1,2,3,\cdots,K\Big\}.

When
K=0<1
, the event
B(0)=\emptyset
by definition (due to event
\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)>\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for some }j=1,2,3,\cdots,K\Big\}
).
For
K=1,\cdots,l^{*}
, Lemma
J.16
implies that
B(K)=\emptyset
for all
\eta
sufficiently small.
In summary,
B=\emptyset
for all
\eta
sufficiently small.

Now we focus on the following two events

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for all }j=2,3,\cdots,K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}\Big\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}>J_{\mathcal{G}}\},

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for all }j=2,3,\cdots,K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}\Big\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}.

On the one hand,
event
C
can be decomposed as follows.
Let

\mathbb{J}^{\uparrow}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\bm{j}\in\{1,2,\cdots,m\}^{k}:\ k=|\bm{j}|,\ \sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(\alpha_{j_{i}}-1)<J_{\mathcal{G}}<\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\alpha_{j_{i}}-1)\}

be the set that contains all the types
\bm{j}
such that the accumulated cost reach
J_{\mathcal{G}}
if and only if the last element is kept.
One can see that there are only finitely many elements in
\mathbb{J}^{\uparrow}
.
Now we have
C=\bigcup_{\bm{j}\in\mathbb{J}^{\uparrow}}C(\bm{j})
where (see the definition in
eq.

J.10
)

\displaystyle C(\bm{j})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for all }j=2,3,\cdots,K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cap\big\{\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}}(\delta)\big)\text{ is of type-}\bm{j}\big\}.

For any
\bm{j}\in\mathbb{J}^{\uparrow}
, observe that (let
k_{\bm{j}}=|\bm{j}|
)

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(C(\bm{j}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(W^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)\in E_{\bm{j}_{i}}\ \forall i\in[K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}]\text{ and }T^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{i-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{2\hat{t}}{\eta}\text{ for all }i=2,3,\cdots,K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\prod_{i=1}^{k_{\bm{j}}}\frac{H_{\bm{j}_{i}}(\delta/\eta)}{H(\delta/\eta)}\big)\cdot\big(\mathbb{P}(T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\leq 2\hat{t}/\eta)\big)^{k_{\bm{j}}-1}.

The last equality is due to independence of
W^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)
and
T^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{i-1}(\delta)
.
The regularly varying natures of functions
H_{j}
and
H
imply that

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{k_{\bm{j}}}\frac{H_{\bm{j}_{i}}(\delta/\eta)}{H(\delta/\eta)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{k_{\bm{j}}}\frac{H_{\bm{j}_{i}}(1/\eta)}{H(1/\eta)}}=\frac{\delta^{k_{\bm{j}}\alpha_{1}}}{\delta^{\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\bm{j}}}\alpha_{\bm{j}_{i}}}}.

Meanwhile, from Lemma
G.4
and the regularly varying nature of function
H_{j}
and
H
(in particular, repeating the same calculations that leads to
eq.

J.46
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\big(\mathbb{P}(T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\leq 2\hat{t}/\eta)\big)^{k_{\bm{j}}-1}}{\big(\frac{H(1/\eta)}{\eta}\cdot\frac{2\hat{t}}{\delta^{\alpha_{1}}}\big)^{k_{\bm{j}}-1}}=1.

Therefore, we have yielded that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(C(\bm{j}))}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{\bm{j}}(\eta)}\leq\frac{\delta^{k_{\bm{j}}\alpha_{1}}}{\delta^{\sum_{i=1}^{k_{\bm{j}}}\alpha_{\bm{j}_{i}}}}\cdot{\big(\frac{2\hat{t}}{\delta^{\alpha_{1}}}\big)^{k_{\bm{j}}-1}}<\infty

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{\bm{j}}(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{k_{\bm{j}}}H_{\bm{j}_{i}}(1/\eta)}{\eta^{k_{\bm{j}}-1}H(1/\eta)}=o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta})

due to
eq.

J.55
. Therefore, one can see that as
\eta\downarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(C)=o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta}).

Furthermore, the discussion above have shown that for all
\eta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(B^{\times}_{2,\text{(I)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta})+\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\times}).

From Lemma
J.10
and
J.12
,
one can see that for all
N>1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta
and all
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small,

\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})=o(\eta^{N}).

Fix such
\delta>0
.
In conclusion, we now have that

\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(B^{\times}_{2,\text{(I)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)=o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta})
.

On the other hand, on event
D
, Assumption
I.7
shows that (see the definition in
eq.

J.39
)

\displaystyle\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta}(\delta)}=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

\displaystyle=\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\big\{\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}}(\delta)\big)\text{ is of type-}\bm{j}\text{ for some }\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})\big\}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\big\{\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}}(\delta)\big)\text{ is of type-}\bm{j}\text{ for some }\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle\cap\Big\{\forall j=2,3,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta},\ \ T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq 2\hat{t}/\eta\Big\}\cap\big\{\min_{j=0,1,\cdots,T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)}\bm{d}\big(X^{\eta}_{j},\mathcal{G}^{c}\big)\leq\bar{\epsilon}\big\}

\displaystyle\cap\Big\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\ s.t.\ T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>2\bar{t}/\eta\text{ OR }\exists j=1,2,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\ s.t.\ \eta\left\lVert W^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\}.

In particular, using Lemma
J.15
, one can see that when
\eta
is sufficiently small, on event
B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap D
we have
\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{k}\right\rVert<3\epsilon
for all
k<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)
and

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}-\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)\right\rVert<2\epsilon.

Here

\displaystyle\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,X^{(1)};\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0

with

\bm{T}=\big(0,T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),T^{\eta}_{3}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,T^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}+1}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}(\delta)\big)
,

\bm{W}=\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}(\delta)\big)
,
and
X^{(1)}=X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)-1}
.
Besides,
eq.

J.17
dictates that, on event
B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap D
, we must have
\inf_{s\geq 0}\bm{d}(\bm{z}^{\eta}(s),\mathcal{G}^{c})\geq 100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon},
hence

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)]}\bm{d}(X^{\eta}_{k},\mathcal{G}^{c})>50l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}.

However, this clearly contradicts the definition of event
B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
.
In conclusion, we have established that (for all
\eta
sufficiently small)
B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap D=\emptyset
and

\displaystyle\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(B^{\times}_{2,\text{(II)}}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\big)\leq\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\times}).

However, as per the argument above, our choice of sufficiently small
\delta>0
ensures that
\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})=o(\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta})
and concludes the proof.
∎

Furthermore, starting from the local minimum
\bm{0}
, it is unlikely that the SGD iterates will be extremely close to
\partial\mathcal{G}
when the accumulated cost of large jumps reaches
J_{\mathcal{G}}
. The next lemma provides an upper bound for the probability of the said scenario.
For any set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define its
\epsilon-
enlargement as the following open set

A^{\epsilon}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{x:\ \bm{d}(x,A)<\epsilon\}.

Lemma J.21
.

For any
\epsilon\in\big(0,\bar{\epsilon}/(3+3\rho^{*})\big)
and any
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}\leq\delta^{\alpha_{1}}\Psi(\epsilon)

where
B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\{\bm{d}(X^{\eta}_{T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)},\ \partial\mathcal{G})<\epsilon\}

and
\Psi(\epsilon)=\mu\Big(h^{-1}\big((\partial\mathcal{G})^{(3+3\rho^{*})\epsilon}\big)\Big)
with
h
defined in
eq.

J.38
and
\mu
defined in
eq.

J.48
, and
\rho^{*}\in(0,\infty)
is the constant provided in Corollary
J.7
.

Proof.

Again, given the fixed
\epsilon>0
, we can define
\hat{t}=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
and choose
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
as the largest possible value such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)},\ \ \bar{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon,\ \ \widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.
We stress that
\bar{t}
is a fixed constant while
\hat{t}
depends on the value of
\epsilon
, and for any sufficiently small
\epsilon
we will have
\hat{t}>\bar{t}
.

Let us define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}\cup A^{\times}_{2}
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,l^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}\Big\}

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.
Consider the following decomposition of event
B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap(A^{\times})^{c}
:

\displaystyle C_{\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}}>J_{\mathcal{G}}\}.

\displaystyle C_{\text{(II)}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\Big\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)>2\bar{t}/\eta\text{ or }\exists j=1,2,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}\ s.t.\ \eta\left\lVert W^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\}

\displaystyle C_{\text{(III)}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big(B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\cap(A^{\times})^{c}\big)\char 92\relax\big(C_{\text{(I)}}\cup C_{\text{(II)}}\big).

Using Lemma
J.20
, we know that for all
\delta>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(C_{\text{(I)}}\cup C_{\text{(II)}}\big)}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}=0.

As a result,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(B^{\times}_{3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\times})}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}+\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(C_{\text{(III)}})}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}.

From Lemma
J.10
and
J.12
,
one can see that for all
N>1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}
and all
\delta>0
that is sufficiently small, we have

\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})=o(\eta^{N})=o(\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)).

Moving on, we focus on bounding the probability of event
C_{\text{(III)}}
.
In particular, note that

\displaystyle C_{\text{(III)}}=(A^{\times})^{c}\cap\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\eta,\delta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\{\bm{d}(X^{\eta}_{T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)},\ \partial\mathcal{G})<\epsilon\}

\displaystyle\cap\Big\{\forall j=2,3,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta},\ T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq 2\bar{t}/\eta\text{ and }\forall j=1,2,\cdots,K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta},\ \eta\left\lVert W^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)\right\rVert\geq\bar{\delta}\Big\}.

By applying Lemma
J.15
on event
C_{\text{(III)}}
,
one can see that when
\eta
is sufficiently small, on this event we have
\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{k}\right\rVert<3\epsilon
for all
k<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)
and

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)3}-\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)\right\rVert<2\epsilon.

Here

\displaystyle\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,X^{(1)};\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0

with

\bm{T}=\big(0,T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),T^{\eta}_{3}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,T^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}-1}^{\eta,\delta}}(\delta)\big)
,

\bm{W}=\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{K_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}^{\eta,\delta}}(\delta)\big)
,
and
X^{(1)}=X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)-1}
.
To better control the location of
\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)
, we also construct

\displaystyle\bm{z}^{\eta}_{0}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,\bm{0};\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0

where the only difference is that we substitute the initial value
X^{(1)}
with
\bm{0}
.
Note that on event
C_{\text{(III)}}
, we have
\left\lVert X^{(1)}\right\rVert<3\epsilon
.
By applying Corollary
J.7
(the condition about line segments contained in
\mathcal{G}
is verified due to
eq.

J.18
),
we have the following bound on event
C_{\text{(III)}}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\sum_{i=1}^{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathbb{G}}}}\bm{T}_{i}]}\left\lVert\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)-\bm{z}^{\eta}_{0}(s)\right\rVert<3\rho^{*}\epsilon.

Now recall Definition
J.1
.
Combining all the bounds we have obtained so far, we see that, when
\eta
is sufficiently small, on event
C_{\text{(III)}}
we have

\displaystyle\bm{d}\Big(\bm{z}^{\eta}_{0}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathbb{G}}}}\bm{T}_{i}\big),\ \partial\mathcal{G}\Big)<(2+3\rho^{*})\epsilon

and
\bm{W}
is of type-
\bm{j}
for some
\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})
.
It then follows immediately from Corollary
J.14
that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(C_{\text{(III)}})}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}\leq\limsup_{\eta}\frac{p\big((\partial\mathcal{G})^{(2+3\rho^{*})\epsilon},\delta,\eta\big)}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{\alpha_{1}}\mu\Big(h^{-1}\big(\overline{(\partial\mathcal{G})^{(2+3\rho^{*})\epsilon}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{\alpha_{1}}\mu\Big(h^{-1}\big({(\partial\mathcal{G})^{(3+3\rho^{*})\epsilon}}\big)\Big)

and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lastly, we provide a lower bound for the probability of the
most likely
way of escape,
i.e., due to
k^{*}
large jumps with accumulated cost
J_{\mathcal{G}}
and relatively short inter-arrival time less (when compared to
\bar{t}/\eta
).
We stress that, in the next result, the value of constant
c_{*}>0
would not vary with the choice of parameters
\epsilon,\delta
.

Lemma J.22
.

There exists some
c_{*}>0
such that the following claim holds for
any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/(4+3\rho^{*}))
and any sufficiently small
\delta>0
:

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\inf_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\circ}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}\geq c_{*}\delta^{\alpha_{1}}

where the event is defined as

\displaystyle A^{\circ}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\Big\{\sigma(\eta)=T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)\Big\}\cap\Big\{T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)\leq\frac{2\bar{t}}{\eta}\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,k^{*}\Big\}

and
\rho^{*}\in(0,\infty)
is the constant provided in Corollary
J.7
.

Proof.

First of all, Assumption
I.7
implies that

\displaystyle\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}<\infty\}\cap\Big\{\sigma(\eta)=T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta,\delta)\Big\}=\{K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}=k^{*}\}\cap\Big\{\sigma(\eta)=T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)\Big\}.

We also stress that
K^{\eta,\delta}_{J_{\mathcal{G}}}=k^{*}
means that
\sigma(\eta)\wedge R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\geq T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
(i.e. the arrival time of the
k^{*}-
th large jumps and that the accumulated cost of large jumps
W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
is exactly
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.

Due to
eq.

J.19
and
eq.

J.17
,
there exists some
\bm{j}\in\bm{j}(\bm{i}^{*})
,
some
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}=(\widetilde{\theta}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{\theta}_{k^{*}})
with
\widetilde{\theta}_{i}\in F_{i}\ \forall i
,
some
\widetilde{\bm{r}}=(\widetilde{r}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{r}_{k^{*}})
such that
\widetilde{r}_{i}\geq\bar{\delta}\ \forall i
,
some
\widetilde{\bm{t}}=(\widetilde{t}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{t}_{k^{*}})
such that
\widetilde{t}_{1}=0
and
\widetilde{t}_{i}\in(0,\bar{t})
for all
i=2,\cdots,k^{*}

such that

\displaystyle\bm{d}\big(h(\widetilde{\bm{r}},\widetilde{\bm{\theta}},\widetilde{\bm{t}}),\ \mathcal{G}\big)>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}

where
h
is the mapping defined in
eq.

J.38
.
Let
\widetilde{y}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}h(\widetilde{\bm{r}},\widetilde{\bm{\theta}},\widetilde{\bm{t}}).

Moreover, the continuity of mapping
h
implies the existence of some
\Delta>0
such that
(\widetilde{\bm{r}},\widetilde{\bm{\theta}},\widetilde{\bm{t}})\in\mathcal{X}\subseteq h^{-1}\big(B(\widetilde{y},\bar{\epsilon})\big)

where the open domain
\mathcal{X}
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{(\bm{r},\bm{\theta},\bm{t}):\ t_{1}=0\text{ and }\ \left\lVert\theta_{i}-\widetilde{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert<\Delta,\ |r_{i}-\widetilde{r}_{i}|<\Delta,\ |t_{i}-\widetilde{t}_{i}|<\Delta\ \forall i\in[k^{*}]\}.

In particular, the parameter
\Delta
can be chosen small enough with

\displaystyle\Delta<\min_{i=2,\cdots,k^{*}}\widetilde{t}_{i},\ \Delta<\min_{i=1,\cdots,k^{*}}\widetilde{r}_{i}\wedge 1

so that for any
(\bm{r},\bm{\theta},\bm{t})\in\mathcal{X}
, we have
t_{i}>0
for all
i=2,\cdots,k^{*}
and
r_{i}>0
for all
i\in[k^{*}]
.
Fix such
\Delta>0
.
Meanwhile, from the definition of the measure
\mu(\cdot)
in
eq.

J.40
eq.

J.48

and the fact that
\widetilde{\theta}_{i}\in F_{\bm{j}_{i}}
where the closed set
F_{\bm{j}_{i}}
is the support of the probability measure
S_{\bm{j}_{i}}
on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

(see Assumption
I.6
),
one can see that
\mu(\mathcal{X})>0
.

Let
\bm{R}=(R_{i})_{i=1}^{k^{*}},\bm{\Theta}=(\Theta_{i})_{i=1}^{k^{*}},\bm{T}=(T_{i})_{i=1}^{k^{*}}
with
R_{i}=\eta\left\lVert W^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)\right\rVert,\ \Theta_{i}=W^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)/\left\lVert W^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)\right\rVert
and
T_{i}=\eta(T^{\eta}_{i}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{i-1}(\delta))
for all
i\geq 2
and
T_{1}=0
. Now recall Definition
J.1
and consider the following event

\displaystyle B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{(\bm{R},\bm{\Theta},\bm{T})\in\mathcal{X}\}=\{Z_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)}\text{ is of type-}(h(\mathcal{X}),\eta,\delta)\}.

Corollary
J.14
then gives the bound

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\mathbb{P}(B)}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}\geq\delta^{\alpha_{1}}\mu(\mathcal{X})>0.

From now on, we fix some
c_{*}\in(0,\mu(\mathcal{X}))
.
Given the fixed
\epsilon>0
, we can define
\hat{t}=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
and choose
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
as the largest possible value such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\exp(2\hat{t}M)},\ \ \bar{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon,\ \ \widetilde{\rho}(\hat{t})\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon

where functions
\bar{\rho}(\cdot),\widetilde{\rho}(\cdot)
are defined in Corollary
J.6
and
J.9
respectively.
We stress that
\bar{t}
is a fixed constant while
\hat{t}
depends on the value of
\epsilon
, and for any sufficiently small
\epsilon
we will have
\hat{t}>\bar{t}
.

Let us define an event
A^{\times}=A^{\times}_{1}\cup A^{\times}_{2}
where

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{\exists n<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)\ s.t.\ \left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}(x)\right\rVert>3\epsilon\Big\},

\displaystyle A^{\times}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists j=2,\cdots,k^{*}\ s.t.

\displaystyle\ \max_{k=1,2,\cdots,(T^{\eta}_{j}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)-1)\wedge(2\hat{t}/\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+1}+\cdots+Z_{T^{\eta}_{j-1}(\delta)+j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}\Big\}

where the positive integer
l^{*}
is defined in
eq.

J.1
.

Now we focus on the event
B\char 92\relax A^{\times}
.
On one hand, Lemma
J.15
shows that, for all
\eta
sufficiently small,
on this event we have
\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{k}\right\rVert<3\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}
for all
k<T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)
and

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)]}\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{\lfloor s\rfloor+T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta}-\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)\right\rVert<2\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}.

Here

\displaystyle\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,X^{(1)};\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0

with

\bm{T}=\big(0,T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),T^{\eta}_{3}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)-T^{\eta}_{k^{*}-1}(\delta)\big)
,

\bm{W}=\big(W^{\eta}_{1}(\delta),W^{\eta}_{2}(\delta),\cdots,W^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)\big)
,
and
X^{(1)}=X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta)-1}
.
To better control the location of
\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)
, we also construct

\displaystyle\bm{z}^{\eta}_{0}(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}^{\eta}\big(s,\bm{0};\ \bm{T},\bm{W}\big)\ \forall s\geq 0

where the only difference is that we substitute the initial value
X^{(1)}
with
\bm{0}
.

First of all, from
eq.

J.20
, we see that

\displaystyle\bm{d}(\bm{z}^{\eta}(s),\mathcal{G}^{c})>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon},\ \bm{d}(\bm{z}_{0}^{\eta}(s),\mathcal{G}^{c})>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}\ \ \forall s\in[0,\sum_{i=1}^{k^{*}}\bm{T}_{i}).

Besides, due to
\mathcal{X}\subseteq h^{-1}\big(B(\widetilde{y},\bar{\epsilon})\big)
, we have that

\bm{d}\big(\bm{z}_{0}^{\eta}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k^{*}}\bm{T}_{i}\big),\mathcal{G}\big)>99l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}.

Next, using Corollary
J.7
, we have that

\sup_{\in[0,\sum_{i=1}^{k^{*}}\bm{T}_{i}]}\left\lVert\bm{z}^{\eta}(s)-\bm{z}^{\eta}_{0}(s)\right\rVert<3\rho^{*}\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}
,
which further implies that

\displaystyle\bm{d}\big(\bm{z}^{\eta}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k^{*}}\bm{T}_{i}\big),\mathcal{G}\big)>99l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}-\bar{\epsilon}\geq 98l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}.

Now we have the following facts regarding the distance between SGD iterates
X^{\eta}_{j}
and the domain
\mathcal{G}
.
First, on event
B\char 92\relax A^{\times}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\bm{d}(X^{\eta}_{j},\mathcal{G}^{c})>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}-\bar{\epsilon}\geq 99l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}\ \ \forall j<T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta),

implying that
\sigma(\eta)\geq T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
.

Next, if
R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
, then there exists some
T^{\eta}_{1}(\delta\leq j<T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
such that
\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{j}\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon

hence
\left\lVert\bm{z}^{\eta}(j-T^{\eta}_{1})\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon+2\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}<100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}
.
However, in light of
eq.

J.20
we know that on event
\{R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)\}\cap(B\char 92\relax A^{\times})
,
we have

\displaystyle\bm{d}\big(\bm{z}^{\eta}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k^{*}}\bm{T}_{i}\big),\mathcal{G}^{c}\big)>100l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}

and yield a contradiction.
Therefore, on event
B\char 92\relax A^{\times}
we must have
R(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\geq T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
.

Besides, bounding the gap between
X^{\eta}_{j},\bm{z}^{\eta}(j)
and
\bm{z}^{\eta}_{0}(j)
at time
j=T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
using results above, we can show that

\displaystyle\bm{d}(X^{\eta}_{T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)},\mathcal{G})>98l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}-\bar{\epsilon}\geq 97l^{*}\bar{\epsilon}.

Therefore, we must have
\sigma(\eta)=T^{\eta}_{k^{*}}(\delta)
.
In summary, we have shown that, for all
\eta
sufficiently small,
B\char 92\relax A^{\times}\subseteq A^{\circ}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
.
Combining all the bounds above, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\inf_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(A^{\circ}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\mathbb{P}(B)}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}-\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}

\displaystyle\geq\delta^{\alpha_{1}}c_{*}-\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}(A^{\times})}{\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)}.

To conclude the proof, it suffices to invoke Lemma
J.10
and
J.12

with some
N>1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}
.
∎

J.4
Proof of the main result

Now we are ready to state the main result and provide upper and lower bounds to the distribution of the scaled first exit time from domain
\mathcal{G}
.
In particular, we define a scaling function

\displaystyle\lambda(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\widetilde{\lambda}(\eta)H(1/\eta)

(J.56)

where the function
\widetilde{\lambda}(\cdot)
is defined in
eq.

J.50
.
One can easily see that
\lambda\in RV_{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}}(\eta)
.
We show that the scaled first exit time
\lambda(\eta)\sigma(\eta)
converges in distribution to an exponential random variable as
\eta\downarrow 0
,
which implies that, in expectation, the first exit time is roughly a
1/\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}}
term and its order is dictated by the minimum cost for exit
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.

Proposition J.23
.

Given any
C\in(0,1),u>0
, the following inequalities hold for all
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\sigma(\eta)\lambda(\eta)>u)

\displaystyle\leq 2C+\exp(-(1-C)^{2}qu),

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\sigma(\eta)\lambda(\eta)>u)

\displaystyle\geq-C+\exp(-(1+C)qu)

where
q=\mu\big(h^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\big)
.

Before presenting the proof to Proposition
J.23
, we make some preparations.
First, we introduce stopping times (for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\tau_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n>\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\eta\left\lVert Z_{n}\right\rVert>\delta\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\min\{n\geq\tau_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{n}\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon\}

with the convention that
\tau_{0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\widetilde{\tau}_{0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=0.

Intuitively, at each
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
the SGD iterates have just returned to a small neighborhood of the local minimum
\bm{0}
.
Due to Markov property of
X^{\eta}_{n}
, the SGD iterates almost
regenerate
at each
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
despite the previous trajectory, and the times
(\widetilde{\tau}_{k})_{k\geq 1}
partitions the entire timeline into different segments that can almost be interpreted as
regeneration cycles
,
where
\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}
can be understood as the starting point of the
k-
th cycle.
In light of the embedded (informal) regeneration process, one natural approach is to determine the dynamics and probability of making an exit on one (hence every) cycle, and this will be carried out with the help of technical results in the previous section.
It is worth noticing that
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
are defined under the proviso that
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\geq\tau_{k}
where
\tau_{k}
is the first big jump during the
k-
th cycle.
Considering results such as Lemma
J.12
,
it is reasonable to expect that the SGD iterates would be trapped at local minimum until a large jump strikes.
Therefore, we define the (informal) regeneration points
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
in such a way that a cycle
[\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{k})
ends only if we have observed at least one large jump already.
Regarding the notations, we add a remark that when there is no ambiguity we will drop the dependency on
\epsilon,\delta,\eta
and simply write
\tau_{k},\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
.

Specifically, we are interested in the large jumps and the accumulated cost thereof during each cycle, which definitely entails some systematic bookkeeping.
For all
k\geq 1
, the random variable

\displaystyle\textbf{j}_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\#\{n=\tau_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta),\tau_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)+1,\cdots,\widetilde{\tau}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\wedge\sigma(\eta):\eta\left\lVert Z_{n}\right\rVert>\delta\}

can be understood as the count of large jumps during the
k-
th cycle. Here are two remarks on this definition.

•

First, for any
k
with
\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k}
, we have
\textbf{j}_{k}=0
.
Recall that the object we study here is the first exit time
\sigma(\eta)
, so there is virtually no need to keep track of the dynamics of the SGD after it leaves the domain
\mathcal{G}
.

•

The random variable
\textbf{j}_{k}
is measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}
, the stopped
\sigma-
algebra generated by the stopping time
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)
, which, intuitively, is stating that one should be able to determine the number of large jumps during the
k-
th cycle when this cycle ends.

Furthermore, for each
k=1,2,\cdots
, let

\displaystyle T_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\tau_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\wedge\sigma(\eta),

\displaystyle T_{k,j}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\big\{n>T_{k,j-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta):\eta\left\lVert Z_{n}\right\rVert>\delta\big\}\wedge\sigma(\eta)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\ \ \forall j\geq 2,

\displaystyle W_{k,j}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}Z_{T_{k,j}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)}\ \ \forall j\geq 1

with the convention
T_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)
.
Note that for any
k\geq 1,j\geq 1
,
T_{k,j}
is a stopping time.
Besides, for any
k
with
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}<\sigma(\eta)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}+1\leq T_{k,j}\leq\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)\ \ \forall j\in[\textbf{j}_{k}],

(J.57)

and the sequences
\big(T_{k,j}\big)_{j=1}^{\textbf{j}_{k}}
and
\big(W_{k,j}\big)_{j=1}^{\textbf{j}_{k}}

are the arrival times and sizes of
large
jumps during the
k-
th cycle, respectively.
Again, when there is no ambiguity we will drop the dependency on
\epsilon,\delta,\eta
and simply write
T_{k,j}
and
W_{k,j}
.

We are now able to keep track of the accumulated cost of jumps during each cycle.
For any
k\geq 1,i\geq 1
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(i;\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{l=1}^{i}J(W_{k,i}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)).

Moreover, we define the following index

\displaystyle\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k;\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\begin{cases}\min\{j=1,2,\cdots,\textbf{j}_{k}:\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(j;\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}\}&\text{ if }\widetilde{\tau}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)<\sigma(\eta)\\
\infty&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

with the convention that
\min\emptyset=\infty
.
That is to say, if
\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\geq\sigma(\eta)
or
\mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}_{k})<J_{\mathcal{G}}
, we define
\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)
as
\infty
;
otherwise
\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)
is the index for the first large jump during the
k-
th cycle that drives the accumulated cost of large jumps to reach
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
For clarity of the presentation below, we also define

\displaystyle T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k;\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbbm{1}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty\}T_{k,\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)}+\mathbbm{1}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)=\infty\}0,

\displaystyle X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k;\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbbm{1}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty\}X^{\eta}_{T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k;\epsilon,\delta,\eta)}+\mathbbm{1}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)=\infty\}\bm{0}.

In other words, when
\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty
, the random variable
X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k;\epsilon,\delta\eta)
is equal to
X^{\eta}_{T_{k,\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)}}
, the location of the SGD iterates right when the accumulated cost on the
k-
th cycle reaches
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
Notation wise, we will drop the dependency on
\epsilon,\delta,\eta
again and simply write
\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k),X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)
or
\mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(j)
when there is no risk of ambiguity.

As have mentioned above, we want to zoom in on the each cycle and analyze the probability of each possible case.
To be specific, we want to introduce a series of scenarios, formally defined as several events, that exhaust all the possibilities during a cycle.
The events will be denoted in the form of
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
or
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{j}
largely following the next few rules.
First, we say an event/scenario is
atypical
if its probability is rather small, and we assign it with superscript
\times
;
otherwise we say the event is
typical
and add a superscript
\circ
.
Besides, the subscript
k
indicates the cycle that the event concerns.
Lastly, events with label (of type)
A
usually describe how the SGD iterates try to escape from
\mathcal{G}
, whereas events with label
B
focuses on how the SGD iterates return to the local minimum.

Now we proceed and formally define the said series of events.
First, for each
k\geq 1
, define the event

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\exists i\in[l^{*}]\ s.t.\ \max_{j=T_{k,i-1}+1,T_{k,i-1}+2,\cdots,(T_{k,i}-1)\wedge(T_{k,i-1}+\lceil\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\rceil)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T_{k,i}+1}+\cdots+Z_{j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)\Big\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \cup\Big\{\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{j}\right\rVert>3\epsilon\ \text{ for some }j=\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}+1,\cdots,T_{k,1}-1\Big\}

(J.58)

with
\hat{t}(\epsilon)=c_{1}+c_{1}\log(1/\epsilon)
and function
\widetilde{\epsilon}(\cdot)
defined in
eq.

J.51
.
It is similar to the event
A^{\times}
defined in
eq.

J.52
eq.

J.53
in the previous section, and its probability will be controlled using a similar approach.

Next, consider event (for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\Big\{\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}_{k})<J_{\mathcal{G}}\Big\}

(J.59)

that describes an atypical case where the exit occurs during the
k-
th cycle yet the accumulated cost of all large jumps is less than
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
Another atypical event is defined as

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty\}\cap\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)\ s.t.\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\}.

(J.60)

This event describes the case where the accumulated cost of large jumps in a cycle has reached
J_{\mathcal{G}}
but the inter-arrival time between some large jumps are unusually long.
We stress that, by definition of
\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)
,
we have

\{\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty\}=\{\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}<\sigma(\eta)\}\cap\{J^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\bm{j}_{k})\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}\}
.

Moving on, we consider the following events (defined for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,3}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}_{k})<J_{\mathcal{G}},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}<\sigma(\eta)\}\cap\Big\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,\textbf{j}_{k}\ s.t.\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big\}

(J.61)

that describes another similar case where the
k-
th cycle ends with return to the local minimum but the inter-arrival time between some large jumps are unusually long.

The next event

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,4}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}

\displaystyle\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty,\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k))=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\{\bm{d}(X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k),\mathcal{G}^{c})\leq\bar{\epsilon}\}\cap\Big\{\exists j\in[\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)]\ s.t.\ \eta\left\lVert W_{k,j}\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\}

(J.62)

describes the case where the accumulated cost on the
k-
th cycle has hit
J_{\mathcal{G}}
exactly at some point with SGD iterates being rather close to
\mathcal{G}^{c}
yet some large jumps are not large enough when compared to the fixed constant
\bar{\delta}
.

Meanwhile, with events

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,5}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty,\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k))=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\Big\{\bm{d}(X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k),\partial\mathcal{G})<\epsilon\Big\},

(J.63)

we analyze the case where the SGD iterates reaches somewhere close the the boundary set
\partial\mathcal{G}
with large jumps of cost equal to
J_{\mathcal{G}}
. Lastly, define event

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,6}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty,\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k))>J_{\mathcal{G}}\}

(J.64)

for the case where the accumulated cost of large jumps on the
k-
th cycle exceeds
J_{\mathcal{G}}
without hitting it.
As an amalgamation of these atypical scenarios, we let

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\bigcup_{i=0}^{6}\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,i}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta).

(J.65)

Next, we analyze the probability of some events
(\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k})_{k\geq 1}
that concern the behavior of SGD iterates during the
k-
th cycle after
T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)
.
Let us define

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty,\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k))=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\{\bm{d}(X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k),\mathcal{G}^{c})\geq\epsilon\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \cap\Big\{T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq 2\frac{\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)\Big\}

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)}>\rho(\epsilon)/\eta\}\cup\{\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\}

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,1}\cap\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}

(J.66)

where
\rho(\cdot)
is the function in Lemma
J.11
.
From the definition of
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
, in particular the inclusion of
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k,2}
, one can see that the intuitive interpretation of event
\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}
is that the SGD iterates
did not return
to local minimum efficiently or even escaped from
\mathcal{G}
after large jumps with cost equal to
J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
In comparison, the following events will characterize what would typically happen during each attempt:

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty,\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k))=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\{\sigma(\eta)=T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \cap\Big\{T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}\leq\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\ \forall j=2,3,\cdots,\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)\Big\},

(J.67)

\displaystyle\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\{\sigma(\eta)>\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}-T_{k,1}\leq\frac{2l^{*}\hat{t}(\epsilon)+\rho(\epsilon)}{\eta}\}\cap\{\mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}_{k})\leq J_{\mathcal{G}}\}.

(J.68)

Intuitively speaking,
\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{k}
tells us that the exit happened right at
T^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)
with accumulated cost of large jumps in the cycle being exactly
J_{\mathcal{G}}
,
and
\textbf{B}^{\circ}_{k}
requires that that the first exit did not occur during the
k-
th cycle, and the SGD iterates returned to local minimum rather efficiently.
It is worth noticing that, by definition,
\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<l^{*}
if
\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty
.

Our immediate next goal is to analyze the event

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\bigcup_{k\geq 1}\bigg(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{i}\cup\textbf{A}^{\circ}_{i}\big)^{c}\bigg)\cap\big(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k}\cup\textbf{B}^{\times}_{k}\big).

(J.69)

In particular, we show that its probability can be made arbitrarily small with proper
\epsilon,\delta,\eta
,
implying that we will almost always observe the event
(\textbf{A}^{\times})^{c}
.

Lemma J.24
.

Given any
C>0
, the following claim holds for all
\epsilon>0,\delta>0
sufficiently small:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{x:\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))\leq C.

Proof.

We start by bounding for the probabilities of all the
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,i}
events.
Fix some
N>1+J_{\mathcal{G}}
.
For

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\exists i\in[l^{*}]\ s.t.\ \max_{j=T_{k,i-1}+1,T_{k,i-1}+2,\cdots,(T_{k,i}-1)\wedge(T_{k,i-1}+\lceil\frac{2\hat{t}(\epsilon)}{\eta}\rceil)\wedge\widetilde{\tau}_{k}\wedge\sigma(\eta)}\eta\left\lVert Z_{T_{k,i}+1}+\cdots+Z_{j}\right\rVert>\widetilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)\Big\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \cup\Big\{\left\lVert X^{\eta}_{j}\right\rVert>3\epsilon\ \text{ for some }j=\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{k-1}+1,\cdots,T_{k,1}-1\Big\},

using Lemma
J.10
and
J.12
,
one can show the existence of some
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}/3)
such that for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, there is some
\delta_{0}(\epsilon)>0
with

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,0}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\eta^{N}}=0\ \ \ \forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}(\epsilon)).

(J.70)

Next, for
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta)=\Big\{\sigma(\eta)<\widetilde{\tau}_{k},\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}_{k})<J_{\mathcal{G}}\Big\},

it follows from Lemma
J.17
that, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, there exists some
0<\delta_{1}(\epsilon)\leq\delta_{0}(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,1}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\eta^{N}}=0\ \ \ \forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{1}(\epsilon)).

(J.71)

For event
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,2}=\{\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty\}\cap\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)\ s.t.\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\}
,
Lemma
J.18
implies that, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, there exists some
0<\delta_{2}(\epsilon)\leq\delta_{1}(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,2}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\eta^{N}}=0\ \ \ \forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{2}(\epsilon)).

(J.72)

For event
\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,3}=\{\mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}_{k})<J_{\mathcal{G}},\ \widetilde{\tau}_{k}<\sigma(\eta)\}\cap\Big\{\exists j=2,3,\cdots,\textbf{j}_{k}\ s.t.\ T_{k,j}-T_{k,j-1}>2\hat{t}(\epsilon)/\eta\Big\}
,
thanks to Lemma
J.19
, one can see that, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, there exists some
0<\delta_{3}(\epsilon)\leq\delta_{2}(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,3}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\eta^{N}}=0\ \ \ \forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{3}(\epsilon)).

(J.73)

For event

\displaystyle\textbf{A}^{\times}_{k,4}=\{\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)<\infty,\ \mathcal{J}^{\text{cycle}}_{k}(\textbf{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k))=J_{\mathcal{G}}\}\cap\{\bm{d}(X^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k),\mathcal{G}^{c})\leq\bar{\epsilon}\}\cap\Big\{\exists j\in[\bm{j}^{\geq J_{\mathcal{G}}}(k)]\ s.t.\ \eta\left\lVert W_{k,j}\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}\Big\},

using Lemma
J.20
, one can see the existence of some
\Delta>0
such that, for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, there is some
0<\delta_{4}(\epsilon)\leq\delta_{3}(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta}\frac{\sup_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\textbf{A}^{\times}_{1,4}(\epsilon,\delta,\eta))}{\eta^{1+J_{\mathcal{G}}-\alpha_{1}+\Delta}}=0\ \ \ \forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{4}(\epsilon)).

(J.74)

As for event
